# CLAUDE.md

The rules for working in this repository (branches, releases) are in AGENTS.md:

@AGENTS.md

This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.

## What this is

A ComfyUI custom node pack whose real product is a **frontend viewer panel**: a floating, dockable image/video/3D viewer with tabs, history, comparison, playback, a file browser, drawing tools and a 3D previz tool. Python here is the thin half — four nodes and a set of `/imageviewer/*` HTTP routes; the JavaScript in `js/` is the thick half.

`__init__.py` exports the nodes, imports `viewer_api` (which registers the routes as a side effect of import), and sets `WEB_DIRECTORY = "js"`, which is how ComfyUI serves the frontend from `/extensions/ComfyUI-ImageViewer/`.

The repository root holds only what the ComfyUI custom node layout needs there (`__init__.py`, `pyproject.toml`, `requirements.txt`, `js/`) plus `docs/`, `tests/` and `vendor/`. Every other Python module is in the `src/` package (`src/nodes.py`, `src/viewer_api.py`, ...) and imports its siblings relatively (`from . import timeline_io`); a module named below without a folder is in `src/`.

## Running and testing

Nothing to build: the JS is plain ES modules served as-is, and Python needs no compilation step. There is a test suite in `tests/` (`D:/AI/agentY/.venv/Scripts/python.exe -m unittest discover -s tests` and `node --test tests/*.mjs` - node's own discovery finds nothing, the files are named `test_*.mjs`); the tests that need PyAV (`test_timeline_media_routes.py`) are skipped there and run with ComfyUI's interpreter, `D:/ai/comfyui/.venv/Scripts/python.exe -m unittest discover -s tests -p "test_timeline_media_routes.py"`. Browser tests serve `tests/harness/*.html` (the real viewer js, ComfyUI stubbed) through `tests/harness/server.mjs` with Playwright. Beyond those, two things stand in for a test runner, and both are worth rebuilding when you change anything non-trivial.

Use ComfyUI's own interpreter, never a bare `python`:

```bash
D:/ai/comfyui/.venv/Scripts/python.exe -s <script>      # -s: ignore user site-packages
node --check js/imageViewer_model3d.js                  # the only "lint" the JS has
D:/ai/comfyui/.venv/Scripts/python.exe -m py_compile src/viewer_api.py
```

**Python-side checks** run the real modules with ComfyUI stubbed, in a scratch directory (not in the repo). The pattern each such script follows:

```python
sys.path.insert(0, r"D:\ai\comfyui")          # for folder_paths and comfy_extras
import folder_paths
folder_paths.set_output_directory(tmp); folder_paths.set_input_directory(...)   # scratch dirs
sys.modules["server"] = <module exposing PromptServer.instance.app + send_sync>
sys.modules["imageviewer"] = <module whose __path__ is this repo's src/>               # relative imports
importlib.import_module("imageviewer.viewer_api")   # registers routes onto the aiohttp app
```
Routes are then driven over real HTTP with `aiohttp.test_utils.TestServer/TestClient`. Nodes are exercised by calling their `FUNCTION` directly.

**Frontend checks** run the real modules in a page: a small aiohttp server serves `js/` plus stubs for ComfyUI's `scripts/app.js` and `scripts/api.js`, and a test script is injected as `<script type="module">`, writing results to a `window.__*` object that is then read out of the page. Two pitfalls: modules are cached hard (hard-reload, or `fetch(url, {cache: "reload"})` before reloading), and a backgrounded tab throttles timers and stalls `requestAnimationFrame`, which makes anything waiting on a frame appear to hang.

## The old name

Until 1.18 the viewer was called "bEpic" (`js/bEpicViewer_*.js`, `bEpicSendToViewer`, `/bepic/*`, `bepic-*` classes, `bepic.*` keys). It is **ComfyUI Image Viewer** now: `imageViewer_*` files and identifiers, `imageviewer` for lowercase names (routes `/imageviewer/*`, events, CSS, localStorage, the .otio namespace `metadata.imageviewer`, the .ora's `imageviewer.json`), `IMAGE_VIEWER_*` environment variables, `comfyui-image-viewer-panel` for the element, `ImageViewer.*` settings and commands, `~/.comfyui_image_viewer/` per user. Do not bring "bEpic" back for anything of the viewer's. What a user saved under the old names still reads: `legacy_names.py` (env vars, the per-user folder, settings ids, a cut's notes), `nodes.py` `LEGACY_NODE_IDS` (the old node ids as hidden aliases), `viewer_api._safe_add` (every route also under `/bepic/`), `paint_io.read_ora` (`bepic.json`), `js/imageViewer_legacy.js` (localStorage, ComfyUI settings and keybindings, node types in a loaded workflow, `window.bEpicViewerApi`); tests in `tests/test_legacy_names.py`. Names that are NOT the viewer's keep "bEpic": the studio's node packs and layout (`bepic_templates`, Get/Set Path, `bepic_animatable`, the bEpic layout), and the Worlds pack (`ComfyUI-bEpicWorlds`, `/bepic_worlds/*`).

## Architecture

### The panel is one Web Component assembled from mixins

`js/imageViewer.js` defines `<comfyui-image-viewer-panel>` (Shadow DOM, so ComfyUI's CSS cannot reach it), loads `imageViewer.html` / `.css` / the icon JSON by `fetch`, and `Object.assign`s every mixin onto the class prototype. Each `imageViewer_mixin*.js` / feature file is therefore **one flat object of methods sharing one `this`** — there is no inheritance and no module boundary at runtime. Consequences worth knowing before editing:

- a method name collides across mixins silently; the last `Object.assign` wins,
- `this.container` is the `.panel-container` inside the shadow root — reach elements through it (or `this.shadowRoot`), never `document`,
- the panel can be **undocked into another browser window**: use `this._viewerWindow()` and the element's `ownerDocument` rather than the globals, or timers and observers fire in the wrong realm.

Defaults live in **Settings → ComfyUI Image Viewer** (`imageViewer_settings.js`: the table, `pref()`/`setPref()`, `PrefsMixin.applyViewerPref` for a change while the panel is up). A few settings *are* panel state and are written back when the panel changes it (history limit, browser kinds and sequence folding). The pure scene model is told its new-shot and render defaults (`setNewSceneDefaults` / `setRenderDefaults`) rather than importing ComfyUI. The settings are also kept **per OS user**, outside ComfyUI's user directory (`viewer_settings.py`, `~/.comfyui_image_viewer/settings.json` or `IMAGE_VIEWER_SETTINGS`; routes `/imageviewer/settings`): AYON starts ComfyUI with a `--user-directory` inside each project, so ComfyUI's own store is per project. `applyStartupPrefs` loads the file before the panel is built and lays it over ComfyUI's values; every setting's `onChange` writes back. Class names in content rendered into ComfyUI's own DOM (the settings dialog) need a prefix: Tailwind owns names like `fixed`. **Colours** (`imageViewer_theme.js`, `THEME`, `PRESETS`; tests `tests/test_theme.mjs`, `tests/test_theme_browser.py`): the stylesheets (imageViewer.css, Paint's `STYLE` in the editor) keep their colours; each colour writes rules with the SAME selector as the ones it changes (same specificity, later) into one of two `<style data-iv-theme>` appended last to the shadow root - `edit` (the Edit page's own parts, always on) and `chrome` (panels, bars, buttons, Paint, Dependencies; `media` `not all` off a cut's tab only when *Use the colours* is "edit" - the default is "all"; `_themeSync` from `editTlTabShown` / `editTlTabHidden`). `base` is the stylesheet's colour, `def` the default = the Paint preset's (Sebastian 2026-11-10: Paint's look is the new default, presets reach the whole viewer). *Colour preset* (`ImageViewer.Colours.Preset`, `applyPreset` / `writePreset` in the settings) writes a preset's colours into the colour settings; a colour changed by hand sets it to `matchPreset` (a preset or "custom"); neither happens at load (`old` undefined) or during the startup load of the user's file. *Follow ComfyUI* (`COMFY`, "comfy", the preset's default; Sebastian 2026-11-10; `followComfyOnce` in `applyStartupPrefs` moves a kept "paint" - the default before, written by Reset colours - whose colours are Paint's to it ONCE, the hidden `ImageViewer.Colours.FollowSet` saying it is done) is no table: `comfyValues(read, canvas)` maps ComfyUI's palette - CSS variables on the main page's `<html>`, the 1.58 theme tokens (`--background`, `--card`, `--secondary`, `--foreground`, `--border`, `--primary`) before the older comfy_base names (`COMFY_VARS`), LiteGraph's `clear_background_color` for the picture - to every colour; `cssColourReader` resolves var() / color-mix() on a probe and spells them through a 1-pixel canvas; `_themeValues` takes them instead of the settings while following, `_themeWatchComfy` (a MutationObserver on `<html>`'s style / class / data-theme and `<head>`'s children) re-applies on a palette change, and `followComfy` writes them into the colour rows (so a change by hand starts from them and makes the preset Custom; in comfy mode a colour row's change does not repaint). **Popped out**: the styles were copied into the popout's `<head>` at undock, so `_themeHosts` writes the two theme styles there too and `_themeApply` sets the popout body's background (what `:host` is in the page); `_undockInto` calls `_themeApply`. The accent is not a table: `accentRules` scans the shadow root's style sheets for declarations with the stylesheet's orange (`rgb(255, 102, 0)`, as the CSSOM spells it) and writes them again with the accent - so `_themeApply` runs again when Paint brings its own sheet (`imageViewer_paintPanel.js`). A new colour row's selectors must exist in a stylesheet (the node test checks imageViewer.css and the paint editor).

The viewer floats over the canvas (`position: fixed`, appended to `document.body`) or is **docked to the page** as a panel beside it (`imageViewer_mixinPageDock.js`, everything named `pageDock`; not `mixinDock`, which arranges the viewer's inner panels, nor "undock", the popout window). ComfyUI's page is a grid whose outer cells are empty elements (`#comfyui-body-left` / `-right` / `-bottom`); a placeholder of the docked size goes into one, which makes the canvas area yield, and the viewer — never re-parented, that would pause a playing video — is kept exactly over it (`_pageDockSync`, on resize and on its own `style` changing). The cells are page structure, not an API: a missing one means staying floating. Side and size are settings (`Dock.*`).

State that must survive a reload goes through `queuePersistViewerState()` into `localStorage`; a previz scene additionally lives on its node's hidden `scene_data` widget (see below).

### The 3D side

- `imageViewer_scene3d.js` — the scene **data model only**: plain objects, no DOM, no three.js. Items are a flat list with a `parent` id; transforms are `T(position)·T(pivot)·R·S·T(-pivot)·offset`; keyframes are cubic Hermite with per-key easing. Because it is pure data it can be unit-tested under plain `node`.
- **New things come in at the world origin** (Sebastian 2026-11-12: a plane came in a few units above the grid - shapes, image planes and lights landed at the orbit centre, which after Frame all is a model's middle). Create's shapes, Image plane…, History drops and lights are placed from the origin, never from `viewFocus()`; a camera still comes in where the view is (that is what it is for), a model at the origin. A centred solid (box, sphere, cylinder, cone, torus) and an image plane stand ON the grid with translate 0 0 0 and the pivot at the middle of their base: `scene3d.groundOffset()` (a 0.5 lift) in their `offset`, the frozen-transform matrix every consumer already reads (USD, constraints, rig, freeze); the Channel Box says "frozen" only for any other offset (`isGroundOffset`). Several image planes stand side by side along +X from the origin (`_previzPlaneSpot`). Test `test_new_things_come_in_at_the_world_origin_standing_on_the_grid` in `tests/test_view3d_drop_history_browser.py`.
- `imageViewer_model3d.js` — turns that model into three.js objects and owns the viewport (camera, gizmo, grid, picking, Maya-style navigation, rendering a shot). Each item becomes `root → body → inner(offset) → geometry/children`; the split matters: pivot maths reads *body* space, frozen transforms live on *inner*.
- `imageViewer_mixinPreviz.js` + `previzChannels` / `previzCurves` / `previzRender` / `previzUndo` — the panels and the edits (outliner, channel box, curve editor, render dialog, undo stack).
- **Display layers, selection sets, Character Picker, references** (`imageViewer_sceneLayers.js` - pure: `scene.displayLayers` / `selectionSets` / `references`, read by `parseScene`, tests `tests/test_scene_layers.mjs`; panels in `imageViewer_previzLayers.js`, `PrevizLayersMixin`, dock ids `layers3d` ("Layers": Display / Sets / References tabs) and `picker`; browser test `tests/test_display_layers_browser.py`). A layer is visible + mode normal / template / reference + colour; an item is on one layer, children take their parent's (`displayStates`). The view: a hidden layer hides (`applyFrame`), template = the item's OWN meshes get a wire in the layer colour (`_syncTemplates`, `_templated`; `setMaterialMode` leaves them; hidden in renders), template and reference are not pickable (`_pick`, `idsInRect` via `isPickable`); the outliner can still select them. A set is a remembered selection (`previzSelectIds`). The picker lays a character's controls out from their rest matrices (`pickerLayout`, front: the character's left on the right; side), sets whose members are all that character's are its buttons. A reference: `{id, namespace, path, follow: latest | pinned, version, mtime}`; its items carry `ref: {id, src, base}` and get the id `refItemId(ref, src)` so reloads keep ids; `mergeReference` takes the file's version of everything except the `OVERRIDES` fields the shot changed (differ from `base`); the file's layers and sets come in namespaced. GET `/imageviewer/scene_ref?path=&follow=` (`previz.resolve_reference` / `scene_versions`: the export rules' `<name>_v###.json` or the shot layout's `v###/<shot>_v###_<name>.json`; tests `tests/test_scene_ref.py`). A 3D tab coming up checks its references at most every 15 s (`_plMaybeCheck`). Referenced items cannot be renamed, deleted or moved inside the reference (only its top items move). Nested references come in flat.
- **New Atlas world** (`imageViewer_atlasCreate.js`, `AtlasCreateMixin`; helpers `worldNameFrom` / `buildCreateRequest` in `imageViewer_atlasRender.js`; the 3D menu's "New Atlas world…" and the Previz panel's "New world"): a prompt and / or up to 8 pictures (History selection, the file picker, desktop drops through `/imageviewer/timeline_upload`) -> POST `/imageviewer/atlas_create` (pictures resolved and `may_serve`-checked in the route, never taken from the page as paths to pass on) -> `atlas_render.start_create` relays to the agentY host's `/agentY/atlas_create`, which runs `atlas_create_world` and opens the world in the 3D space itself. **Engine: Echo** (Sebastian 2026-11-12: a second world generator beside Atlas): the same dialog, `atlas-engine` select (Atlas - World Labs / Echo - SpAItial; the Atlas menu's "New Echo world…" opens it on Echo; the last engine in localStorage `imageviewer.viewer.worldEngine`). Echo takes ONE picture (a 360 panorama works) OR words - never both, the prompt is disabled beside a picture (`maxImagesFor`); its own selects `.echo-model` (echo-2 / echo-hq), `.echo-pano` (auto / true / false), `.echo-mesh` ("" / mesh-simplified / mesh) replace the aspect and the mesh detail. Body `{engine: "echo", name, prompt, images, model, is_pano, mesh}` (`buildCreateRequest`), checked again by `atlas_render.clean_create_request` (ENGINES, ECHO_MODELS, ECHO_MESH) and run by agentY's `/agentY/atlas_create` with core's `echo_create_world`, which opens the splat in the 3D space itself; errors worded by `explainEchoError`. Tests `test_clean_create_request_for_echo`, the Echo test in `tests/test_atlas_create.mjs`, `test_the_echo_engine_takes_one_picture_and_sends_its_own_choices`. Tests `tests/test_atlas_render.py` (CreateTests), `tests/test_atlas_create.mjs`, `tests/test_atlas_create_browser.py`.
- **Chisel this view** (`imageViewer_atlasChisel.js`, `AtlasChiselMixin`; helpers `chiselCamera` / `chiselMode` / `buildChiselRequest` in `imageViewer_atlasRender.js`; the Previz panel's "Chisel" (shown with an Atlas world) and the 3D menu's "Chisel this view…"): Atlas Chisel at the camera looked through, built on the 3D space's geometry: `Model3DView.renderDepth(w, h)` (inside beginRender/endRender) renders planar depth of the solid meshes (world mesh, boxes, models; not splats, splat bounds, image planes, lines/helpers) with an override ShaderMaterial into a float target, read back top-down; `depthInMeshUnits` divides by the world's scale (model_world), the page sends it as base64 float32 (`depth: {w, h, data}`, plus `voxel_size` 0-0.4 from the "Loosen" slider), and the route writes it as Atlas's 16-bit log-depth PNG (`atlas_render.decode_depth` / `write_log_depth`, input/edits/chisel_depth_*.png, z_min = 0.5th percentile, z_max = max) -> `depth_path` + `z_min` / `z_max` to the host. Geometry is on by default (checkbox) with a greyscale preview in the stage; with neither geometry nor a picture Go is off (core refuses that). A prompt with the geometry paints the view; a picture of the view (the view as drawn here at 1280 x 720 via `beginRender` / `renderFrame` - it then carries that picture's own 16:9 camera, `chiselCamera` - or one dropped / picked / the History's selection, taken to be from the camera at send) is repainted, and with a mask painted on it in the dialog (a red wash in the picture's own pixels; sent as white = change, black = keep) only that part changes; optional style picture; the world's nearest views go along unless switched off. The view, the mask and desktop drops are uploaded through `/imageviewer/timeline_upload` with a new name each time -> POST `/imageviewer/atlas_chisel` (image / mask / style resolved by `_atlas_picture`, `may_serve` + png/jpg/webp; the page never names a path to pass on) -> `atlas_render.start_chisel` -> the agentY host's `/agentY/atlas_chisel` (core's `atlas_chisel`, delivered like an Atlas render); the result is pushed into the History by `previzAtlasShow` ("Atlas chisel"). **Drops from Windows Explorer** for both Atlas dialogs are `imageViewer_atlasDrops.js` (`wirePictureDrop`: desktop Files and the History payload, anywhere on the dialog). Both dialogs are NOT modal (`floatDialog`, `.rd-float`: the shade takes no pointer events and has no tint, only the box does; the header drags the box), so the History strip stays usable and a thumbnail can be dragged in (Sebastian 2026-11-11); test `test_the_new_world_dialog_leaves_the_history_usable_…`. Tests `tests/test_atlas_render.py` (ChiselTests), `tests/test_atlas_create.mjs`, `tests/test_atlas_create_browser.py`, `tests/test_chisel_depth_browser.py` (real three.js depth of a box).
- **The Atlas menu, auto-orient, splat export** (Sebastian 2026-11-11). Every Atlas tool is in ONE menu, `previzAtlasMenuEntries` (`imageViewer_previzAtlasOrient.js`): the Previz panel's "Atlas ▾" button (`.previz-atlas-menu`, lit `.has-world` with a world open) and the bar's 3D menu as a submenu (an entry with `sub` in `_previzOpenMenu` opens `.previz-submenu` beside its row on hover / click; GROUPS items take `sub: (p) => entries`). **Auto-orient** (`imageViewer_atlasOrient.js`, pure, `tests/test_atlas_orient.mjs`): the mesh lies in the first meshed frame's camera frame, so a photo looking down gives a tilted floor. GET `/imageviewer/atlas_world?path=` (`atlas_render.world_cameras`, `path_access.is_allowed`) gives every meshed camera's right / up / fwd / pos in the mesh frame; up = square to all the cameras' level right axes (2+ cameras) or the first camera's up, refined by a RANSAC floor plane among the lowest mesh points (`findFloor`); the world item gets rotation (XYZ deg) + position so up is +Y, the first view looks level down -Z, the floor is at y = 0 (`placeWorld`), and unanimated top-level cameras are carried along (`worldDelta` / `carry`); one undo step. It runs when the world model loads (`hooks.onModelLoaded` -> `previzAtlasMaybeOrient`) if `item.atlasOrient.frames` (kept by `parseScene`) differs from world.json's meshed frame count - so on first open and after every added view - and from the menu. **Export Gaussian splat…** (`imageViewer_atlasSplat.js`): POST `/imageviewer/atlas_splat` (dry run = plan + price, then `confirm_cost`) -> the agentY host's `/agentY/atlas_splat` (`atlas_views_to_splat`, `open_in_viewer` off); the PLY joins the History (`focus: false`). Atlas renders also only join the History now (`previzAtlasShow` pushes `focus: false`): the 3D space stays up; "Add this view to the world" is on by default. Closing a 3D tab goes to the Viewer tab and, if that holds a 3D model (an Atlas world's world.glb lands there when made), opens its newest 2D snapshot (`_viewerTabTo2d`). Tests: `test_atlas_create_browser.py` (`test_the_atlas_menu_…`, `test_auto_orient_…`, `test_closing_a_3d_tab_…`), `test_atlas_render.py` (`WorldCameraTests`, `SplatRelayTests`).
- **An Atlas world's views, point cloud, Rebuild mesh** (Sebastian 2026-11-11, as World Labs' Viewfinder shows a world; `imageViewer_atlas3d.js` `Atlas3DMixin` on Model3DView, `imageViewer_previzAtlasViews.js` `PrevizAtlasViewsMixin` on the panel). `atlas_world` also answers `views`: every frame of world.json with a camera and a picture on disk (renders added to the world too, depth or not) with `image` (absolute), `aspect`, `fov` (vertical, from fy), `source`. The panel reads them whenever the world model (re)loads (`previzAtlasMaybeOrient` -> `previzAtlasLoadViews` -> `view.atlasSetViews(id, views)`), so a render added to the world appears after its reload. They are NOT scene items: a group `atlasviews` under the world's body (so it follows orient), per view a wire frustum (`imageViewerCameraHelper`, the Display menu's cameras) and the picture on a plane named `imageplane` at a share of the world's size in front (`_atlasViewDistance`); no raycast, hidden in every render (`_renderHideOverlays`) and the depth pass; `item.atlasViews === false` hides them. `item.atlasPoints`: each mesh of the world drawn as `THREE.Points` beside it (its own position attribute, colours read from its original material's map through a canvas, sRGB -> linear), the mesh hidden; `renderDepth` shows the meshes for the pass (`_atlasDepthBegin`). Both kept by `parseScene`, synced at the end of `applyFrame` (`_atlasSync`). Atlas menu: Show views, Show as point cloud, Camera from view ▸ (a scene camera parented to the world at that view's place and fov, gate = the picture's aspect), Rebuild mesh (POST `/imageviewer/atlas_rebuild`, `same_origin` + `is_allowed` -> `atlas_render.rebuild_mesh` -> the agentY host's `/agentY/atlas_rebuild`: agentY-core's `build_world_mesh` again from the depth maps, which since core #18 cuts the spikes at silhouettes / frame edges; then `reloadModel`). **Splat export**: `SPLAT_MIN_VIEWS` (12, World Labs' recommendation) is in the dialog's hint, and the plan line warns (`.rd-hint.warn`) below it; the dialog closes while the export runs (`_atlasSplatBusy`, `_atlasSplatRun`): it runs on, a notice says when the PLY is in the History, and reopening says it is running. Tests: `test_chisel_depth_browser.py` `test_an_atlas_world_shows_its_views_and_as_a_point_cloud` (real three.js), `test_atlas_create_browser.py` `test_the_splat_export_says_twelve_views_…`, `test_atlas_render.py` `test_views_are_…`, `RebuildRelayTests`.
- **The Channel Box is the Parameters panel** (Sebastian 2026-11-12; `_cbParams` / `_cbParamsBox` in `imageViewer_previzChannels.js`, called from `_enterModelMode` / `_exitModelMode`): no dock panel of its own any more. `#channels-panel` (`this.channelsPanel`, class `cb-params`, hidden outside the panel) is moved into `paramsPanel`; on a 3D tab the panel gets `.cb-mode` (ComfyUI's widgets, a tool's options, `.comp-params`, the lock and the ∿ button step aside; title "Channel Box"), the params monitor stands down on `_cbParamsMode`, and whether it is open is remembered apart from the other tabs (`_cbParamsWant`, restored to `_cbParamsOpenBefore` on the way out), as Comp's `_cpParams`. The dock id "channels" is an alias for "params" (`DOCK_ALIASES` in mixinDock: isPanelDocked / isPanelFront / setPanelDocked), so older callers and saved layouts still work. Test `test_the_channel_box_is_the_parameters_panel` in `tests/test_view3d_menubar_browser.py`.
- **Working keys and the Display menu** (Maya's way, clean-room; `imageViewer_view3dTools.js` `View3DToolsMixin` on Model3DView, `imageViewer_previzHotkeys.js` `PrevizHotkeysMixin` on the panel; test `tests/test_previz_hotkeys_browser.py`). F (the keymap's FitView on a previz tab -> `previzFKey`): over the Outliner (`_hoverTarget`, the container's last pointermove target) unfolds the selection's parents and scrolls to it (`previzRevealInOutliner`), else `frameItems(selection)` keeps the view direction and dollies to fit, nothing selected -> `resetView`. Q is the Select tool (`gizmoMode` "select": no gizmo, the primary boxed like the rest). A left press without Alt that misses a gizmo handle (and, with W / E / R, misses every object) starts a marquee (`_mqDown` / `idsInRect`: a vertex inside, or a 7x7 grid of rays); clicks and boxes go through `hooks.onMarquee` -> `previzSelectMany(ids, mode)` with Maya's modifiers (plain replace, Shift toggle, Ctrl remove, Ctrl+Shift add). S keys all channels, Shift+S the Channel Box's selected ones. K held (`previzKeyDown` before the keymap) + left drag scrubs: the view's width is the timeline's range. Typing into one of several selected Channel Box channels sets every one (`previzSetChannels`, relative forms per channel, one undo step). **Display** (the toolbar's Display menu, Shift+1..8 by `e.code`, commands `View3DShow*`): polygons, curves (rig controls, lines), joints, cameras, image planes, splats, point clouds, grid; kept per viewer in localStorage `imageviewer.viewer.display3d`. Hidden kinds are three.js LAYERS (`DISPLAY_KINDS`, sorted onto them in `_dispApply` before each viewport frame; the camera's and the picking ray's mask leave them out), not `visible` - except splats, which Spark draws by `visible`. A render (`beginRender`) enables every layer. Joints: per skinned model a LineSegments + Points in world space (`_jointsSync`), depth-tested unless X-Ray Joints, hidden in renders.
- **The menu bar, tool column, panes and axes** (Sebastian 2026-11-12, as Maya's / Blender's panel menus and Houdini's views). Model3DView's root is `.model-menubar` (30 px: `.model-mb-menus`, filled by the panel through `hooks.buildMenuBar` = `previzBuildMenuBar`, and `.model-mb-right` = shading select, Grid, Display ▾, the layout buttons) over `.model-stage` (canvas, gate, `.model-panes`; `_resize` / `_aspect` / `_gateRect` measure `this.stage`), plus `.model-toolcol` (Q W E R, Local / World, Pivot, Walk; `data-tool`). The Outliner is the tree only now. `imageViewer_previzMenuBar.js` (`PrevizMenuBarMixin`): File, Edit, Create, Rig, Animate, Constrain, World, View, Windows (`MENUS`), each built when it opens from the entry lists (`_previzFileMenuEntries`, `_previzEditMenuEntries`, `_previzAddMenuEntries`, `_previzRigMenuEntries(anchor)` sorted by `rigMenuOf` - "file" is the character exports -, `_previzConstrainMenuEntries`, `previzAtlasMenuEntries`); buttons keep the old Outliner classes (`previz-rig`, `previz-anim`, `previz-constrain`, `previz-atlas-menu`, `previz-mb-file` ...); one open, hovering another opens it; `previzMenuBarOpen(name)`. **Panes** (`imageViewer_view3dPanes.js`, pure `paneRects` / `cleanPaneState` / `setSlotView` / `axesLayout` tested in `tests/test_view3d_panes.mjs`, `View3DPanesMixin` on Model3DView): ONE canvas, each pane drawn by setViewport / setScissor (`_paneRenderAll`, the canvas cleared #111 first); layouts single / 2h / 2v / 3 / quad, default views top, persp, front, right (`PANE_VIEWS`, `PANE_LAYOUTS`), a view in one pane only (choosing it elsewhere swaps), localStorage `imageviewer.viewer.view3dPanes`. The pane under the mouse is active (capture listeners on the stage) and the CANVAS answers its box (`getBoundingClientRect` / `clientWidth` / `clientHeight` overridden in `_paneHookCanvas`), so the orbit controls, gizmo, picking and marquee work unchanged; the gizmos are put on each pane's camera by `_paneGizmoFit`, which scales their size by stage height / pane height (three sizes the handles - and their pick shapes - as a share of the view's height) and, after the panes are drawn, makes their handles again for the active pane, which the hover and press test (test `tests/test_view3d_pane_gizmo_browser.py`); `activeCameraObject()` is the pane's camera (`_perspCameraObject` the old one), flat views an OrthographicCamera per kind (`_paneCams`, framed by `_paneFrameOrtho`), no tumble, Alt+left pans, the grid turned per pane; the gate only in `_panePlain`; `reportCamera` skipped for flat panes. **Axes**: an SVG per pane bottom right (`_paneAxesDraw` every tick from `axesLayout(camera quaternion)`), a click = `lookAlongAxis` (a flat pane switches to that view), a drag tumbles. **Keys in the menus**: an entry's `cmd` (a keymap action's short name; `viewerActionKeyLabel` gives its effective combo, so rebinds show) or `keys` (written out, e.g. Shift+1, which goes by `e.code`) becomes `data-key` on the row, drawn by `.previz-menu-item[data-key]::after` (the row's textContent stays its label). Animate starts with `_previzKeyMenuEntries`, Edit has a Tool submenu. **Space over a pane** (`View3DMaximisePane`, before PlayPause in the keymap, the first enabled action of a combo wins): `paneSpaceTarget` (the stage's `_paneHoverXY`) = the pane in a split layout, "restore" in a pane maximised from one (`s.restore`, which `setPaneLayout` clears when a layout is picked by hand), else null and Space plays; key repeat ignored. Browser test `tests/test_view3d_menubar_browser.py`. Not done: world pins / overlays placed per pane in multi-view. **Grid options** (Sebastian 2026-11-12; `imageViewer_view3dGrid.js`, `View3DGridMixin` on Model3DView, pure `cleanGridOptions` / `gridLayout` tested in `tests/test_view3d_grid.mjs`): size (edge to edge), spacing, a major line every N cells, "Scale with the model" (the old powers-of-ten fit on Frame all, `_gridSetAutoScale`; off = scale 1); localStorage `imageviewer.viewer.grid3d`; opened from the `.model-grid-more` caret beside Grid and View > Grid options… (`toggleGridMenu`, `.model-grid-menu`, the Display menu's box). The grid is a LineSegments with vertex colours (`_gridMake`, `type` "GridHelper", name "grid"), rebuilt in place by `_gridRebuild` (keeps visible, rotation, layers); at most `MAX_LINES` lines (the spacing grows). **File > Import Model…** (`previzImportModel`, keymap `PrevizAddModel`): the File Browser's selected models go in at once, else `openFilePicker` (multiple, `MODEL_IMPORT_EXTS`); Create has no Model… any more. Tests `test_the_grid_has_a_size_and_a_spacing`, `test_models_come_in_through_file_import_model` (screenshots with `VIEW3D_SHOTS=<folder>`).
- **A camera's look-at target; the world origin on the grid** (Sebastian 2026-11-12, clean-room). The bug behind it: `_tick` ran `controls.update()` every frame, and OrbitControls' update makes its object look at the controls' centre, so a scene camera looked through ALWAYS faced that point (its rotation and roll overruled). Now `_tick` skips update (and damping) while the controls drive a scene camera (`userData.imageViewerItemId`); the controls still update on each drag step, and `_aimNavStart` (controls `start`, `_tumbleStart`) puts their centre on the camera's view axis and remembers its roll, `_aimNavChange` (controls `change`) puts the roll back (`imageViewer_cameraAim3d.js`, `CameraAim3DMixin` on Model3DView). **Look At** (`imageViewer_cameraAim.js`, pure, tests `tests/test_camera_aim.mjs`): `item.aim = {on, target}` (world space; `readAim` in `parseScene`, absent = off), the target's numbers are extra channels `aim:x` / `aim:y` / `aim:z` (static reader / writer registered in scene3d, keyable). The Channel Box's camera shape has a "Look At" on / off row (default off) and, when on, Target X / Y / Z and Roll (`aim:roll`, degrees, `item.aim.roll`: the turn about the line to the target; the Rotate channels are left alone while aimed, and the Rotate tool's drag on an aimed camera writes Roll - `transform.aimRoll`; switching on starts Roll at the camera's current roll) (`previzSetAimOn`: on puts the target where the camera already looks - `defaultAimTarget`, the orbit centre's distance when looked through -, off writes the aimed direction into its rotation). The view: `_aimApply` in `applyFrame` turns the camera to the target keeping its roll (`_aimRollOf`: twist about the view axis against looking level with +Y up), `_aimSync` draws a cross + line (`entry.aimViz`, "cameras" display kind, hidden in renders) and puts a second TransformControls (`_aimGizmo`, translate, world) on `_aimHandle` while that camera is selected, in every tool; a drag -> `hooks.onAimTarget` -> `previzSetAimTarget` (one undo step). `_mqDown` / `_onPointerUp` leave a press on it alone (`_aimGizmoHot`). Navigating through an aimed camera orbits its target and reports it (`_aimReported` -> `transform.aimTarget`, written by `previzApplyTransform`). **Show origin** (grid options, default on, `origin` in `imageviewer.viewer.grid3d`): `gridOrigin` beside the grid (not a child, so a flat pane's turned grid leaves it in world axes): X red and Z blue across the grid, a two-cell green Y stub, a white dot (`originLines`, `ORIGIN_COLORS`, `_gridOriginSync` - shown with the grid, scaled with it, re-added if the scene was made again). Browser test `tests/test_camera_aim_browser.py`.
- **The Outliner's tree, search and filter** (Sebastian 2026-11-12; clean-room, its own look: 22 px rows, stripes and shades in white over the panel's ground so presets carry it, an indent guide per level, a Lucide icon per kind coloured by kind, chevron arrows, the eye / look-through at the right). `imageViewer_outlinerData.js` is pure (`outlinerRows`, `boneRows`, `OUTLINER_KINDS` / `OUTLINER_STATES`, `cleanOutlinerFilter`; tests `tests/test_outliner_data.mjs`); `imageViewer_previzOutliner.js` (`PrevizOutlinerMixin`, `Object.assign`ed onto PrevizMixin at the end of mixinPreviz) draws it: `_olBuildHead` (`.ol-head`: `.ol-search`, `.ol-filter-btn`), `_olDrawTree` (from `_previzRenderPanel`), `_previzOutlinerRow` / `_previzSkeletonRows` / `_previzBonesOf` (moved here), the filter popover `.ol-menu` (`_olToggleFilterMenu`). A search keeps matches (items, or a model through its bones) and their parents (`.context`, greyed), all unfolded; Enter = `previzOutlinerSelectMatches`. A hidden kind's children are drawn a level up. Filter in localStorage `imageviewer.viewer.outlinerFilter` ({hidden, state}); the search is not kept. Shift+click on an arrow folds the branch (`descendantIds`); `previzOutlinerFoldAll`. Row clicks (`_olClickSelect`, Sebastian 2026-11-12, as the Curve Editor's channel list): plain selects, Ctrl toggles, Shift selects every visible row (`data-select-id`, FK bone rows by their control) from the anchor (`_olAnchor`, the last click without Shift) to the clicked one, Ctrl+Shift adds it (`previzSelectMany(ids, mode, {primary})`). A right-click on the empty space opens Create ▸ and Import Model… (`_previzOutlinerSpaceMenuEntries`). The keyframe dot is `.ol-keys` (`.previz-anim` is the Animate menu's button class). Browser test `tests/test_outliner_browser.py` (`OUTLINER_SHOTS=<dir>` saves screenshots).
- **Framing masks** (Sebastian 2026-11-12; `imageViewer_frameMasks.js`, `FrameMasksMixin`; pure `parseRatio` / `maskRect` / `maskOf` / `cleanState` tested in `tests/test_frame_masks.mjs`, the panel in `tests/test_frame_masks_browser.py`, `FRAME_MASK_SHOTS=<dir>` for screenshots). The picture dimmed outside an aspect ratio (`FRAME_MASKS` presets or a typed one, "2.2" / "3:2"), opacity 0..1, edge line, ratio label; one mask at a time, localStorage `imageviewer.viewer.frameMasks`. Button `#frame-mask-btn` + caret `#frame-mask-more-btn` right of Toggle Overlays (menu `#frame-mask-menu`), keymap `ToggleFrameMask` Shift+G. Drawn as a clipping box with a hole whose spread box-shadow is the shade: the Viewer's `#frame-mask` is placed by `frameMaskSync(box)` from `updateImageFrame` (the picture's untransformed box taken through zoom / pan, so the line stays 1 px) and serves a cut's tab in Edit too; in 3D Model3DView's `_gateTell` -> `hooks.onGate` -> `_frameMaskGate` puts one in the stage at the camera gate, the whole stage without a camera, none with split panes. Hidden with the overlays off and in contact / model / view / paint / motion / comp modes (imageViewer.css).
- **Lights** (Sebastian 2026-11-12; the existing world kind `light`, now with a `type`; data in `imageViewer_worldData.js` - `LIGHT_TYPES` point / spot / directional / area / dome, `LIGHT_DEFAULTS`, `SHADOW_TYPES`, `lightSettings`, `makeLightItem(id, type)` (no type = the World kit's Lamp: a point light in a glowing tube, its old numbers), `worldFields(item)` (a light's Channel Box rows are its type's: `types` / `fixture` on `WORLD_FIELDS.light`), `studioLightsOn(scene, env)`; tests `tests/test_world_lights.mjs`; browser `tests/test_view3d_lights_browser.py`, `LIGHTS_SHOTS=<dir>` for screenshots). Built by `_buildLight` (`imageViewer_world3d.js`) as PLAIN three.js lights - PointLight, SpotLight, DirectionalLight, RectAreaLight, HemisphereLight - on purpose, so a path tracer reading the three.js scene (the renderer assessment thread's three-gpu-pathtracer) takes them as they are; never a custom or shader-only light. Spot / directional / area shine along the item's -Y (target a child at (0,-1,0); the RectAreaLight turned -90° about X), so the item's turn aims them; a dome is added to the scene itself, straight up (`worldExtras`). Area lights load `vendor/three/RectAreaLightUniformsLib.js` (+ its 300 kB LTC tables) the first time (`loadRectArea`). A light without a fixture has a yellow line glyph (`_lightGlyph`, `imageViewerLightGlyph`): pickable, on the Display menu's 9th kind "lights" (layer 8, Shift+9), hidden in renders unless helpers are kept. `_lightsFrame` (every viewport frame from `_tick`, every `renderFrame`, and `_worldSyncStudio`): ComfyUI's studio rig (`this.lights`) is on only by `studioLightsOn` - scene `defaultLights` "on" / "off" or, left out, auto: off while the scene has a visible light or an environment (View > Default lights, `previzSetDefaultLights`, one undo step, kept by `parseScene`); the shadow map is on with an environment or a shadowing light (PCFShadowMap then, so `softness` = `shadow.radius` works; PCFSoft for an environment alone); `_lightsSync` makes every non-world mesh cast and take shadows while a light shadows (`imageViewerNoShadow` keeps fixtures and glyphs out) and fits a directional light's shadow box round the scene. Create > Light ▸ (`_previzLightMenuEntries`, `previzAddLight(type)`); changing Type takes the new type's intensity (`previzSetWorldField`). Not done: keying a light's numbers, the agent's scene ops and USD export do not know lights.
- **History items dropped into the 3D space** (Sebastian 2026-11-12): on a 3D tab the viewport's drop of the `application/x-imageviewer-history` payload (History thumbnails, file browser rows) goes to `previzAddDropped` (the Outliner's drop too), one undo step for the lot: models, splats and 3D files (`_frameIsModel`) become model items as File > Import Model makes them, pictures and FILMS image planes (shaped to the picture: `_buildImagePlane`, a unit tall), standing on the floor at the view's focus, each in the next free place along X (2 sizes apart, so drops one after another do not overlap), at the scene's size (`max(1, view._gridAutoScale)`: a scene in centimetres gets planes 100 tall); items naming only a ComfyUI file (filename / subfolder / type) come in as they are, blob: ones are copied into ./input/3d (`_previzSrcFor`); a sequence comes in as the frame dragged; sound is left out (a notice). Other spaces are unchanged. **A film on an image plane** (`imageViewer_view3dVideoPlane.js`, `View3DVideoPlaneMixin` on Model3DView; pure `isVideoSrc` / `videoTime` in `tests/test_view3d_video_plane.mjs`): a muted `<video>` on a `VideoTexture` (`entry.video`), never played - `applyFrame` seeks it to the scene frame (`_videoPlaneSeek`: frame 0 is its first frame, `item.plane.offset` shifts that, the last frame held past its end), and `previzRender` awaits `prepareVideoPlanes(f)` before each frame so a render shows the frame asked for. Browser test `tests/test_view3d_drop_history_browser.py` (real webm / png made with ffmpeg, `HARNESS_MEDIA_DIR`).
- three.js is **vendored in `vendor/three/`, deliberately outside `js/`**, so ComfyUI does not serve or import it at startup; it is fetched on demand through `/imageviewer/lib/three/<name>`.
- **Gaussian splats** (.spz, .splat, .ksplat, .sog, and a .ply whose vertices carry `scale_*` + `rot_*` — `isSplatPly`, ComfyUI's own rule) are drawn by Spark (`vendor/three/spark.module.js`, loaded with the first splat) as `SplatMesh` objects in the ordinary scene, turned 180° about X as ComfyUI's viewer turns them. One `SparkRenderer` per view (`_ensureSpark`, remade with the WebGL renderer). A splat has no geometry in three's sense, so a hidden `splatbounds` box beside it is what framing and picking measure. Server side, `model_writer` saves an in-memory `SPLAT` as .ply through core's `SplatToFile3D`, and never converts a splat to a mesh format (`is_splat_file`).
- **Path traced, the second renderer** (Sebastian 2026-11-12; `imageViewer_view3dPathTrace.js`, `View3DPathTraceMixin` on Model3DView; three-gpu-pathtracer 0.0.24 + three-mesh-bvh, MIT, in `vendor/three/`, loaded on first use; tests `tests/test_path_trace_settings.mjs`, `tests/test_path_trace_browser.py` - SwiftShader traces on the CPU, so few samples). A shading mode, `materialMode` "pathtraced" (`MATERIAL_MODES`, meshes keep their original materials, keymap `View3DPathtraced`), and the render dialog's Renderer (`scene.render.renderer` raster | pathtraced, `samples`, `bounces`; `RENDERERS`, `previz_shot.RENDER_KEYS`, the agent's `_asa_render`). Viewport: `_tick` -> `_ptViewportTick(cam)` (null = draw raster: not loaded, failed, moving; in a split layout `_paneRenderAll` calls `_ptViewportTick(cam, pane)` for the ACTIVE pane only - its viewport / scissor set, the tracer sized to it by `_ptSetSize` (`synchronizeRenderSize` off, the tracer's own `_pathTracer` / `_lowResPathTracer`), `setCamera` when another pane becomes active, `_ptMore` asks for the next tick, the badge says "(active view)"; the other panes raster): `_ptSync` compares signatures (meshes + matrices + materials + skinned bones + frame / lights + env / camera + size) and calls `setScene` / `updateLights` / `updateCamera`; one `renderSample` (2x2 tiles) per tick until `PT_VIEW_MAX`; `.model-pt-badge` counts samples. "Raster while moving" (View menu, off by default, localStorage `imageviewer.viewer.ptRasterWhileMoving`, `ptRasterWhileMoving` / `ptSetRasterWhileMoving`): `_ptMoving(cam)` - the scene signature changed since the last tick, or the gizmo is dragging - draws raster (no `setScene` per frame) until `PT_RESUME_MS` after the last change, a timer asking for the frame that resumes. What is traced = `_ptContent(cam)`: visible meshes on shown display layers outside `_ptOverlayRoots()` (grid, gizmo, pivot mark, pins, selection boxes, joints, Atlas views, helpers / axes, rig controls, templates, splats and their bounds); the rest is hidden around `setScene`. `_ptDrawOverlays` then draws everything else raster over the traced picture: a depth-only pass of the traced meshes (`colorWrite: false` override), then the scene without them, `autoClear` off. `_ptWithSceneLook`: no `scene.environment` and the studio ambient VISIBLE (default lights on) -> a `GradientEquirectTexture` at the ambient's strength, else no environment (default lights off: only the scene's own lights); the clear colour as background unless transparent. `_ptFillLights`: the default rig's directional fills are not traced (key + environment only), nor lights under a hidden parent (`_ptUnseenLights`: the tracer reads a light's own `visible` only; `_ptLightSig` uses the effective visibility). Renders: `renderFramePathTraced(frame, w, h, {samples, bounces, shouldStop, onSample})`, async, inside beginRender / endRender, no supersampling (`aa` 1), bloom not applied; `previzRender` awaits it per frame. Not traced: splats, custom shaders (clay / normal / wireframe stay raster), ambient and hemisphere lights; no depth of field yet (`PhysicalCamera`). The WebGPU tracer (0.0.25+, OIDN denoiser) needs three r185.

### Server side

- `viewer_api.py` — all `/imageviewer/*` routes, registered at import. Everything taking a path is filtered through `path_access.py`, which confines reads to ComfyUI's input/output/temp plus folders the user allows (`~/.comfyui_image_viewer/roots.txt` — per OS user, NOT in ComfyUI's folder: a launcher's `git stash -u` there swept the old `bepic_viewer_roots.txt` away at every start; that file is still read and adopted — or `IMAGE_VIEWER_ROOTS`). Routes with side effects are POST only. `/imageviewer/roots` (the settings page's folder editor) is the one route that widens that list, so it is fenced by `path_access.may_edit`: same machine (loopback, or the server's own address), ComfyUI's own Origin, JSON POST; `IMAGE_VIEWER_ROOTS_REMOTE_EDIT=1` allows other machines, `IMAGE_VIEWER_ROOTS_LOCKED=1` none; the Host must be this machine's address or name (DNS rebinding). **A route that hands back a file's bytes serves media only** (`path_access.may_serve`: `MEDIA_EXTS`, which includes a glTF's `.bin` / `.mtl`): listing and writing need `is_allowed`, returning content needs `may_serve`. The list never holds a drive root, the home folder or above, a system folder or a hidden one (`too_broad`, enforced when read as well as when edited), and inside an added folder nothing below a dot folder or AppData is allowed. The Comfy registry's reviewer judges exactly this: versions were flagged `arbitrary-file-read`, `path-traversal`, `credential-exfiltration` (read the verdict in `status_reason.message`, `?include_status_reason=true`). **No route may start a process on the host** — the Explorer-opening routes were removed for exactly this reason, and the ComfyUI registry rejected the repo while they existed.
- `usd_io.py` / `abc_io.py` — USD stages and Alembic caches. The viewport speaks glTF only, so both are flattened server-side into a cached GLB "display proxy" and served through the ordinary file route. `abc_io.py` reads the Ogawa container itself (no Alembic bindings exist on PyPI); an Alembic cache is geometry per frame, so its proxy is keyed by `frame`.
- `color_io.py` — the viewer's colour transform: input colourspace → sRGB through OpenColorIO (`$OCIO`, else the built-in config; sRGB and Linear in numpy without it). The target is fixed (the config's sRGB output, the rule `bepic_templates`' colour node starts on); the input is picked in the viewer (`imageViewer_color.js`, the menu in the exposure bar) and travels as `cs=` on `/imageviewer/raw_view`, `/imageviewer/view_file` and `/imageviewer/thumb`. `media_resolve.proxy_for_colorspace` converts and caches one file per source and colourspace (JPEG, or PNG with alpha); without `cs` the older fixed proxy (`proxy_for_display`) still answers. The node therefore hands the viewer the saved EXRs themselves and writes no PNG copies. `buildImgUrl(obj, {noColor: true})` is for anything that is not the picture being looked at. A converted picture takes the **exposure** on the server too (`ev=`, a tenth of a stop per cache entry): input → working linear, multiply, → sRGB. `applyExposure`'s CSS brightness makes up only the stops the element on screen does not already carry (`el._imageViewerEv`, read off the loaded URL in `_setImgSrcCached`), which is all of them for a picture shown as it is and the live preview while a converted frame is on its way (`queueExposureReload`).
- `timeline_io.py` — the cut that lies beside a film, and the **Edit panel** (dock id `editTimeline`, the timeline button in the transport; `imageViewer_editTimeline.js`). A cut is an OpenTimelineIO file; one lies beside a film of the same name (`cut.mp4` / `cut.otio`; agentY's edit stage writes one, and so can Resolve or Premiere), one is started in the panel (New, or the first clips added). **Every open cut has a tab of its own** (key `edit:…`, no media frames, kept by the stale-tab sweep) **and plays there before anything is rendered** - see *Playing a cut* below. On a film with a cut beside it the timeline button lights up and opens that cut's tab; it is not opened by itself. The panel shows the cut of the tab that is up (`_editTlTab`; the others wait in `_editTlCuts`, all of them in localStorage `imageviewer.viewer.edits`, each with how it is looked at - zoom, track heights, solo - as `view`). **The Edit panels - Edit, Inspector, Effects, Mixer, Source (`EDIT_PANELS`) - are up only while a cut's tab is**: `switchTab` hides them for any other tab (`editTlTabHidden`) and brings back the ones that were open (`_editTlWanted`, localStorage `imageviewer.viewer.editPanels`) with the next cut. The Edit panel is a column of track headers that stays and, beside it, a strip as wide as the zoom makes it (`view.zoom`, Ctrl+wheel about the pointer, the wheel or the middle button to pan): a ruler (drag to scrub) that stays on top, then two sections that scroll up and down on their own - the video tracks (texts, the higher video tracks, V1) above a splitter, the sound (A1, the sound tracks) below it; see *The timeline's ruler, readout, sections and heights* below. A header carries the track's switch (off: saved, not rendered), its solo (`view.solo`, a way of looking: played, neither saved nor rendered - `liveTracks`) and, on its lower edge, its height. A clip is coloured by right-click or in the Inspector (`metadata.imageviewer.color` in the file). A dissolve is a bar over its join (`.edit-mix`, selection kind `mix`): its edges resize it, the Inspector has its length. A clip's body is dragged along its track or onto another one (`_editTlTrackUnder`, `transferClip`; a ghost shows where it would land): onto V1 it lies over what is there (an overwrite, `overwriteV1`; Ctrl+Shift: between the shots, an insert), onto any other track it is placed; off V1 it leaves a gap (`liftV1`). It is an editor: drag a shot's edge to trim, a shot to move, a text to move; drop rows of the file browser / history thumbs (the `application/x-imageviewer-history` payload) or a node dragged off the canvas (the page is watched for the release - LiteGraph keeps the pointer - and the node is put back) to add clips. **Tracks** (`cut.tracks`, `timeline_io.extra_tracks`): V1 is the cut itself - one shot after the other, dissolves, and GAPS (see *Modes, gaps, linked sound, snapping* below) - and A1 the bed under the whole film; every further track holds clips that are PLACED (`at`), and nothing moves when one does. A clip on a higher video track covers what is under it, **picture and sound** (the player has one element sounding at a time, and `timeline_render.covered` / `film_sound` follow the same rule so that the film is what was played); sound-track clips lie under everything. In the file they are OTIO tracks with gaps, the first audio track always being A1 (an empty one is written when a cut has sound tracks but no bed, or the first of them would be read back as the bed). Two clips of one track that share a moment: the later one is on top in the page, and on save the earlier ends where the later begins. An edit without a `tracks` / `off` key (an older page) leaves what the file holds of them alone. The numbers of the selection are in the **Inspector** and transitions, texts and plain colours in **Effects** (`imageViewer_editPanels.js`): the Inspector is in the viewer's own look (the panels' ground, the Parameters header, the Edit panel's buttons, cyan numbers - on purpose not any other program's; it was restyled once for looking like one) and holds the clip's name, tabs (Video / Audio / Timing / File; Title for a text, Transition for a dissolve), sections with a switch and a reset, a reset per row - and every row is something the preview plays and the render writes, no control that only looks like one. **A clip's transform, cropping and opacity** (`clip.fx`, `metadata.imageviewer.fx`; the table `FX` is in `timeline_io.py` and in the data module, and the two must agree): zoom, position and anchor point as fractions of the frame (y UP), rotation in degrees clockwise, a crop per side, opacity; `toff` / `coff` / `ooff` switch a section off and keep its numbers (`clipFx` is the effect as DRAWN, `rawFx` as its numbers stand). One arithmetic, twice: `fxPlace` gives the layer its CSS transform (put after the viewer's own) and a clip-path that is the frame taken back through that transform and cut to the crop; `timeline_render.lay` does the same with an affine transform and an alpha that is the picture's box less its crop - a change to one is a change to the other, and `fx_check`-style pixel tests of the render are cheap to rebuild. A clip on a higher track that no longer covers the frame has what is beneath it as `under` with `over: true` (`pictureAt`) and the player draws that ONE picture beneath, as it does for a dissolve; the render draws them all. A slider is live while dragged (`editPlayFxLive`, which changes no cut - a redraw would take the slider from under the pointer) and commits when let go. Not there, rather than there and dead: pitch and yaw, crop softness, dynamic zoom, speed, keyframes. An Effects item is dragged onto the timeline (`application/x-imageviewer-effect`) or double-clicked; a plain colour is a PNG the server makes (POST `/imageviewer/timeline_solid`, one file per colour and size), because a clip is a piece of a file; a text made here is a marker with no `ref`, drawn at once. Save (POST `/imageviewer/timeline`) writes the cut into the `.otio` and stamps it (`metadata.imageviewer.edited`); the film it lies beside is not touched. A tab closed with unsaved changes says so once and stays. **Render** (POST `/imageviewer/timeline_render`, `timeline_render.py`) makes the film of the saved cut in this process through PyAV - a ComfyUI dependency, so no program is started and the no-process rule holds: shots scaled to fit and padded, a dissolve blended frame by frame, the shots' own sound and the bed mixed in memory (hence `MAX_SECONDS`). It never writes over the film a cut lies beside (that may be somebody's render): the result lands by the export rule as the next version, with its own `.otio` marked `rendered`. **Titles** are clips (see *Titles* below); the render draws them (`timeline_render.title_frames`, through `title_render`), the Edit tab draws them while it plays, the same way. `texts_left_out` stays in the answer, at 0; `notes` says when a title's font was not on the machine. With no film, dropped clips start a new cut, saved by the export rule (kind `edit`). Rules worth knowing: the panel works on clips at FULL length with the dissolve leading into each (`timeline_io.editable`), OTIO lets clips meet mid-dissolve (`rebuild`) - a dissolve is split into whole frames that add up, or the film comes back a frame short; an existing clip is saved by `ref` (its place in the file) so everything another program wrote into it survives, a new one by `file`, which must pass `path_access.is_allowed`; a clip's length comes from POST `/imageviewer/timeline_probe` (PyAV reads the container header in this process - the server may not start a process to ask), and only where that fails from the browser (`<video>` metadata). `imageViewer_editTimelineData.js` is the arithmetic (pure, every edit returns a new cut; run it under node after a change).
- **Cutting like an editor** (`imageViewer_editKeys.js`, `imageViewer_editMedia.js`, `imageViewer_editAuto.js`, all mixins onto the panel; their arithmetic in `imageViewer_editTimelineData.js`, tested in `tests/test_edit_timeline_data.mjs`, the panel in `tests/test_edit_timeline_browser.py` against `tests/harness/edit.html`). **Undo**: every change of the cut goes through `_editTlApply`, which pushes the cut it replaces onto a stack per cut's tab (`_editTlHistories`, `historyPush` / `historyUndo` / `historyRedo`; not kept across a reload); a drag is one step - `_editTlDrag` records the cut of the press when it is let go, and `_editTlApply` stands down while `_editTlDragging`. A save renumbers the file's items, so the stacks are `detach`ed then (their clips held by `file`, not `ref`). **The blade** (`_editTlBlade`, toolbar ✂ or B) cuts the clip clicked (`splitClip`; both halves keep the clip's `ref` - `rebuild` deep-copies the item it names, so another program's metadata survives in both; no dissolve is made; refused in a dissolve or a frame from an end); Ctrl+B / S cut at the playhead (`splitAt`: the selected clip, else every clip there); Del `liftClip` (a gap stays - on V1 too, `liftV1`; a selected gap is closed), Shift+Del `rippleDelete`; a clip linked to its sound / picture takes it along (`deleteWithLink`); the blade cuts a linked pair together (`splitWithLink`). **Keys** (`editTlKeyDown`, asked first by `handleKeyDown`, which runs on the viewer's window, popped out or not): they act only while the composed path holds the panel's body - it takes the focus on a press (`tabIndex`, a capture `pointerdown`) - and never in a field (`handleKeyDown`'s `isTyping`, which now counts a SELECT); a key it takes is `stopImmediatePropagation`ed, so ComfyUI's own Ctrl+Z never sees it. J runs backwards on a timer, I / O set the transport's own `playbackRange` (see *In and out* below), Up / Down go to `editPoints`. **Drops** (`_editTlAdd`): the history payload, desktop files (`Files`; copied by POST `/imageviewer/timeline_upload` into `<input>/edits` - media only, base name made safe, same name and size used again, never written over; a viewport drop on an Edit tab does the same), ComfyUI queue / assets pictures (`text/uri-list` of its `/view`, found by POST `/imageviewer/timeline_locate`, as are history items that only have `filename`), canvas nodes (the track under the release is used too). Films and pictures go on V1 between the shots or placed on a video track; sound always to a sound track (the one dropped on, the first, or a new one). A drop snaps (`_editTlSnap`, through the one snapping - `_editTlSnapAt`) and the target row is lit (`.landing`); a drop inside a V1 gap is placed at that moment (`insertAt`), elsewhere on V1 it is an insert. Lengths come from POST `/imageviewer/timeline_probe` (PyAV, the container header) and only then from a `<video>`. **Inside the clips** (`editMediaSchedule` after every refresh, scroll, zoom, resize, arriving reading): a canvas per VISIBLE clip, as wide as its visible part; a filmstrip - tiles at fixed places along the clip, each the frame of its moment of in..out, the sheet's count rounded to `STRIP_COUNTS` from the zoom and drawn from the nearest loaded count until the right one arrives - with the waveform on its lower 36% when the film has sound; a clip that is a PART of its file (a piece after the blade, two clips of one file, a slip) asks for a sheet of its own part - `start` / `end` on `/imageviewer/timeline_strip` (`timeline_media.strip_window`, cached per window, `X-Strip-Start` / `-End`), a window around its source in..out on a grid of a power of two about half its length (`stripWindow`), so a small slip or trim stays on one sheet; the whole-file sheet (or the nearest sheet that covers the part, `_emNearest(.., part)`) stands in while it comes, and during a drag (`_editTlDragging`) nothing new is asked for; the peaks are of the whole file at up to 400 buckets a second and each clip reads its own source moments, so the waveform needed no change (`_emTiles` / `_emSheet` / `_emWaveRange` on a block say what was drawn - `test_17` reads them); a sound clip (and the bed) is all waveform, scaled by its volume. At most 3 readings in the air (1 while playing), the ones no visible clip wants are aborted at the next draw, the cache is capped (`MAX_ENTRIES`); `_emPending` / `_emDrawn` say how far a draw got (the tests wait on them). Server side `timeline_media.py`: GET `/imageviewer/timeline_peaks` (min/max int8 per bucket at 25/100/400 per second), GET `/imageviewer/timeline_strip` (n frames on one JPEG, layout in `X-Strip-*` headers), GET `/imageviewer/timeline_beats`, GET `/imageviewer/timeline_shots`, GET `/imageviewer/timeline_cuts`; all `may_serve`-guarded (path or filename/type/subfolder), cached in ComfyUI's temp as `ImageViewer_<kind>_<stem>_<sha1 of path|mtime|size>`, with an ETag (304), computed in a pool of 2 threads of their own (`_media_work`) so the default executor stays free for the picture being played. **Auto edit** (dialog `.edit-dialog` inside the panel's body - popout-safe): `cutOnBeats` / `snapCutsToBeats` (beats of the bed or a sound clip: `beatsInFilm`; agentY's `read_beats` ported - numpy STFT in place of scipy's, FFT autocorrelation, same numbers), `evenRhythm` / `fitLength`, `rhythmFrom` (agentY's `read_rhythm`: ffmpeg's scene score measured on 64x36 grey frames), `bestPart`; shots never longer than their file (`room`, from probe durations); one undo step, `describeChanges` says what changed. **Detect cuts** (the bar's wand menu, a clip's menu, or the dialog): `/imageviewer/timeline_cuts` = `detect_cuts` - hard cuts are peaks of the scene score that stand `ratio` above the median around them (adaptive) and are spikes, not steps (the first frame of a dissolve is a step); dissolves / fades are humps of the half-second difference (`long`) with no hard cut in them, their middle the hump's centroid less half a span. The panel shows `cutMarks` (inside in..out, score ≥ `cutThreshold(sensitivity)`, ratio ≥ 2.5) as `.edit-cutmark`s with a slider in `.edit-cutbar`; a click leaves one out; "Split at all" is `splitAtMany` per clip, one step. Any change of the cut drops the marks. **Ask agentY…**: saves the cut, then `window.agentYViewerBridge.feedback({text})` with the brief and the `.otio`'s path (the timeline routes now answer `otio`, its absolute path) and the edit_timeline `load` op to use; `agentLoses` lists what agentY's `from_otio` drops (extra tracks, `fx`, text looks) as a warning in the dialog and in the message. The next cut tab that opens (agentY's render, `panel="edit"`) is taken as the answer: its bar offers "Take into <cut>" (`editTlTakeAnswer`: the answer `detach`ed into the asking cut, one undo step).
- **Trimming like an editor; in and out** (`imageViewer_editTrim.js`, `EditTrimMixin`; arithmetic in the data module, tested in `tests/test_edit_timeline_trim.mjs`, the panel in `tests/test_edit_timeline_browser.py`). What a press on a clip does is decided by `_editTlEdgeAct` (where it lands, what is held) and shown BEFORE the press by `_editTlHover`: an editor's cursor (SVG data-URL cursors, `TRIM_CURSORS`), the side that will move lit (`.edit-trimhl`, `data-side`), and a tooltip with the edit point's timecode (`.edit-trimtip`, `D.timecode`, HH:MM:SS:FF). Within `EDGE_PX` of a block's edge: left of a V1 cut is the outgoing shot's OUT (`trimEnd`, ripple), right of it the incoming IN (`trimStart`, ripple); within `ROLL_PX` of the join (the middle zone - chosen over a modifier, so it needs no key) it is a ROLL (`rollEdit`: the cut moves, the film keeps its length; not on a join with a dissolve - there the dissolve's bar is on top: its edges change its length, its body rolls the cut under it, `_editTlMixRoll`). Modifiers: Alt on the body = SLIP (`slipClip`, in and out move together, the picture follows the pointer, held to the source, `.edit-slipband` shows where the source lies; `,` / `.` a frame, Shift a second - by `e.code`, so the layout does not matter); Alt+Shift on a V1 body = SLIDE (`slideClip`: the shot keeps its source and length and moves, the neighbour before ends later and the one after begins later in its source; refused at either end of the cut; on placed tracks a plain drag already moves a clip keeping its length); Alt on an edge = RETIME (`retimeClip`, "trim to fill": source in / out kept, `speed` = what fills the new length, 10 %..1000 %); Ctrl on the out-edge = LOOP EXTEND (`loopExtend`; Ctrl+L / Ctrl+Shift+L one more turn / ping-pong, `loopOnce`). Every trim knows where its source ends (`_editTlDur` from `/imageviewer/timeline_probe`, asked once per file in the background by `_editTlKnowFiles`): `outRoom` / `inRoom` hold trims, rolls and slides there; a still (`isStillImage`) has no source length, so it grows as far as it is pulled at either edge. Each drag, key and Inspector field is one undo step (`_editTlDrag` records the press's cut). Edge drags snap their moving edge (`_editTlTrimPoint`, through `_editTlSnapAt`). **Speed and loop on a clip**: `speed` (absent = 1), `loop` ("loop" / "pingpong") with `span` (seconds of source per turn); a looped retimed clip loops the RETIMED clip. `srcUse`, `srcLocal(clip, u)` (the source moment u seconds into the clip) and `clipSegment` (the stretch the player can run without a jump: to the end of a turn, `rev` for a ping-pong's way back) are the arithmetic; `pictureAt` gives `segAt` / `base` / `speed` / `rev`. A looped clip is not cut by the blade or Detect cuts (take its loop off first); a split of a retimed clip keeps the speed. In the file: `metadata.imageviewer.speed` plus OTIO's own `LinearTimeWarp` effect (`time_scalar`, which Resolve reads; read back when there is no imageviewer speed), `metadata.imageviewer.loop` / `span`; `source_range` stays start-in-source / duration-on-the-timeline, so a reader that knows neither (agentY's `from_otio`, which now logs it and names it in `otio_dropped`) plays the plain clip from its in point; `toSave` always states `speed` / `loop` so that taking one off reaches the file. A dissolve's source offsets are scaled by the incoming clip's speed (`editable` / `rebuild`). Drawn: a retimed block is striped orange (`.retimed`) with a "62 %" badge, a looped one has its seams and a loop mark drawn on the strip (`_emSeams`) and a "↻ ×3" badge; the filmstrip and waveform read the source through `srcLocal`. **Preview**: a retimed film plays at `playbackRate` × speed with `preservesPitch` off (tape: pitched), muted outside 0.25x..4x; a ping-pong's way back and a rate the browser refuses (<1/16, >16) are STEPPED (wall clock, the film sought each frame); a loop's seam is a seek. **Render** (`timeline_render.clip_frames` / `source_moment` / `clip_sound`): nearest source frame at each film frame's moment (so 2x plays every other frame, 0.5x shows each twice), runs read forwards, a ping-pong's way back read forwards and reversed in memory; sound resampled like tape, silent outside `SOUND_SPEEDS`; `render(frames=(first, last))` renders only those frames (POST `/imageviewer/timeline_render` `frames`). Tests: `tests/test_timeline_retime_loop.py` (PyAV counter clip; ComfyUI's interpreter). **In and out**: the transport's own `playbackRange` (frames, both shown) is the one range; `_editTlSetRange` is the one place that sets it, and keeps it per cut in `view.range` (switchTab clears the transport's; `editPlayEnter` puts it back). The ruler draws it (`_editTlRulerMarks`: `.edit-range` band, `.edit-mark` handles - dragged, snapping to edit points and the playhead - and `.edit-markline`s down the tracks); Ctrl+drag on the ruler draws a range, right-click offers set in / out here, mark the shot, clear; the transport's own Ctrl-drag calls `editTlRangeChanged` so both bars stay one. Keys: I / O, X (mark the selected clip, else the V1 shot under the playhead - Resolve's "mark clip"), Alt+X and Shift+X clear (Shift+X as well because Alt can be taken by the window manager), Alt+I / Alt+O clear one end, Shift+I / Shift+O go to it. Used by loop playback (`_editPlayRange`), Export film ("In-out only" row, sent as `frames`), Auto edit (scope "the shots in the in-out range", the default while one is set) and Detect cuts (only the shots and the marks inside it). **One playhead**: the Edit panel draws `currentFrame` - the same number the viewer's bar, its frame field, J K L, the arrows and playback move - so there is nothing to keep in step and nothing to loop back: `_editTlPlayhead` runs from `_toolsFrameChanged` once per frame (measured: 24 fps of stills, drawn once a frame, never a `setFrame` while playing), and zoomed in it scrolls to keep the playhead in view when the FRAME moved (not on a redraw, not under a ruler scrub). The panel exists only with its cut's tab (`editTlTabHidden`); a move of the Edit playhead from anywhere else (`_editTlSeek`, `_editTlTransport`) brings that tab up first. Popped out, the whole viewer moves, so the same holds there.
- **Modes, gaps, linked sound, snapping** (as Resolve's; arithmetic in the data module, tested in `tests/test_edit_gaps_links.mjs` and `tests/test_edit_snap.mjs`, the render and the file in `tests/test_timeline_gaps_links.py`, the panel in `tests/test_edit_timeline_browser.py`). **Modes**: `_editTlTrimMode` (T, the toolbar's Trim) or not (A, Select - the default), the blade (B) over either; `editTlMode` switches, the lit tool button shows it, `ui.body` gets `.trim` / `.blade`. `_editTlEdgeAct` decides by mode. Selection: an edge is `trim-in` / `trim-out` = `liftTrimStart` / `liftTrimEnd` on V1 (no ripple: a gap opens or is filled, never into the neighbour; placed tracks as before); the body is a move (V1: `moveShot` = `liftV1` + `overwriteV1` - laid over what is there, pieces it covers cut away, one it falls inside split around it, a looped piece's head played on plainly; Ctrl+Shift+drag is the old insert, `moveClip`). Trim: an edge is `ripple-in` / `ripple-out` (`trimStart` / `trimEnd` on V1, `rippleTrackStart` / `rippleTrackEnd` on placed tracks: the clips after it on its track move), the body's upper half `slip`, its lower half `slide` (`slideClip` - a gap beside the shot gives and takes like a neighbour; `slideTrackClip` with the touching clips or the empty time). The middle of a cut (`_editTlCutPair`: two clips that touch, no dissolve, no gap) rolls in both modes, on placed tracks too (`rollTrack`); Alt / Alt+Shift / Ctrl keep slip / slide / retime / loop. Retime and loop extend still ripple on V1 in both modes. One computation for drags, keys and E: `_editTlTrimCompute(info, start, d)`. In every mode a click on an edge selects the edit point - see *Edit points* below. Cursors `TRIM_CURSORS["ripple-in" / "ripple-out"]` beside roll, slip, slide. **Gaps**: a V1 clip's `gap` = the empty seconds before it (`clipStarts` adds it; `mainLength` is the last end); no dissolve into or out of one (`settle` zeroes the `transition` of a clip with a gap) - documented choice, not a dissolve to black. `restart(cut, j, at)` is the primitive: clip j begins at `at`, the time to the clip before becoming its gap (or, overlapping, its dissolve); everything after keeps its place because it is placed relative to j's end. `v1Gaps` / `gapAt` / `closeGap`, selection kind `gap` (`.edit-gap` blocks; Del / Shift+Del close one; its menu "Close gap"). `playAt` is null in a gap; `pictureAt` gives black there (or the higher track) until the next clip begins. In the file: a Gap item before the clip (`rebuild`), read back by `editable` (`gap` on the clip after it); a dissolve into or out of a gap written by another program (a fade from / to black) is read as a cut. `timeline_render.read_cut` makes a gap a `blank` clip whose frames are None (`covered` draws black or the overlays) and whose sound is silence. agentY's `from_otio` has no gaps: it closes them and names each in `otio_dropped`. **Saving**: a new cut lands where `export_rules.target("edit", ...)` says: with a context node on the canvas that is `<output>/<project>/<shot>/edits/v001/<shot>_v001_<name>.otio`, without one `<output>/edits/`. The panel always says the whole path (`_editTlSavedTo` / `_editTlSavedShow`, `.edit-saved`, with Copy path and Show in file browser; a failure stays up and the cut stays unsaved, `_editTlSaveFailed`), and the Inspector's Timeline section keeps it (File, `editTlFilePath`). **Save cut as…** (Ctrl+Shift+S, `editTlSaveAsDialog`, POST `/imageviewer/timeline_save_as`: same_origin, `path_access` on the folder, `cut_file_name`, 409 unless overwrite, `save_as` on the source's document); the tab goes on with the new file. **Open cut…** (`editTlOpenDialog`, `editTlOpenPath`; `/imageviewer/browse` lists `.otio` as kind `cut`, the file browser opens those too). **Linked sound** (Resolve's Link Clips): one rule: a clip never sounds AND has a sound clip; a `mute`d picture draws no waveform (`_emDraw`), its strip takes the whole height, linked or not, and the sound clip keeps its own. From either part: `relinkAudio` (Ctrl+Alt+L on one clip, by the link group; the offset stays, `syncOffset`, `.edit-badge-sync` on both parts), `resyncAudio` (the sound back onto the picture), `mergeAudio` (the sound clip gone, the picture sounding, the group over; one step). Before those: `unlinkClip` (right-click "Unlink audio / video", Ctrl+Alt+L) puts the film's sound on the first sound track free there (else a new one) as a clip of the same file - same in, length, speed, loop - and mutes the film (`mute`; `clipVolume` 0 in the player, skipped in `film_sound`); the two share `link` (group id `L<n>`, `newLinkId`) with `unlinked`. "Link clips" (`linkClips`: a selected video clip and sound clip of one file) drops `unlinked`; while linked, `followLink(before, after, sel, {ripple})` gives the partner the clip's start / end / source-in deltas (and speed / loop) after the panel's moves and trims, on V1 through `moveShot`. A split piece has no partner (`splitClip` drops `link`; `splitWithLink` re-pairs the halves). `_editTlLinkBadge`: `.edit-badge-link` (a chain; `.off` broken) on films with sound and on paired clips. In the file `metadata.imageviewer.link` / `unlinked` / `mute` (`timeline_io.clip_link` / `_set_link`; `toSave` always states them). agentY's `from_otio` plays a film whose sound was split off with its own sound again (silent where the sound clip is gone) and says so in `otio_dropped`, with the offset; other clips of further tracks are named as left out. **Snapping** (N, the magnet `.edit-snap-btn`; `view.snap`, kept with the cut's view in localStorage; `view.snapBeats` the note sub-switch, off): ONE place - `_editTlSnapAt(t, exclude, {noHead, noRange})` / `_editTlSnapSpanAt` over `D.snapTargets` (the start, every clip edge on every track including sound clips, gap edges, texts, the playhead, in / out, the beats Auto edit read - `_editTlBeatsKnown`), resolved by `D.snapResolve` within `D.SNAP_PX` (9 px whatever the zoom, `snapSeconds`; at one distance a clip edge beats the playhead). The targets come from the cut at the press (`_editTlDragBase`); the dragged clip, its partner and what a ripple moves are left out (`_editTlTrimExclude`). Used by moves, edge trims of both modes, roll / ripple / slide (their moving edit point; a slip moves none), the blade, the in / out handles and the ruler range, drops, text drags and the ruler scrub. `.edit-snapline` is drawn at the target (`flash`, the target `.snap-hit`) and put away on release. S held DURING a drag flips snapping for that drag (`_editTlSnapFlip`; `editTlKeyDown` hands S to the drag first, so it is no split then); keyboard nudges never snap.
- **Selection, edit points, the scale** (as Resolve's; arithmetic in the data module, tested in `tests/test_edit_selection_points.mjs`, the panel in `tests/test_edit_timeline_browser.py`, its `test_01`..`test_10`). **The scale**: `lay.total` is the seconds the strip's whole width stands for (`viewSpan(contentEnd, zoom)`), `lay.film` the film's length: every % on the strip is of `lay.total`. Zoom runs `MIN_ZOOM` 0.25 to `MAX_ZOOM` 64 (`clampZoom`); 1 is Fit (Shift+Z); below 1 the strip stays the panel's width and the film takes that share, with empty timeline after it (ruler, moves, drops; a seek there clamps to the last frame; V1 past its end: `v1DropAt().past`, a gap before the clip). Above Fit, an edit keeps the seconds per pixel (`keepScale`, `view.end`); Fit stays Fit. **A drag holds the scale**: `_editTlLock` (set in `_editTlDrag`, cleared on let go) makes `editTlRefresh` keep the seconds per pixel, growing the strip if the film grows; never lay something out as a share of the film's length during a drag. **Snapping lands on frames**: `_editTlDrag` hands `onMove` the time read off the timeline as it stands, minus the time at the press (no pixels-at-the-press factor, so a scroll during a drag does not matter). `_editTlSnapAt` answers ON the target's frame; `_editTlSnapSpanAt` (`snapMove`) answers the start that lands, already final: by its END a span's start is the target less its length, even if that is no whole frame - do not `toFrame` it again. A trim rounds the EDGE, `d = landed - t0`, not the distance. `_editTlSnapLine` keeps `_editTlSnapTarget`, re-placed by `_editTlSnapPlace` from every refresh. `snapExclude` leaves out the dragged clip, its partner and the gaps beside them (their near edge is the clip's own edge, which it would stick to). The playhead is drawn at the START of its frame, where a clip snapped to it lands. **Several selected**: `_editTlMulti` is a list of any `isItemSel` kind: `clip`, `tclip`, `text`, `sound` (the bed, `.edit-snd`, `data-bed`), `gap`; `_editTlSelAll` is all of them, `_editTlSelList` the clips only (Recreate, links, `selectedClips`). `selId`: `V1:i`, `<track>:i`, `T:i`, `A1:i`, `gap:i`. Ctrl+click toggles on any track, Shift+click adds (a range on one track). A plain press on one of several in Selection: `_editTlGroupDown` drags them together - `D.moveGroup`, as Resolve's: all by the same time (the pressed one's edges snapping; held only at 0) and the same number of rows (`_editTlRowIndex` / `_editTlRowsMoved`, read off the rows as they are NOW - a redraw during the drag replaces the row elements, which is why a vertical group move once did nothing; positive is down; a picture stays on video tracks, a sound on sound tracks - not A1; past the top video / bottom sound track a new track, below V1 held), LIFTED first (gaps stay where they were; what stays on V1 keeps its place) and laid down OVER what is at the new place (`overwriteV1` / `overwriteTrack`: covered clips go, overlapped ones are trimmed, one a clip falls inside is split); a linked partner not selected itself goes along in time on its own track; texts move in time; one undo step; refused (null) only if V1 would be left without a shot. (Before, `moveSelection` stopped the group at the first clip that was not moving - the user's "not further than the previous clips" - and it is still what moves ONE clip by `,` / `.` in Selection: into a gap and no further.) `,` / `.` on several move them by `moveGroup` too, in both modes; the Trim mode's body press slips / slides the pressed clip, as Resolve's, so a group is dragged in Selection. A linked pair alone is one clip and drags as one; a click without a move makes it the single selection. **Marquee** `_editTlMarqueeDown`, on `ui.scroll`: a press on nothing (a `.edit-track`, `.edit-room`, the rows, the area, not the ruler) dragged draws `.edit-marquee`; `marqueeSel` by rows and time; Shift / Ctrl adds.
