Clips only, in the Trim mode too: it does not select edit points, the panel has one selected edit point. **New tracks**: `.edit-room` rows (`data-room` video / audio, 12 px) above the top video row and below the bottom row; `_editTlRoomAt(y)` goes by the row rects, so T and the ruler count as above, and past the panel's edge when the tracks overflow it. `_editTlRoomFor`: a picture goes up, a sound down. `_editTlLand` / `transferToNewTrack` on the drop, `_editTlAdd(.., room)` for drops from outside and canvas nodes: one undo step, no track until it is let go; `_editTlGhost` and `_editTlDropMark` show it (`.landing`, `data-label` "+ V3"). **Edit points**: `_editTlEditSel` = `{ act, sel, side, a, b }`, set by a click on an edge in every mode (`_editTlTrimDown`) or `editTlEditStep` (Shift+S / Shift+A, three steps a cut as Resolve's: `editSteps` turns `editStops` into LEFT (the outgoing out, `trim-out`), BOTH (a roll, where two clips touch), RIGHT (the incoming in, `trim-in`) - only the ones there are - and `nextEditStep` goes one on (Shift+S) or back (Shift+A) from the selected edit point's step (`pointState`), or from the playhead with none: forwards the first step at or after its frame, backwards the last at or before; `stepPoint` makes the edit point; on the selected clip's or edit's track, else V1; the old `nextStop` / `stopPoint` are still exported). **Play around edit**: in the Trim mode with an edit point selected, `_editPlayRange` is `_editPlayAround` - `PLAY_AROUND` (1 s) before the cut to 1 s after it, held to the film, always looped, played from its pre-roll; asked every frame, so a `,` / `.` while it plays moves the loop with the cut (`editTlNudgeEdit` then leaves the playhead and the two-up alone); the window is `.edit-around` on the ruler (`_editTlEditMarkDraw`); leaving the Trim mode or the edit point gives the in / out back. The two-up is not shown while playing (Resolve shows the film then), only stopped. What it does is decided when it moves: `pointAct(point, trim)` - a roll stays one, an edge is `ripple-*` in Trim, `trim-*` in Selection (`_editTlEditLive`). `editTlNudge` is `,` / `.`: an edit point trims (`editTlNudgeEdit`, the playhead staying on it); else the selected MOVE (Selection: `moveSelection`, into a gap and no further; Trim, one clip: `slideClip` / `slideTrackClip`, falling back to a move at an end of the track); never snapped. Alt+`,` / `.` slip (`editTlSlipBy`). `_editTlEditMarkDraw`: green `.edit-editpoint` on each edge that moves, inside its clip (`ui.editMark`..`4`, a linked partner's too). Esc lets go of the edit point, then the selection. **Deleting**: `editTlDelete` -> `deleteSelection(cut, list, ripple)`, one undo step: partners too, V1 from the end, a gap before the clip it lies before, tracks closed up per track, V1 keeps its last shot (`kept`). A dissolve (`mix`) on its own as before. The bed deleted reaches the file: `toSave` sends `sound`, `rebuild` keeps only the bed clips named. The cursors are drawn on a 15 px grid at half-pixel coordinates (`cursor`), hot spot 7 7. **The bar is ONE row** (`flex-wrap: nowrap`, the name and badge shrink with an ellipsis), laid out as Resolve's: file buttons | Add | the modes (Selection, Trim, Dynamic, Blade) | Source viewer, Insert / Overwrite / Replace + ▾ | Snapping + ▾ (beats) | the wand menu `.edit-tools-btn` (Split at the playhead, Auto edit…, Detect cuts, Undo, Redo) | name | speaker + ▾ (scrubbing), proxy, zoom. A caret is `.edit-caret`, opened by `_previzOpenMenu`; `_editTlHas` (set by `editTlRefresh`) is whether the cut has anything laid out. A new rare action goes into a menu, not onto the bar. **The bar's icons** are Lucide's, a fixed table of shapes built as SVG DOM (`imageViewer_editIcons.js`, `setEditIcon`). **Keys listed**: the panel has no button of its own for them; the viewer's Show Hotkeys overlay lists `EDIT_KEYS` instead of its own keys while a cut's tab is up with the panel, or the timeline has the focus (`_helpIsEdit`, `#help-edit`); `?` opens it from the timeline even with the pointer off the viewer.
- **Markers** (Resolve's M; `imageViewer_editMarkers.js`, `EditMarkersMixin`; arithmetic in the data module's "markers" section, tested in `tests/test_edit_markers.mjs`; the file in `tests/test_edit_markers.py`; the FCPXML in `tests/test_resolve_export.py` `test_markers_are_markers_of_the_v1_element_they_lie_in`; the panel in `tests/test_edit_resolve_tools_browser.py`). A TIMELINE marker is `cut.markers` = `[{t, len, name, note, color, ref?}]` (film seconds; it stays put when clips move); a CLIP marker is `c.markers` = `[{src, len, ...}]` with `src` in seconds of the clip's SOURCE, so it rides with moves, trims, slips and speed (`clipMarkU` gives its place in the clip, null while a trim hides it - kept; `clipMarkSrc` the other way; a still's `src` is clip time). `allMarkers(cut)` is every visible one in film time with `which` = `{kind: "marker", i}` / `{kind: "cmarker", sel, i}` (`markerOf`, `addMarker` - on the clip when `sel` is given and `t` lies in it, never doubled on a frame -, `editMarker`, `moveMarker`, `removeMarkers`, `nextMarker`, `markersAt`); `markerId` names one among the snap targets (kind `marker`), so a marker's drag leaves itself out. `MARKER_COLORS` / `MARKER_HEX` (OTIO's colour names). `toSave` always states `markers` (the cut's and each clip's), `sameCut` compares them, `detach` drops their `ref`s. **File** (`timeline_io.read_markers` / `_write_markers` / `film_markers`): timeline markers are the FIRST VIDEO TRACK's markers - NOT the stack's, which are agentY's texts and become titles - clip markers the clip's own (OTIO media time; `len` x speed), each with `metadata.imageviewer.marker` {note, len}, `comment` and an upper-case OTIO colour; every marker of those lists is read (Resolve's too; Fuchsia -> magenta), and `ref` keeps what another program wrote into it; an edit without `markers` leaves them alone. **Panel**: M (`editTlMarkerAdd`: on the selected clip when the playhead is in it; on a frame with a marker it opens that one), Alt+M (`editTlMarkerDeleteHere`), Shift+Up / Down (`editTlMarkerStep`), Del with `_editTlMarkSel` (a press on anything but a marker lets it go, in the body's capture pointerdown), Esc. Drawn by `_editTlMarkersDraw` (from `_editTlRulerMarks`: `.edit-markers` > `.edit-marker`, `.span` a duration band, colour in `--mk`) and `_editTlClipMarks` (`.edit-cmarker` flags along a clip's top); `_editTlMarkerBind`: click seeks and selects, drag moves through `_editTlDrag` (one step, `_editTlSnapAt` leaving itself out), double-click opens `editTlMarkerDialog` (`.edit-markerdlg`: name, colour, duration in frames, notes), right-click `_editTlMarkerMenu`. `viewerState().edit.markers` lists them for agentY. **Resolve**: `build_model`'s `markers` (all of them, as timeline markers at their frame, in-out shifted) become `<marker>`s of the V1 element they lie in, before its anchored clips; colours are not carried (said in the README).
- **Lift / extract the in-out range, Swap Clips, Select Forward** (as Resolve's; `imageViewer_editRange.js`, `EditRangeMixin`; arithmetic `liftRange` / `swapClip` / `selectForward` at the end of the data module, tested in `tests/test_edit_range_swap.mjs`; the panel in `tests/test_edit_resolve_tools_browser.py`). `editTlDelete` with NO selection hands over to `editTlLiftRange(ripple)` when an in and out are set (`_editTlRangeSeconds`: [start / fps, (end + 1) / fps)); also the ruler's menu (`_editTlRangeMenuRows`). `liftRange(cut, a, b, ripple, mask)`: `splitAt` at the out and the in on the `editMask` tracks (linked partners cut too), the pieces wholly inside go through `deleteSelection` (a lift); a clip of a masked track still crossing the in or the out (a dissolve, a loop) refuses the whole edit with `error`. Extract then takes the range's length off the empty time before V1's next shot (V1 masked), `rippleTracks` the other masked tracks back from the out, and moves timeline markers after the out (those inside go). The bed (A1) and old texts are not touched; V1 keeps its last shot (`kept`). The playhead goes to the in after an extract. `swapClip(cut, sel, dir)` (Ctrl+Shift+, / .): V1 shots trade places with gaps and dissolves left at their joins (`gap` / `transition` swapped back), a placed clip trades places with the nearest clip on that side keeping the gap between; both `followLink`. `selectForward(cut, t, track | null, dir)` (Y / Alt+Y forward: ends after the playhead; Shift+Y / Alt+Shift+Y backward: begins before it - Resolve's Ctrl+Y stays Redo): V1 and placed tracks, locked ones left out, made the selection by `_editTlSetSelList`.
- **Dynamic trim** (W, Resolve's; `imageViewer_editDynamic.js`, `EditDynamicMixin`; the bar's `.edit-dynamic-btn`, `ui.body.dynamic`; test in `tests/test_edit_resolve_tools_browser.py`). `_editTlDynamic` on: `editTlKeyDown` hands J / K / L to `editTlDynKey(dir)` first, which takes them when there is something to move (`_editTlDynTarget`: the edit point -> "edit", else ONE selected clip -> "slip" in the Trim mode / "slide" in Selection) or a run going; otherwise they shuttle. A run (`_editTlDyn` = {what, dir, rate, cut, sel, info, live, history, frames}) ticks at the cut's fps and computes every tick from the cut at its START and the frames moved in all (`_editTlDynCut`: `_editTlTrimCompute(live, …)`, `slipClip`, `slideClip` / `slideTrackClip` + `followLink`) - stepping by nudges accumulated the 1e-4 rounding (82.01 frames after 14 steps). The same key again doubles the rate up to 8x, the other reverses at 1x; K, a press in the body, leaving the tab or a held edit stop it (`_editTlDynStop`), and the steps `_editTlApply` recorded are replaced by ONE (`historyPush(run.history, run.cut)`). The playhead follows the edit (`_editTlEditTime`), the two-up pulses as for the keys.
- **Video transitions: dip to a colour, wipes** (arithmetic in the data module's "video transitions" section - `TRANSITION_TYPES`, `WIPE_DIRS`, `cleanTfx`, `setTransitionLook`, `transitionLook` - tested in `tests/test_edit_transitions.mjs`; the file, the render and the JS / Python parity in `tests/test_edit_transitions.py`; the panel in `tests/test_edit_resolve_tools_browser.py`). A V1 dissolve (`transition` on the clip coming in) is a cross dissolve unless that clip has `tfx`: `{type: "dip", color: "#rrggbb"}` (the shot going out fades to the colour over the first half, the one coming in from it over the second) or `{type: "wipe", dir: right | left | down | up}` (a hard edge; "right" = uncovered from the left). `settle` drops `tfx` with the dissolve; `toSave` always states it (null), `sameCut` compares it; `swapClip` leaves it at its join. Preview (`_editPlayMix` -> `transitionLook` -> `_editPlayLook`): a dip sets the two layers' opacities and the viewport's background colour (`ep.dipHost`, put back after), a wipe a CSS `mask-image` gradient on the layer coming in (`ep.masked`); both cleared on the next frame without one and in `editPlayLeave`. Render: `timeline_render.transition_frame(tfx, out, inn, a)` from `film_pictures` (`read_cut` carries `tfx`). File: `metadata.imageviewer.transition` on the OTIO Transition (`timeline_io.clean_tfx`; written by `rebuild`, read by `editable`), `transition_type` still `SMPTE_Dissolve` and `name` "Dip to Color" / "Wipe", so others read a dissolve. UI: Effects items (Dip to Black / White, Wipe; `fx.look`), the mix bar's right-click menu (`.edit-mix.dip` / `.wipe`, `data-look`), the Inspector's Transition tab (Type `ttype`, Colour `tcolor`, Direction `tdir`). Resolve: still `Cross Dissolve` (README line); `agentLoses` names them. Not done: transition alignment (the panel's dissolve is an overlap of the clips' own frames, so start / centre / end on the edit changes nothing it shows) and Smooth Cut (needs optical flow).
- **Freeze frame, reverse** (Resolve's; arithmetic in the data module - `motionOf`, `stepOf`, `srcSpan`, `setMotion`, `MOTIONS` - tested in `tests/test_edit_freeze_reverse.mjs`; the file, the render and the JS / Python `srcLocal` / `source_moment` parity in `tests/test_edit_freeze_reverse.py`; the FCPXML in `tests/test_resolve_export.py` `test_a_reverse_runs_back_…`; the panel in `tests/test_edit_resolve_tools_browser.py`). `c.motion` = "reverse" | "freeze" (not on stills or titles; `loopOf` is "" while it is set, `setLoop` with a mode takes it off, `setMotion` takes a loop off). A REVERSED clip's `in` is the source moment at its HEAD and the source runs back from it at its speed (`srcLocal` = in - u x speed, `srcSpan` = {lo, hi} the stretch shown); a FROZEN one holds `in` (no speed, `srcUse` 0). `stepOf` (speed, -speed, 0) is what the head of a clip moves the source by: `headTrim`, `splitClip`, `srcAt`, the dissolve heads in `timeline_io.editable` / `rebuild` (`_step`); the tail of a reversed clip moves no source, so `trimEnd` needs nothing. Rooms: a reverse's `outRoom` is `in / speed - length` (back to the source's start), its `inRoom(c, durations)` the source after `in` (Infinity unknown); a freeze has none either way. `slipClip` holds a reverse's `in` to [use, known]. `retimeClip` on a freeze only sets the length, `setSpeed` refuses it. `setMotion(cut, sel, motion, u)`: forwards <-> reverse keep the same stretch (in +/- length x speed), a freeze takes the moment `u` into the clip (the panel's `editTlMotion` passes the playhead's frame middle, else 0) and drops the speed. `followLink` carries motion to a partner. Silent: `xfadeable` refuses it, the scrub's `audible`, `clipSegment` answers `rev: true` (speed 0 for a freeze), so the player STEPS it (`ep.stepped`, sought each frame by `srcLocal`) and the sound follower pauses it; a dissolve's under-layer is sought, never played. `cutMarks` gives none, `fitSource` leaves it. File: `metadata.imageviewer.motion` plus OTIO's own effect - `FreezeFrame.1` (time_scalar 0) or `LinearTimeWarp.1` with time_scalar -speed; read back from either when the metadata does not say (`clip_time`). `source_range` starts at the moment at the clip's head either way, so a reader that knows neither plays forwards from there (`agentLoses` says so). Render: `source_moment` / `_runs` follow it (a reverse is one backward run, read forwards and handed out reversed), `clip_sound` is None. Resolve: `_turns` gives a reverse one piece at -speed (a backward `timeMap`) and a freeze one at speed 0 (a `timeMap` whose value stands still); README lines say to check both. UI: the clip menu (Freeze frame / Reverse clip, ticked when on), the Inspector's Timing row Motion (`data-field="motion"`), the block `.frozen` / `.reversed` with "❚❚ freeze" / "◀◀" in its time badge. No keys (Resolve's Shift+R is the speed reset here). Speed ramps: the next bullet.
- **Speed ramps** (Resolve's speed points, kept simpler; arithmetic in the data module - `rampOf`, `rampAdvance`, `srcAdvance`, `shiftRamp`, `shareArea` - tested in `tests/test_edit_speed_ramp.mjs`; the file, the render and the JS / Python parity in `tests/test_edit_speed_ramp.py`; the FCPXML in `tests/test_resolve_export.py` `test_a_speed_ramp_…`; the panel in `tests/test_edit_resolve_tools_browser.py`). The speed is a KEY PARAM, `spd` (`KEY_PARAMS` / `timeline_io.KEY_PARAMS`, range `MIN_SPEED`..`MAX_SPEED`, a multiple of the clip's own speed, clip time, any ease): `rampOf(c)` is its keys (none on a still, a title, or a frozen / reversed clip - motion wins, the keys stay and come back). A ramped clip KEEPS ITS LENGTH; what it shows at u is `in + rampAdvance(keys, u)`, the exact integral of `keyValue` (linear, ease in / out / in-out as `shareArea`, hold) - before the first key its value, after the last its value. `srcAdvance(c, d)` is what the head moves the source by (`headTrim`, `splitClip`, `srcAt`; a head trim also `shiftRamp`s the keys so the rest of the clip shows what it showed; `splitFx` divides them at a split). `srcUse` is the integral over the length; `inRoom` / `outRoom` use the first / last speed. `retimeClip` / `setSpeed` refuse a ramped clip, `loopOf` is "" for it, `autoKey` of a plain `spd` is `setSpeed`; `paramAt(c, "spd")` is `speedOf` without keys. Silent and stepped like a reverse (`clipSegment` `rev: true`, `xfadeable`, the scrub's `audible`, `clip_sound` None; the dissolve under-layer is sought). `clipMarkU` inverts the ramp by halving. Python: `timeline_io._ramp` / `ramp_advance` / `_share_area`, `source_moment` and `_runs` follow it, `timeline_render.clip_frames` reads it per frame. Resolve: one piece per clip, its `timeMap` a point every `RAMP_STEP` (4) frames and at each key, straight between (`ramp`, `ramp_in` on the piece; `_local_start` 0); EDL gets the mean speed. UI: the Inspector's Speed % row has the diamond (`data-keys="spd"`), its number is the speed at the playhead, a change is a key; "Retime to" and Loop are off while ramped; the block's time badge says "ramp"; the keys are the ordinary diamonds on the block. Not done: a ramp that changes the clip's length (Resolve's), and a curve editor for it.
- **The Source viewer, three-point editing, reverse match frame** (Resolve's source monitor; `imageViewer_editSource.js`, `EditSourceMixin`; NOT a dock panel: `editSourcePanel` is `.src-monitor`, made in imageViewer.js right before `#viewport` in `.main-area`, so it stands LEFT of the picture; ONE monitor display, as Resolve's (Sebastian 2026-11-10): there is no "Source viewer off" - `_editSrcOn` = a cut's tab is up, `_editSrcUp` = the source on screen; laid out by `_editSrcLayout` from `editTlTabShown` / `editTlTabHidden` (`_editSrcLayout(false)` sets `_editSrcGone`: switchTab asks while the cut's tab is still `activeTab`, so the switch, the monitor bar and `.mon-active` are taken down by that flag - they once stayed up over Paint). Single / Dual: `_editSrcViewer()` = {v: 2, mode dual | single, show source | timeline} in localStorage `imageviewer.viewer.editViewer` (an older save without `v` whose `_editTlWanted().editSource` was off becomes single / timeline); dual = side by side (flex halves), single = `#viewport` `display: none` while the source shows; `editSrcViewerMode` / `editSrcToggle(on)` (= dual on / off) / `editSrcViewerButton`: going single keeps the active monitor on screen; the ONLY switch is `.src-viewctl` = the `.src-mode` toggle (`.on` / `aria-pressed` while dual, 32 x 28 px; no bar button, no Timeline-group or caret-menu item; there is no swap button - Q and the caret menu swap a single viewer); `.src-close` is hidden in single (`.src-monitor.single`) and in dual makes it single on the timeline: placed by `_editSrcCtlPlace` at the top right of the viewer that stands at the right - in `#viewport` while it is on screen, else in the source's `.src-head` before its close button), Q = `editSrcSwap` (timeline and monitor keys; showing the source stops the cut's playback); the way back is automatic too (issue #106): `_editSrcToTimeline` puts a single viewer that shows the source back on the timeline when the timeline is worked - a press on the Edit timeline's tracks / ruler / headers (`.edit-main`, the body's capture pointerdown; not its bar, so the source edit buttons leave the source up), its transport keys (`_editTlTransport`), `editPlayStart`, reverse match frame; it does not `editTlRefresh` (that would replace the rows under the press); test `test_a_single_viewer_goes_back_and_forth_…`). **Two monitors** (Sebastian 2026-11-10): the timeline viewer has a bar of its own like the source's (`_editMonBarPlace` / `_editMonBarDraw`: `.tl-monbar` at the bottom of `#viewport` while `_editMonTlShown`, `.tlm-scrub` with the in / out band, play, timecode, the clip's path - the viewer's `.path-bar` is hidden under `.has-monbar`); it IS `currentFrame`, drawn from `_editTlPlayhead`, so it is ganged with the Edit timeline. The active monitor (`editMonActive` / `editMonSet`, `_editMon`): a press on the source's body or on `#viewport` / the Edit timeline makes that one active; side by side the active one has a white outline (`.mon-active`, an `::after` border); one on screen is always the active one. `editMonKeyDown` (asked by `handleKeyDown` after `editTlKeyDown`): the source focused, or active with the viewer hovered -> `_editSrcKey`; the timeline viewer active and hovered -> its transport keys go through `editTlKeyDown` (`_editTlKeyForce` lets `_editTlFocused` pass). The source has J / K / L (`editSrcShuttle`: L faster each press up to 8x, J backwards on a timer) and the timeline viewer's pan / zoom (`_editSrcWireView`: wheel at the pointer, left / middle drag pans, right drag zooms; F = `editSrcFit`; `_editSrcZ`, reset per file) - test `test_both_monitors_have_a_bar_…`. Edit buttons in the edit bar (`.edit-srcedits` > `.edit-srcedit[data-edit]` for insert / overwrite / replace only, as Resolve's bar; place, ripple, fit, append and single / dual / Q in the `.edit-src-more` caret menu; SOURCE_BUTTONS' 5th field is the icon: `srcInsert` .. `srcAppend` in `imageViewer_editIcons.js`, drawn in Lucide's manner, `_editSrcButtons`). Track patching in the headers (no menu): on every cut's tab, `_editTlHeadRow` puts `.edit-hpatch` at each header's left (`.edit-body.patching` widens the column); lit "V1" on `_editSrcDest(cut, false)`, "A1" on the sound destination; a click is `editSrcPatchTo(id)` -> `view.srcTrack` / `view.srcAudio` (else V1 / the first sound track - the selected clip no longer decides); arithmetic `threePoint` / `sourceEdit` / `SOURCE_EDITS` / `findSource` at the end of the data module, tested in `tests/test_edit_source.mjs`; the panel in `tests/test_edit_resolve_tools_browser.py` `test_the_source_viewer_…`). `this._editSrc` = `{file, name, still, sound, dur, in, out, head}` (seconds of the file on the CUT's frame grid; `out` is where the range ends, the out frame counted in; localStorage `imageviewer.viewer.editSource`, read lazily by `_editSrcState`). The panel (`.src-body`, focusable: Space plays its own `<video>` / `<audio>` / `<img>`, I / O, Alt+X / Shift+X, arrows, Home / End, F9..F12, Shift+F - `_editSrcKey`) is rebuilt only when the file changes (`dataset.file`), else `_editSrcDraw` moves the scrub line, the `.src-range` band and the `.src-tc` readout (`dataset.head` in frames). Loaded by `editSrcLoad(item, src)` (probe for its length; opens the panel; kept without showing when the tab changed meanwhile) or `editSrcLoadItems(items)` (the first, found through `_editTlResolve`): a drop (the History payload - History thumbnails and file browser rows - or desktop files), a plain History click on a cut's tab, a canvas node let go over it (`_editTlWatchCanvas` checks `editSourcePanel` before the timeline; the node is put back), the clip menu's "Open in Source viewer", and match frame (F), which always loads it (a single viewer swaps to the source). **Three points** `threePoint(src, tl)`: both source marks -> their length (a lone timeline OUT back-times); a timeline range with at most one source mark -> the range's length; else source in (or playhead) to its end; no end (a still) -> error; a still in the panel uses its marks' length, the range, or 3 s. **Edits** `sourceEdit(cut, kind, clip, at, {track, audio, mask, head, srcHead, dur, tlIn, tlOut})` -> `{cut, sel}` / `{error}`: insert = `insertEdit` (a sound: split + move that track's clips on), overwrite = `overwriteV1` / `overwriteTrack`, replace = the clip under the playhead keeps its place and length, in = srcHead - (head - clip start) (held to the source both ways; its speed, loop, motion, link, takes, markers, fades go, its fx stay), place = the lowest free unlocked video track above `track`, else `addTrack`, fit = `overwrite` at tlIn with `speed` = source length / range (MIN_SPEED..MAX_SPEED), append = after V1's last shot (or the track's last clip), ripple = the V1 shot under the playhead becomes the source range and `rippleTracks` moves the masked tracks by the difference. A sound with no sound track to go to gets one. `editSrcEdit(kind)` is one `_editTlApply`, selects the clip and seeks to its end (not for replace). F9..F12 also from the timeline (`editTlKeyDown`, `sourceEditKey`) - a browser may keep F11 for full screen; the buttons are there. **Reverse match frame** Shift+F (`editSrcMatchBack`): `findSource(cut, file, src)` - the first clip (V1, then the tracks) whose stretch of that file shows the moment (frame by frame through `srcLocal`, interpolated inside the frame), selected, the playhead there. Not done: the source's own sound / picture patching (a film always brings both), a source at its own frame rate.
- **Compound clips** (Resolve's New Compound Clip / Decompose in Place; arithmetic at the end of the data module - `isCompound`, `compoundLength`, `makeCompound`, `decomposeCompound`, `flattenCut`, `regroupCut` - tested in `tests/test_edit_compound.mjs`; the file in `tests/test_edit_compound.py`; the panel in `tests/test_edit_resolve_tools_browser.py` `test_compound_clip_…`). A compound is a clip (V1 or a video track) with `compound = {id, cut}` and no file; `in` / `length` are a window on the inner film. No time of its own (`speedOf` / `loopOf` / `motionOf` / `rampOf` see none, `setSpeed` / `retimeClip` / `setMotion` / `loopExtend` refuse it); `srcRoom` is the inner film. **One rule: it is played and saved FLAT.** `flattenCut` lays the inner cut out in its place (inner V1 into V1 or its track, inner tracks on new tracks `<home>~<n>` right above it with `cpart` and `under`, the inner bed on a sound track), every piece tagged `cpart: {id}` (the outermost compound's; nested ones flatten recursively) and the definitions in `cut.compounds` ({cut, track, slot, at, in, length, name, color, transition, volume, disabled}; `slot` = the home track's place among its kind, since a file renumbers tracks). `_editPlayCut` is `_editFlat(cut)` (memoized by cut identity), as are `_editTlLive` (`liveTracks` lets an `under` track follow its base), the scrub's `scrubSources` and `_editTlMatchAt` (no selection when flattened: the indices differ). `toSave` flattens and sends `compounds` (always, so taking the last one apart reaches the file) and `cpart` on clips and tracks (always stated). File: `metadata.imageviewer.cpart` on clips (`clip_link` / `_set_link`) and tracks (`track_flags` / `_set_flags`), the definitions in the timeline's `metadata.imageviewer.compounds` (`compounds_of`: ids by `_LINK`, at most 200, 4 MB; an edit without the key keeps the file's). `regroupCut` folds the pieces back in `_editTlRead`, `_editTlLoaded` (save, save as) and `editTlChangedIn`. Panel: the clip menu's "New compound clip" (`editTlCompound`, the selection) and "Decompose in place" (`editTlDecompose`), the block `.compound` with an `.edit-badge-compound` (⧉ n). Filmstrips skip it (no file). Not done: opening a compound in a timeline of its own; its own transform / crop are not drawn (the inner clips' are).
- **Match frame** (F, Resolve's; `imageViewer_editMatch.js`, `EditMatchMixin`; arithmetic `D.matchFrame` at the end of the data module, tested in `tests/test_edit_match_frame.mjs`; the panel in `tests/test_edit_resolve_tools_browser.py` `test_match_frame_…`). `matchFrame(cut, t, sel, live)` -> `{file, src, sel, name, still}`: the selected clip when `t` lies in it (any track, sound clips too), else `pictureAt`'s picture on top over the live tracks; `src` = `srcLocal` (speed, loops, reverse, freeze, ramps; a still's 0); a title, a gap, nothing -> null. `editTlMatchFrame` (the F key in `editTlKeyDown` - only with the timeline focused, so the viewer's own F = Fit stays elsewhere; the clip menu's "Match frame (F)") takes the middle of the playhead's frame, makes a snapshot through `_browserFramesFor`, stamps `matchSrc` on it, opens it in the Viewer tab (`showInViewerTab`, `dedupe: false`) and `_editTlMatchSeek`s the film there once it knows its frames (`_videoFrames`, `_videoFps`): frame floor(src x fps). The cut's tab is left, as any Open is. On a cut's tab it loads the source into the Source viewer (`editSrcLoad`; a single viewer swaps to it, as Resolve's) - the Viewer-tab path is for a page without one; Shift+F is the way back (previous bullet).
- **Still frame from the playhead** (Sebastian 2026-11-13; `editTlStillFrame` in `imageViewer_editMatch.js`; G, Alt+G, the wand menu, the clip menu). The frame `_editTlMatchAt` names (match frame's rule: the selected clip when the playhead is in it, else the picture on top; `src` through `srcLocal`) -> POST `/imageviewer/timeline_still` `{file, src, name, context}` (`same_origin`, `may_serve`; `timeline_media.still_frame`: the last source frame that begins at or before `src`, the render's rule, at the source's OWN size; a picture gives itself, alpha kept) -> a PNG at `export_rules.target("frame", "<name>_still", "png")`, the next version, never over a file -> `/imageviewer/push` (group "Edit stills", `focus: false`). It is the SOURCE frame: no fx, titles or transitions. Alt+G also `_editTlAdd`s it as an insert edit at the playhead (one undo step). Shift+G stays the framing mask. Tests: `test_a_still_frame_is_…` in `tests/test_timeline_media_routes.py`, `test_a_still_frame_of_the_playhead_…` in `tests/test_edit_resolve_tools_browser.py`.
- **The audio mixer** (Resolve's Mixer, kept simpler; `imageViewer_editMixer.js`, `EditMixerMixin`, dock id `editMixer`, one of `EDIT_PANELS`, off by default, the bar's Timeline group; its home is the bottom rail right of the Edit timeline (`defaultRail: "bottom"`, size 0.4; `_dockMixerHome` moves it there once from a side rail, flag `imageviewer.viewer.mixerBottom`), movable like any panel; arithmetic `mixerLevel` / `setMixerLevel` / `peakDb` in `imageViewer_audioFxData.js`, tested in `tests/test_edit_mixer.mjs`; the panel in `tests/test_edit_resolve_tools_browser.py` `test_the_mixer_…`). A strip per sound source - V1 (a meter only: its sound is each film's own at its clip volume), A1, every sound track - plus the **master** (`D.MASTER`, "master"): the whole mix after every track, with a fader and a pan that are the cut's master chain `cut.afx.master` (`trackAfx` / `setTrackAfx` / `afxTrack` take "master"; the Inspector's Track FX shows it via the strip's fx button) and an M that is `cut.off.master` (the film's sound off: not heard, not rendered; no S, no lock - it is not a track, `liveTracks` never sees it; `masterActive`). In the file the stack's `metadata.imageviewer.master_afx` / `master_off` (`timeline_io._set_master`; an edit without the keys keeps them); `timeline_render.film_sound` applies the chain to the whole mix (`only` None only - a part baked for Resolve is before it) and returns silence when off; Export to Resolve does not carry it (a `left_out` line). Playback: `ctx.__master` is input -> its chain -> `out` (gain 0 when off) -> speakers + meter, rewired by `_epAfxMasterWire` from the cut (every frame in `_editAfxTick`, and `_editAfxMasterSync` after a Mixer change); while `masterActive` every element is routed through WebAudio so it reaches the master. Tests: `tests/test_edit_mixer.mjs`, `tests/test_edit_mixer_master.py`, `test_the_mixer_…`. NO second model: the fader is the track's LAST Gain effect and the pan its last Pan effect (`D.trackAfx` / `setTrackAfx`), so the render, the file and Resolve's bake already carry them; moved off rest with none it appends one, back at rest an unkeyed one goes, a keyed one takes a key at the playhead (film time), a bypassed one reads as rest and is switched on. A move is one undo step at `change` (while dragged, `_editMixLive` sets the bus's input gain relative to the chain; `editMixSet` puts it back to 1); `_editMixDrag` keeps a redraw from taking the fader from under the pointer. M = `setTrackOff`, S = `view.solo` + `editPlayLiveChanged`; a locked track's controls are disabled (and `_editTlApply` refuses anyway). Redrawn from `editPanelsRefresh` and from `_editTlPlayhead` when the frame moved (a signature skips unchanged ones). **Meters**: each track bus is now input -> its effects -> `out` -> the window's master (`ctx.__master`: a gain to the speakers) with an AnalyserNode (`meter`, fftSize 1024, no smoothing) beside `out` and beside the master; while the Mixer is up (`_editMixOn`) `_editAfxEnsure` puts every element it is asked about through WebAudio and its track's bus (V1 included) so there is something to read - an element once routed stays routed. `_editMixDraw` (rAF in the panel's window while up) reads `getFloatTimeDomainData`, `peakDb`, a held peak mark for `HOLD_MS`, `.hot` above -6 dB, `.over` above -0.1 dB; at rest (not playing) the meters fall to the floor. Not done: a track volume of its own (Gain is it), sends / buses, a meter per channel.
- **Insert at the playhead, the playhead's line, sound when scrubbing, zoom about the playhead, the trim two-up** (as Resolve's; arithmetic in the data module and `imageViewer_editAudio.js`, tested in `tests/test_edit_insert_zoom_scrub.mjs`, the panel in `tests/test_edit_timeline_browser.py` `test_11`..`test_16`). **Insert**: the Add menu's "Files…" and "Selected canvas nodes" are an INSERT edit at the playhead (`_editTlAdd(.., {insert, track})` -> `D.insertEdit`; before, they appended at the end): films and pictures go in in their order - canvas nodes LEFT TO RIGHT by `pos` (then top to bottom, `D.canvasOrder`; `selected_nodes` is keyed by id, so the order they were picked in is not known) - on the video track of the selected clip (`_editTlInsertTrack`), else V1. On V1 a shot under the playhead is split there (`splitWithLink`, its linked sound too) and they go between the halves; at a cut, at that cut; in a gap, at that moment with the rest of the gap after them; past the end, with a gap before; a refused split (a frame from an end, a dissolve, a loop) at the nearer end. Everything that begins at or after the playhead on the OTHER tracks (placed clips, sound clips, texts; not the bed) moves on by the inserted length, so the film after the playhead stays together. On a placed track: its clip there split, that track's clips from the playhead on moved up, the new ones placed one after the other; the other tracks stay. One undo step; the playhead then stands at their end (`_editTlSeek(end)`). Sounds still go to a sound track at the playhead, placed. "Files onto <track>…" is unchanged (placed at the playhead) and drops keep their own rule. **The playhead's line** is grabbed anywhere down the tracks (`_editTlHeadHit`, `HEAD_PX` = 4 either side of `ui.head`): capture `pointermove` / `pointerdown` listeners on `ui.rows`, so the line comes BEFORE the clip under it; the cursor is `ew-resize` (`.head-hot` / `.head-drag`, `!important` over the clips' inline cursors) and the clip's trim hover is put away. The rule against edit points: where an edit point of the track under the pointer (`D.editStops`) lies within half a frame of the playhead, the press is the edit point's (a cut the playhead is parked on can still be clicked); never with the blade. Its drag is the ruler's (`_editTlScrubDrag`, shared): snapped as the ruler is (`noHead`). **Sound** (`EditAudioMixin`): two switches in the bar, Resolve's pair - `.edit-audio-btn` (Lucide `volume-2` / `volume-x`, Ctrl+Shift+M) is ALL the cut's sound, playing and scrubbing (`_editAudioGain` multiplies every volume in `_editPlayMix` / `_editPlaySounds`), scrubbing only is Shift+M (Shift+S is the edit jump) and the speaker's caret menu (`.edit-audio-more`); kept per viewer in localStorage `imageviewer.viewer.editAudio` ({play, scrub}, try/catch); the export / render is not touched. **Scrubbing**: `editScrubAt(t)` is fed by the scrub drags (the raw time under the pointer, not the frame, so a slow drag has a speed), `_editTlGoFrame` (arrows, Home / End, Shift+A / S, a nudge that moves the playhead) and J's backwards timer - never while `isPlaying` (`editPlayStart` calls `_eaSilence`). The AudioContext is made lazily at the first scrub (a gesture), in the viewer's window (the pop-out's when popped out). Each file is decoded once: `_eaFetch` (the file through `buildImgUrl`; over 1 GB skipped) -> `decodeAudioData` (off the main thread) -> mono at `SCRUB.sr` (22050) on an `OfflineAudioContext` (off the main thread too; averaged here only without one), at most 2 at a time, cached in `_eaDecoded` with least-recently-used eviction over `CACHE_BYTES` (300 MB). A grain (`planGrain`, `fillGrain`): `SCRUB.grain` (50 ms) of wall time at `playbackRate` = the drag's speed in film seconds per second (`scrubRate`: clamped 0.25..4; a first move, or one after `SCRUB.fresh`, is a step at 1; no move is null = silence); its samples are the MIX at the playhead read forwards or backwards over grain x rate seconds of film (`scrubSources`: the picture on top as `pictureAt` has it and, in a dissolve, the one going out at 1 - mix; the bed; the clips of the live sound tracks; `clipVolume`, so a muted picture is silent; solo / off by `liveTracks`; outside 0.25x..4x speed silent as in playback; `srcAt` = `srcLocal` without its rounding, so retime and loop read the right samples). Fades of `SCRUB.fade` (12 ms) are baked into each grain and the next one starts as this one fades (a grain waits while the one before has more than its fade to run), so they cross-fade without a click; when the pointer stops no grain is made and the last runs out within 50 ms. A source not decoded yet is left out of the grain. `_eaGrains` keeps the last 64 grains (the tests read it with a stubbed AudioContext and `_eaFetch`). **Zoom about the playhead**: `_editTlZoom(zoom)` without a pointer (Ctrl+= / Ctrl+-, the bar's buttons, Shift+Z) keeps the playhead's x on screen (`D.zoomScroll`; the area's scroll measured from the rects, not `offsetLeft` - the area's offsetParent is not the scroller); a playhead out of view is centred. Ctrl + wheel stays about the pointer (Resolve's wheel zooms at the pointer too). Below Fit nothing scrolls, so the x moves with the scale there. **Trim two-up** (`EditTwoUpMixin`, `imageViewer_editTwoUp.js`; only with the Trim mode on): `editTwoUpShow(info, cut)` at the press of a trim / ripple / roll / slip / slide / retime and on every move of its drag (`_editTlTrimDown`), `editTwoUpPulse` after a nudge (`editTlNudgeEdit`, `editTlNudge`'s slide, `editTlSlipBy`); put away on release, `TWOUP_HOLD` (900 ms) after the last nudge, and in `editTlMode` when the Trim mode is left. `D.twoUpFrames` / `D.edgeFrame`: a roll, trim or ripple shows the outgoing clip's LAST frame left and the incoming clip's FIRST right (the neighbour on V1 by order, on a placed track by time), a slip the clip's new first and last, a slide the last of the clip before and the first of the clip after; the frame is the film frame at that edge, its source moment `srcLocal` of the frame's middle (retime and loop counted), and the `<video>` is sought to exactly that (`seeked` takes the newest wish when a seek was running; the frame that is up stays up meanwhile, as the player's layers do). It is a layer of its own in `#viewport` (`.edit-twoup`, a `<video>` and an `<img>` per side, labels "Out · name · timecode · src timecode", `data-frame` / `data-src-frame` / `data-clip` for the tests), rebuilt when the viewport moved into another document, so full screen and the pop-out carry it; the player's layers and the playhead are not touched. **At rest**: `editTwoUpRest` (called from `editPlaySetFrame`, `editPlayStop`, `editTlMode`, `editTlRefresh`, the pulse's expiry and a drag's end) shows the two-up whenever, in the Trim mode and not playing, the playhead is within half a frame of a `D.editStops` stop of the selected clip's / edit point's track (else V1) - as a roll `{a, b}`, so a film end or gap shows one side and a "start / end of the track" label; it hides at once off a cut, shows after `TWOUP_REST_DELAY` (70 ms, debounce for scrubs); `_etuDrag` / `_etuTimer` (a trim or nudge in progress) are left alone, and a trim that ends on a cut keeps the view. **Pane clicks**: `.edit-twoup-side` takes pointer events (cursor pointer, orange hover outline); a click calls `editTwoUpPick(name)` (pointer / mouse / dblclick / contextmenu stopped at the pane, so nothing reaches the viewer): left -> `stepPoint` state `left` (the outgoing out, `trim-out`), right -> `right`; clicking the side that is the only one selected picks `both` (a roll), a pane while both are selected picks that side; a side the stop lacks (film start / end, gap) does nothing. The cut is `_editTwoUpStop()` (the stop under the playhead on the selected track); `_editTwoUpMark` sets `root.dataset.pick` (left / right / both), drawn as a green outline; focus goes back to the timeline body so `,` / `.` trim. Tests: `test_15c`, `test_15d` (pop-out). Test: `test_15b`.
- **Clips switched off** (D, Resolve's Clip Enable; the clip menu's "Disable clip / Enable clip"): `D.toggleDisabled(cut, list)` - the selected clips with their linked partners (`withPartners`, not an unlinked one); all off when any was on, else all on; one undo step. `disabled: true` on the clip (`tidyLink` keeps only true; `toSave` always states it), in the file `metadata.imageviewer.disabled` AND OTIO's own `enabled: false` (`timeline_io.clip_link` reads either, `_set_link` writes both, `enabled: true` again when switched on). Drawn `.disabled` (dimmed, strip and waveform greyed, the name struck through). Not seen, not heard: `pictureAt` treats a disabled V1 shot as a GAP (in its dissolve the shot going out plays on its own up to where the dissolve began; a dissolve out of it is the shot coming in on its own) and leaves disabled clips of the tracks out (what is under shows, or black); `soundItems` leaves disabled sound clips out (so scrubbing too). The render agrees frame for frame (`timeline_render.read_cut`: a disabled V1 shot becomes a `blank` of its length less both dissolves, the next shot comes in with a cut; disabled track clips are skipped; `tests/test_edit_disabled_keys.py` compares the JS and the Python per frame). The Resolve export writes the clip with `enabled="0"`. agentY's `from_otio` plays them (`agentLoses` says so).
- **Keyframes** (every number of the Inspector: zoom X / Y, position, anchor, rotation, the four crops, opacity, the volume - of a film and of a sound clip; arithmetic in the data module, tested in `tests/test_edit_keyframes.mjs`, the JS / Python parity, the render and the file in `tests/test_edit_disabled_keys.py`, the FCPXML in `tests/test_resolve_export.py`, the panel in `tests/test_edit_timeline_browser.py` `test_21_*`). **Model**: `fx.keys = { param: [{ t, v, i }] }` in `metadata.imageviewer.fx` beside the static numbers (`cleanKeys` / `timeline_io.clean_keys`, the same rules: `KEY_PARAMS` = the FX keys + `vol`, held to their ranges, sorted, one key per moment); `t` is CLIP time - seconds from the clip's first frame on the timeline, NOT source time: a slip, a retime, a loop and a take leave the keys where they are on the timeline; `i` how it goes on to the next key (absent linear, `in` x², `out` 1-(1-x)², `inout` smoothstep, `hold` a step: `easeShare`); a keyed param ignores its static number (`rawFx(clip, u)`, `clipFx(clip, u)`, `clipVolume(c, u)`, `paramAt`); old cuts without keys are unchanged. The flips are switches and are not keyed; the speed is one number per clip (constant: retime has no keys). **Edits** (each a new cut, one undo step): `toggleKeys` (the diamond: every param of the row keyed at u - removed, else added at its value there), `autoKey` (a change in the Inspector: a keyed param gets a key at the playhead, an unkeyed one its number - Resolve's auto-key once animated), `setKeys`, `removeKeys`, `clearKeys` (a row's reset: keys and number), `moveKeysAt`, `setKeyInterp`, `nextKeyTime`, `keyTimes`. `splitFx` divides them at a split (`splitClip`, the blade, the tails of `overwriteV1` / `overwriteTrack`): the second piece's re-based to its own time, a key at the cut on both sides with the value there, so the look does not jump. `unlinkClip` hands the volume keys to the sound clip (they stay on the audio part). **Inspector** (`_editInspKeying`, `_editInspKeys`): per row ◂ ◆ ▸ - the diamond hollow (no keys), lit outline (keys, not here), filled (a key at the playhead); u is the playhead in the clip's time, frame-snapped, disabled outside the clip; right-click it: `_editKeyInterpMenu` (linear / ease in / ease out / ease in and out / hold); the numbers shown are the ones at the playhead, and the Inspector redraws as the frame moves for a keyed clip (`_editTlPlayhead`). **Timeline**: the selected clip's key moments are small diamonds on its block (`_editTlKeyMarks`, `.edit-keymark`, round for an ease, red for a hold): dragged along the clip they move every key of that moment (`_editTlKeyDown` through `_editTlDrag`, onto frames and onto the playhead within the snapping distance; one undo step); a click selects it (`_editTlKeySel`, Del takes it away - `editTlKeyDelete`, Esc or a press elsewhere lets it go); right-click: the ease menu. No separate keyframe lane, and no curve editor: the previz curves panel (`previzCurves.js` / `curveUI.js`) works on the 3D scene model's channels and was not cheap to reuse - not done. **Preview**: each layer knows where its clip begins (`_epOrigin`), `_editPlayU` / `_editPlayT`; `_editPlayMirror` / `_editPlayOpacity` / `_editPlayMix` evaluate at that moment and `_editPlayTick` redraws a keyed clip every frame; keyed sound clips set their volume every frame (`ep.keyedSound`). **Render** (`timeline_render`): `key_value` / `ease_share` / `fx_at` / `effective(fx, u)` are the JS's arithmetic; a keyed clip is laid frame by frame at (k + 0.5) / fps of its own time (`film_pictures`, `covered`); `volume_curve` is a gain per sample from a key value every 10 ms (V1 films, overlays, sound clips). **FCPXML** (`resolve_export._keyframes`): a `<param>` with a `keyframeAnimation` under `adjust-transform` (position / scale / rotation / anchor), `trim-rect` (left / right / top / bottom), `adjust-blend` (amount) and `adjust-volume` (amount in dB), keys at `start` + clip time on the frame grid, each piece (a loop's turn, the in-out range) getting the keys inside it and one at its first frame; ease in / out / in-out -> `easeIn` / `easeOut` / `ease`, a hold -> a second key a frame before the next one at the same value. The EDL ignores keys (the README says so). agentY's `from_otio` plays clips without them and names them in `otio_dropped`; "Ask agentY" warns (`agentLoses`).
- **Several clips selected in the Inspector** (`imageViewer_editPanels.js`, `_editInspRender`; tests `test_22_*` in `tests/test_edit_timeline_browser.py`). The primary selection (`_editTlSel`, the last clicked) is what the Inspector shows; `_editTlSelAll()` is the list. `targetsFor(params)` gives the clips a row reaches, each as an `_editInspKeying` object (its own playhead time `u`, `inside`): pictures (V1 and video-track clips) for the FX params, the clips with sound (sound-track clips, non-still unmuted films) for `vol`; the primary is always one. Every write is built on the one cut and handed to `_editTlApply` once, so a change across N clips is ONE undo step (a slider drag commits once, as before): `setFx` (D.autoKey per target with the SAME absolute patch; a keyed param on a clip the playhead is not inside is skipped, an unkeyed one gets its number), `resetFx` / `clearRow` / `resetVol`, and the diamond (`toggleAll` in `_editInspKeys`: the mode comes from the primary's state, applied to every target the playhead lies inside; disabled when it crosses none). Prev/next key follow the primary. A row whose value differs across its targets (`mixed(param)`) gets `.insp-mixedrow`, its number field an empty value and a dash placeholder (`.insp-mixed`); typing sets it on all. No linked-partner logic is added: the Inspector never followed a link, so nothing is applied twice when both partners are selected. The Timing and File tabs (start, source in, length, speed, retime, loop, dissolve, order, name, colour) are NOT multi-applied - they ripple or are per clip - and the `.insp-multi` header line says so; selected texts share only their look. After an undo the multi-selection is let go (existing behaviour), so tests reselect.
- **Titles** (`imageViewer_editTitles.js` the table / layout / drawing, `imageViewer_editFonts.js` the fonts in the page, `title_render.py` the same in Python; tests `tests/test_edit_titles.mjs`, `tests/test_edit_titles.py`, `tests/test_edit_titles_parity.py`, `tests/test_edit_titles_browser.py`). **A title is a clip**: a clip of a video track (V2 and up - never V1, `transferClip` / `moveGroup` hold it above) with `title` (its words and look) and no file, so every clip edit is its edit - selection, marquee, moves and group moves, snapping, trims of both modes, ripple, roll, slide, the blade, delete / ripple delete, `,` / `.`, D, Copy / Paste Attributes ("Text look" = everything of its look but its words), the multi-clip Inspector, keyframes, undo, edit points, Shift+A / S, the two-up (a title has no frames of its own: it shows black there). `isStillClip(c)` is true for it (no source, speed, loop, in point; it grows as far as it is pulled). It is NOT a picture: `pictureAt` leaves it out (so it neither covers nor silences what is under it), `titlesAt` lists the titles on screen lowest track first with their clip time and `rank`; `timeline_render.covered` lays it like an overlay clip but never as a cover, `film_sound` never lets it replace sound. No dissolves: placed tracks have none (fade with opacity keys). **Look** (`TITLE_NUM` / `TITLE_COLORS` / `TITLE_FLAGS` / `TITLE_CHOICES`, the same table in both languages, `cleanTitle` / `clean_title` hold it): text, font (family), size (% of the frame height), bold, italic, underline, strike, caps, track (1/1000 em), line (× size), align (left / center / right / justify), valign (top / middle / bottom inside the text box), colour + op, outline sc / sw, shadow shc / shx / shy / shb / sho, box bgc / bgo / bgp / bgr (% of the size), the text box x / y (its middle, y down) / w / h (fractions of the frame). Keyed (`fx.keys`, the clip model): `tsize`, `ttrack`, `tline`, `tsw` (`TITLE_KEYS`; `titleAt(c, u)` / `title_at`). **One layout, two drawers**: `layoutTitle` / `layout` place every glyph by the font's own LINEAR advances (the browser measures at 200 px, Pillow at 2048 px - Pillow's advances at a small size are hinted), no kerning and no ligatures (what both can do alike), lines wrapped by words at the box's width, baselines from the face's ascent / descent (the server reads them; the browser is told), the same underline / strike rectangles, synthetic bold (an edge of the fill colour, 3 % of the size) and italic (a shear of 0.2 about the baseline) where the family has no such face (`resolveFace` / `resolve_face`, the same choice; a missing family falls back through `FALLBACK_FAMILIES`). Drawn in one order: box, shadow (Gaussian, sigma = blur), outline (all glyphs first), fill. The parity test renders cases in both and compares bounding boxes (3 px) and ink (Chrome's antialiasing is a shade heavier at small sizes). Complex scripts are drawn glyph by glyph - shaping is lost; a glyph missing in the face is a box in the render and another font in the browser. **Fonts**: `title_render.fonts()` scans the OS's font folders with Pillow (Windows' own and the user's, macOS's, `/usr/share/fonts` ... - no `fc-list`: no process may be started), cached for the process; GET `/imageviewer/fonts` (families, faces with id / asc / desc / url, no paths; `?refresh=1`), GET `/imageviewer/font_file?id=` hands out ONE scanned face by its id (a face of a .ttc taken out as a font of its own, `_ttc_face`) - nothing outside that list can be asked for. The page loads a face as a FontFace `bepicf-<id>` per document (pop-out included) and redraws when it comes; without the list (an older server) the picker offers web-safe families drawn by the browser. A missing family is said in the Inspector (`fontNote`) and in the render's `notes` (logged, and a toast after Export film). **The player**: one canvas per title on screen (`.edit-title-layer`, pooled), laid over the picture's rectangle like the old text layer, the words drawn in the frame there (`paintTitle`), the clip's transform / crop / opacity as a picture layer's (`fxPlace`); z-index 4 above the picture, 2 under it (the picture's layer to 3) when its track is lower and the picture does not cover the frame, not drawn when it does. Redrawn only when its look / size changes (`_sig`); `editPlayTitleLive` previews a slider drag. **Inspector**: tabs Title, Video, Timing, File; the Title tab writes the look to every selected title (one undo step), the words to this one; Lucide toggles (bold, italic, underline, strike, caps, the four aligns, the three vertical aligns; `imageViewer_editIcons.js`); Ctrl+B / I / U act inside the words field only (its keydown is stopped there, so the timeline's Ctrl+B never sees it). **Effects**: Titles add a title at the playhead / drop point on the track dropped on where free, else the top video track, else a new one (`addTitle`). **The file**: a `Clip.1` whose media reference is a `GeneratorReference.1` (`generator_kind` `imageviewer.title`), its look in `metadata.imageviewer.title`, its words also in `metadata.agentY.text` (agentY's `from_otio` reads title clips back as overlays, preset from `metadata.agentY.preset`, else a caption). **Older cuts**: their texts (markers) come in as titles - `timeline_io.texts_to_titles` in `editable`, `D.textsToTitles` for a cut kept in the page - on video tracks of their own above the others (as many as overlap), looking as the text did (the old black edge is the default outline, 7 %), the texts' switch the tracks' switch; `mark` names the marker so its metadata (agentY's preset, params) goes onto the clip, and the markers are gone at the next save. The old text track code (`cut.texts`, `kind: "text"`) is still there for a cut that holds texts in the page, but nothing makes one any more. The Resolve export writes titles as Basic Title `title`s (see its README for what does not come along).
- **Export to Resolve…** (Save menu, next to Export film; `imageViewer_editResolve.js`, `resolve_export.py`, tests `tests/test_resolve_export.py` - its package half needs PyAV, so ComfyUI's interpreter - and `test_resolve_export_*` in `tests/test_edit_timeline_browser.py`). The SAVED cut (saved first if changed) becomes a folder beside it, `<cut>_resolve/` or the next `_v###`, never over one that is there: `media/` (whole copies of the original files, active takes, sound clips, the bed, stills; one file one copy; same names from other folders get `_2`), `<name>.fcpxml` (1.9), `<name>.edl` (CMX3600, V1), `<name>.otio` (media pointing into `media/`, the takes that were not copied dropped), `README.txt`. **Routes**, all POST, `same_origin`, JSON: `/imageviewer/timeline_export_resolve_plan` (writes nothing: size, free, flagged, missing, refused, left out), `/imageviewer/timeline_export_resolve` (403 / 409 target there / 422 missing unless `continue_missing` / 507 no room, else a job id), `_status`, `_cancel`. A source must pass `path_access.may_serve`, not just `is_allowed`: a copy hands its bytes out, and an `.otio` may name anything. `resolve_export.Job` runs in a thread of its own, copies per file tmp + rename into `<name>.partial-<id>`, and renames that folder at the end, so a cancel or failure removes only that folder. **Time**: `build_model` turns the cut into pieces in whole timeline frames with the render's own rounding (`frame_counts`), all on `Fraction` from `rate_of` (23.976 is 24000/1001); `fcpxml` / `edl` only spell them. V1 is the spine from cut to cut with gaps filled, a dissolve a `transition` from where it starts; every other track is anchored to the V1 element it starts in, its `offset` on THAT element's own timeline (with a `timeMap`, the adjusted time: `start` = source / speed). Tracks: `lane` +n pictures, -1 the bed (A1), -2.. sound tracks. A film's sound on a track is the same asset with `srcEnable="audio"`, a muted picture `srcEnable="video"`. A loop is repeated clips (`_turns`), a ping-pong's way back a reversed `timeMap`; a still is `<video>`; titles are Basic Title `title`s on their lane with a `text-style-def` (font, size, face, colour, alignment, outline, shadow, tracking, line spacing; the text box's middle as `adjust-transform` position) - the box, vertical alignment, wrapping, strike, underline and title keys are not carried (the README says so); embedded timecode goes into `start`. **Why absolute `file:///` URLs**: Apple's DTD says an asset's src is a "file: URL", and Resolve's manual (20, ch. 58) says it finds media by the embedded paths and otherwise asks for a folder, searching its subfolders; nothing found says it takes a relative one. **Not verified inside Resolve**: `srcEnable` and the 1.9 element order are from memory of later DTDs (the fetched one is 1.1); the free-version codec flags (`free_version_note`) are from third-party reports and warn only, nothing is transcoded. Do not start `Resolve.exe` to find anything out, not even with `--version`: it starts the program.
- **Copy a clip, Paste Attributes** (Resolve's; `imageViewer_editPaste.js`, `EditPasteMixin`; arithmetic in the data module - `PASTE_ATTRS` / `PASTE_GROUPS`, `pasteCaps`, `pasteSource`, `pasteOffer`, `pasteAttributes`, `clipKeysTo` - tested in `tests/test_edit_paste.mjs`, the panel in `test_23_*` of `tests/test_edit_timeline_browser.py`). Ctrl+C or the clip menu's "Copy" puts a COPY of the selected clip or text, with what kind of thing it is, on `_editTlClipboard` (it survives the clip changing and the tab changing). Alt+V or "Paste Attributes…" opens `.edit-dialog.edit-paste` for the selected clips and texts: boxes grouped as the Inspector's (Video: zoom, position, anchor, rotation, flip, cropping, opacity; Audio: volume; Keyframes: ONE "include keyframes" switch; Timing: speed; Clip: name, colour; Text: its look), All / None, Apply. A box is disabled where the source has no such thing (by kind - a sound has no zoom, a still no speed or sound, a clip no text look; keyframes need keys) or none of the targets can take it; the rule for "has sound" is the Inspector's multi-clip one (a film that is not muted). Apply is one `_editTlApply` (one undo step), each attribute only onto the targets that take it, and puts the multi selection back (a pasted speed changes lengths, which the selection signature reads). Keys are in clip time already, so they land where they stood from the source's start; past the target's end they are cut off with a key at its end holding the value there (`clipKeysTo`). Without the keys switch an animated param is pasted as its value at the source's first frame and the target's own keys of it go. A section switch (`toff` / `coff` / `ooff`) goes with its attributes. The boxes checked are kept in localStorage `imageviewer.viewer.pasteAttrs` (try/catch). Both keys are in `EDIT_KEYS`; `_editTlFocused` is false in a field AND inside any `.edit-dialog`, so a dialog's own keys (Esc, Enter) are its own.
- **Audio crossfades and fades; Lock and the Auto Track Selector** (as Resolve's; arithmetic in the data module's sections "tracks for editing" and "audio transitions and fades", tested in `tests/test_edit_audio_xfade.mjs`; the JS / Python parity, the file, the render and agentY's ops in `tests/test_edit_audio_xfade.py`; the FCPXML in `tests/test_resolve_export.py` `test_audio_fades_and_crossfades_are_fade_handles`; the panel in `tests/test_edit_audio_xfade_browser.py`; the drawing and pointer in `imageViewer_editXfade.js`, `EditXfadeMixin`). **A crossfade** lies on the clip coming IN to a cut, `xfade: { len, curve, align }`, where two sound clips meet on one track: two films of V1 (`xfadeFrom`: no gap, no dissolve - a V1 dissolve already crosses the shots' own sound, which is the decision for "a video dissolve adds a matching audio crossfade": it needs none, so none is added and none can go there; a film whose sound was split onto a track gets its crossfade there - not muted, not a still, not looped) or two clips of a sound track that touch (within 1 ms). Unlike the dissolve (overlap, the film shorter) it MOVES NOTHING: its sound is the clips' handles, the media beyond the cut - `align` "centre" (half before the cut, half after; `xfadeSides`), "start" (the clip going out plays on past its end), "end" (the clip coming in begins before its start). `fitXfade` / `xfadeRoom`: shortened to the media there is (`inRoom` of the incoming, `outRoom` of the outgoing, never longer than the clips beside it), put on the side that has media when its own has none, refused ("not enough media beyond the cut") when neither has; `settle` -> `tidyAudio` drops a crossfade whose clips no longer meet and clamps it. Curves (`curveGain`): "+3" (default, equal power: sin), "0" (straight: the two add to 1), "-3" (x^1.5); a +3 dB crossfade is -3 dB per side at the cut. **Fades** `fadeIn` / `fadeOut` (seconds of the clip, straight lines of gain; on V1's sounding films, sound-track clips and the bed - whose fade out is agentY's `metadata.agentY.fade_out`, 1 s where nothing says, which the render always applied and the preview now does too); a crossfade on a side takes that side's fade away (`setFade` the other way round). One gain: `audioEnv(cut, sel)` -> `{ len, fi, fo, xin, xout }`, `envGain(env, u)` (u from -xin.b to len + xout.a; 0 outside), times the clip's volume / volume keys - `timeline_io.env_gain` / `audio_envs` and `timeline_render.env_curve` are the same arithmetic. **Preview** (026d427's one clock, untouched): the `<video>` on screen and the dissolve's under-layer get `envGain` in `_editPlayMix` (every frame while `ep.envOn`); `soundItems` gives each sound-track clip an `origin` (its own time 0) and `env`, stretched over its handles, and adds V1's handles as items of their own (`handle: "in"` before the cut, `"out"` after it, from `<audio>`; the picture still cuts, the next `<video>` still prerolls muted); `srcAt` reads the source before a clip's in. **Render**: `handled_sound` reads the handles (silence where the file has none before its in), `gain_line` the volume, `env_curve` the fades. **File**: `metadata.imageviewer.afade_in` / `afade_out` / `axfade` on the clip (`clip_audio` / `_set_audio`, always stated by `toSave` so that taking one off reaches the file), the bed's `afade_in` + agentY's `fade_out` (`bed_fades` / `_set_bed_fades`). **Resolve**: fade handles - `<fadeIn>` / `<fadeOut>` in `adjust-volume`'s `amount` param, before its keyframes; on sound tracks the two clips overlap by the crossfade (each piece stretched over its handle) and each fades over all of it; V1's crossfades become fades on the two spine clips over their part plus their handles as audio-only clips on a lane below (approximate, said in the README); curves `linear` / `easeOut` (+3) / `easeIn` (-3), from the shapes, not checked inside Resolve. **Panel**: `Shift+T` (`editTlAudioXfadeKey`: the selected edit point, else the selected clip's nearer cut, else the cut nearest the playhead on V1 and the auto-selected sound tracks), the clip menu ("Add audio crossfade at its start / end"), the bar `.edit-xfade` (selection kind `xfade` `{ track, i }`, `selId` "xf<track>:<i>"; its edges change the length - a centred one by twice the drag - its body selects, right-click: curve, alignment, remove; Del removes), the Inspector's Transition tab for it (Duration, Curve, Alignment) and the clip's Audio tab (Fade in / out for every selected sound clip, Add crossfade at its start / end); fade handles `.edit-fadeh` at the upper corners of sound blocks (the bed's too; rows ≥ 18 px, blocks ≥ 24 px), the faded part shaded (`.edit-fadecurve`); the bed's menu has fade in / out 1 s. **Lock** (`cut.lock[V1/A1/T]`, `k.lock`; the padlock in the header, lucide lock / lock-open) and **Auto Track Selector** (`cut.auto[id] === false` / `k.auto === false` is off; lucide square-dashed-mouse-pointer), both beside the playback switch and solo, distinct by their tooltips, saved (`metadata.imageviewer.lock` / `auto_select`, the stack's `texts_lock` / `texts_auto`; `track_flags` / `_set_flags`), compared by `sameCut` and so undoable. A lock is enforced at three places: the locked row (`.edit-track.locked`, hatched, its children dimmed) swallows presses in the capture phase (the playhead's line still scrubs), `_editTlApply` refuses any edit whose `lockViolation(before, after)` names a locked track (signature of its items and their places), and `_editTlDrag` holds every move and the release that would change one (`_editTlTrackUnder` offers no locked track); `deleteSelection` skips locked items (and partners there), the marquee leaves locked rows out, Paste Attributes and `rippleTracks` skip them. `editMask(cut)` (auto on, not locked) is what the edits that reach beyond their clip use: `rippleV1Remove` (Shift+Del of V1 shots: what begins at or after the shot's end on the masked tracks and texts moves up as far as V1 did - NEW: before this a V1 ripple moved no other track; a move back stops at a clip of that track that stays), `rippleV1Follow` (a V1 ripple trim in the Trim mode, `_editTlTrimCompute`), `insertEdit(…, mask)`, `splitAt(…, mask)` (Ctrl+B / S with no selected clip there), `editPoints(cut, mask)` (Up / Down), `editTlEditStep` (Shift+A / S off V1 when V1's selector is off), `_editTlPasteTargets`. Ripples ON a placed track still move only that track. agentY: `timeline_io.apply_ops` (POST /imageviewer/cut) refuses ops on a locked track and an add with no track skips locked ones; agentY's `from_otio` / `otio_dropped` and agenty_core's `edit_cut` know both.
- **The volume line; sound effects** (the volume line in `imageViewer_editVolLine.js`, `EditVolLineMixin`; the effects' table in `imageViewer_audioFxData.js` - pure, and `audio_fx.py` is the same table for the render, kept in step - the panel side in `imageViewer_editAfx.js` (`EditAfxMixin`: library drops, badges) and `imageViewer_editAfxPlay.js` (`EditAfxPlayMixin`: the WebAudio graph); tests `tests/test_edit_audio_xfade.mjs` (the line), `tests/test_edit_audio_fx.mjs`, `tests/test_edit_audio_fx.py` (JS / Python parity, the file, each effect rendered on test tones, the cut rendered, the Resolve bake), the browser half in `tests/test_edit_audio_xfade_browser.py`). **The volume line** (Media Composer's / Resolve's rubber band) is drawn over every sound clip (sound-track clips, V1's films with sound) on rows of at least `VOL_ROW_PX` (28), on a dB scale (`volY` / `yVol`, 0 dB at the top, `VOL_DB_FLOOR` -60 at the bottom); its points ARE the Inspector's volume keys (`fx.keys.vol`, clip time, their eases), no second model: `volLine`, `setVolLine` (a drag of the line: the clip's volume when flat, else every point by the same dB; the dB in the header's note), `addVolPoint` (Alt+click - Alt, as Resolve's; Alt-drag on a clip's BODY still slips, because the line is its own element, `.edit-volline .hit`), `moveVolPoint` (time held between its neighbours, onto frames and the playhead within `SNAP_PX`, `_editTlVolSnap`), `removeVolPoint` (Del with `_editTlVolSel`, or Alt+click on it), right-click a point: `_editKeyInterpMenu`. Several selected: only the pressed clip changes. A point or fade handle under the playhead's line takes the press itself (`_editTlHeadHit`). The track header's right-click menu has "Show volume curve" (`view.volCurve[id] === false` hides it; kept with the cut's view). Fades and crossfades multiply with it - the player per frame (`clipVolume * envGain`), the render every 10 ms (`gain_line`, `env_curve`). **Sound effects** (Resolve's Effects Library / Fairlight FX): `AFX` - EQ (high-pass, low shelf, peaking mid, high shelf, low-pass), Compressor, Limiter, Reverb (room / hall / plate presets, mix, decay, pre-delay), Delay (time, feedback, mix), Gain, Pan; an effect `{ type, on, p, keys }`, `cleanAfx` / `audio_fx.clean_chain` hold it (ranges, `MAX_AFX`), `KEYED` = mix (reverb, delay), gain, pan - a clip chain's keys in CLIP time, a track's in FILM time (`afxParam` / `param_at`). On a clip `clip.afx` (`afxTakes`: a sound-track clip, or a film that sounds), on a sound track `k.afx`, on the bed `cut.afx.A1` (`afxTrack`, `trackAfx`, `setTrackAfx`); order: clip effects -> clip volume, fades, crossfades -> track effects -> the mix (there is no track volume; a Gain effect on the track is one). A split re-bases the second piece's keys (`shiftAfx`). **Library**: the Effects panel's "Audio FX" category (lucide icons `afxEq` ...), items dragged with their own type `application/x-imageviewer-afx` (`AFX_DRAG`): over a sound clip block it is that clip, over a sound track's empty lane or its header (`.edit-headrow[data-track]`) the whole track (`_editAfxTargetAt`), lit with `.afx-target`; a locked track is not lit and refuses (`editAfxDrop`); a double-click puts it on the selected sound clips, else the selected track (`editAfxAdd`). Marks: "fx" on a clip (`.edit-badge-afx`), a badge per effect in a track's header (`.edit-afxbadge`, EQ / CMP / LIM / REV / DLY / GN / PAN) - a click selects the track (selection kind `track`, `selId` "trk:<id>"; clicking a sound track's name does too) and opens it. **Inspector**: the clip's Audio tab has an "Audio FX" part, a selected track a "Track FX" tab (`afxSection`): per effect its bypass switch, ▲ ▼ remove, its params, a diamond on the keyed ones (a key at the playhead; with keys a change is a key there - `patchAfx`'s auto-key), "+ Add effect…"; with several clips selected adding goes to all, a change to every selected clip that has the same effect at that place. Paste Attributes has an "Audio FX" group (the source's chain in place of the target's; its keys only with "Include keyframes"). **Preview** (`_editAfxEnsure`, called where `_editPlayMix` and `_editPlaySounds` set loudness; the one clock untouched - nothing here seeks or starts): an element is put through WebAudio only the first time a clip or track it plays has effects (MediaElementSource -> the clip's nodes -> a GainNode that now carries the loudness, `_epVol`, the element at volume 1 -> the track's bus, one per track per window, its chain rewired when it changes -> the speakers); nodes: BiquadFilter (pass filters at Q -3.0103 dB = Butterworth), DynamicsCompressor (+ a make-up gain; the limiter is one at ratio 20, knee 0, 1 ms attack), Convolver (normalize off, `reverbIR`: mulberry32 noise darkened by the preset's tone, -60 dB over the decay, unit energy per channel - the same samples as `audio_fx.reverb_ir`), Delay with a feedback Gain, Gain, StereoPanner (`panGains` is its arithmetic); keyed params set each frame (`_editAfxTick`). **Render** (`audio_fx.apply_chain`, from `film_sound` per clip after `handled_sound` and per sound-track bus / the bed): EQ, compressor and limiter as ffmpeg's own filters in PyAV's in-process filter graph (`highpass` / `lowshelf` t=s w=1 / `equalizer` t=q / `highshelf` / `lowpass`, `acompressor` with a linear threshold and make-up and the knee as 10^(dB/20) - hence 0..18 dB -, `alimiter` with `level=false`; `afxFfmpeg` / `ffmpeg_filters` write the same strings - tested); reverb (an FFT convolution with the same impulse response - afir's arithmetic), delay (the feedback loop, block by block), gain and pan in numpy, so their keys follow the preview sample for sample. NOT bit-identical: WebAudio's DynamicsCompressor has its own attack / release curves and an automatic make-up, ffmpeg's acompressor / alimiter theirs (said in `audio_fx.py`). `film_sound(…, only, fx)` renders one part ("V1", "A1", a track) or the dry sound; `render_sound` writes it as a 16-bit WAV. **File**: `metadata.imageviewer.afx` on clips and tracks (the bed's on the first audio track; `clip_audio`, `track_flags`, `_set_audio`, `_set_flags`; `editable()["afx"]["A1"]`). **Resolve**: FCPXML carries no Fairlight FX, so Export to Resolve… has "Bake audio effects" (shown when the cut has effects, on by default; `bake_fx`): `bake_parts` / `bake_model` - each part with effects (V1, a sound track, A1) becomes `media/<part>_fx.wav` (rendered in the job, `render_sound(…, only=part)`, the in-out range too) on that part's lane (V1's films then `srcEnable="video"` and the WAV on a lane of its own), its original clips on a muted lane of their own (`enabled="0"`, named "… (dry)", `audioRole` "<track> (dry)" - unverified inside Resolve how it names the tracks), one README line; the fcpxml names no effect. Off: the README says the clips come without them. Clip effects on a picture track's clip (V2..) are rendered, but are not baked for Resolve.
- **The Edit panel's dialogs stand over the timeline** (`imageViewer_editDialogs.js`, `EditDialogsMixin`; test `test_24_*`). `_editTlCentre(box)` puts any absolutely placed box over the middle of `_editTlUi.body`'s rect, held 8 px inside the window, and centres it again on the window's resize and on the box's or the timeline's ResizeObserver (both from the box's own window, so the pop-out works); it measures its containing block by placing the box at 0,0 first, so it works in the panel, over the whole viewer and in a shade. Every dialog opened from the panel goes through it: Save cut as, Export to Resolve, Auto edit / ask agentY, Recreate, Paste Attributes, and the viewer's shared file picker and export dialog through `_editTlPicker(spec)` / `_editTlExportDialog(spec)` (Open cut, Add files, the Browse buttons, Export film). A new dialog in the Edit panel calls `_editTlCentre` after appending it. Context menus stay at the pointer.
- **Opening a cut, from everywhere** (`imageViewer_editOpen.js`, `EditOpenMixin`; the pure list in `imageViewer_editRecent.js`, tested in `tests/test_edit_open.mjs`; the panel in `tests/test_edit_open_browser.py`; the routes in `tests/test_push_otio_route.py`). Open cut… is on the Edit bar (the `.edit-open` button, Lucide folder-open, next to Save; its `.edit-open-recent` caret opens the Open recent menu), in the Out menu (Open cut… and Open recent…), on **Ctrl+O** in the panel (`editTlKeyDown`, so only with the timeline focused and never while typing; listed in `EDIT_KEYS`) - not on the viewer's top bar (there was a `#open-cut-btn` beside the tabs; Sebastian had it removed 2026-11-04: Open cut belongs to the Edit panel). `editTlOpenMenu` (Open cut… plus the recent cuts) still opens a cut with no cut tab up - `editTlOpenPath` makes the tab and the panel. **Open recent**: the last `RECENT_MAX` (10) `.otio` paths opened (`editTlOpenPath`) or saved (`_editTlLoaded`), newest first, in localStorage `imageviewer.edit.recent` (try/catch); the menu asks `/imageviewer/probe_paths` once (1.2 s cap) and greys (`(missing)`) what is gone. The picker (`editTlOpenDialog`) starts in the folder of the open cut (`editTlFilePath`), else of the newest recent one, else where it was last (`_remember('cut')`). **An .otio from outside**: dropped from the desktop on the viewport or the timeline (`editTlDropHasCut` / `editTlOpenDropped`; the bytes go through `/imageviewer/timeline_upload`, which keeps a cut in `<input>/edits/cuts`, a file of that name being reused only when its BYTES are the same - `timeline_media.place_cut_upload`), and from the agent: `POST /imageviewer/push` with an `.otio` that has NO film beside it (`push_open.split_lone_cuts`, inside `path_access.is_allowed`, the file must exist) sends `imageviewer.edit.open {path}` (the page opens it as a cut) and answers it as `cuts`; with a film beside it the old rule holds (the film is shown, `panel: "edit"` opens the cut). agentY-core's `show_in_viewer` counts `cuts` as shown.
- **Trim mode: the playhead stays on the selected cut** (`_editTlTrimFollow` in `imageViewer_editTrim.js`, called at the top of `editTwoUpRest`, which runs on every redraw, frame change, mode change and stop; test `test_25_*`). With an edit point selected in the Trim mode the playhead goes to it (`_editTlEditTime`), and goes again whenever the point or its moment changes (ripple, roll, , / ., a drag, undo) and after playing stops (`_editTlFollow.played`) - keyed by the point and its frame, so a scrub or an arrow by hand stays put until the cut next moves. While playing nothing is touched (play around edit owns it). A cut out of view is scrolled to the middle (`_editTlCentreOn`, and `_editTlPlayhead`'s own paging centres instead of paging while a point is selected in the Trim mode); never during a drag (the pointer reads time off the scrolled strip, it would run away), and the playhead's paging is off during any drag too. A trim drag in the Trim mode makes its edge the selected edit point from the press, so the playhead follows the edge live; snapping during a drag uses the playhead where it stood at the press (`_editTlDragHead`), not the following one. `editTlMode` resets the follow, so re-entering the Trim mode takes the playhead to the selected cut. Leaving the Trim mode or letting go of the point frees it.
- **The timeline's ruler, readout, sections and heights** (as Resolve's; `imageViewer_editSections.js`, `EditSectionsMixin`; arithmetic `playheadLabel` / `parsePlayhead` / `stepTrackHeight` / `splitHeight` / `TRACK_MIN_PX` / `TRACK_MAX_PX` in the data module, tested in `tests/test_edit_tl_layout.mjs`, the panel in `test_26_*`). The DOM: `.edit-main` = `.edit-heads` (the readout `ui.tc`, then `ui.hbox` = `ui.headsV`, `ui.hsplit`, `ui.headsA`) beside `ui.scroll` (sideways only) > `ui.area` (`height: 100%`, never scrolls up and down) = `ui.ruler`, `ui.rows` (= `ui.secV`, `ui.splitter`, `ui.secA`, each section `overflow-y: auto` with its scroll bar hidden), and the overlays (`ui.head`, `ui.drop`, the snap line, the in / out lines, ghosts, the marquee) absolutely over the whole area - so the ruler stays on top, and the playhead's, snap and in / out lines run through both sections. `editTlRefresh` puts each row (and its header) into its kind's section - texts and video tracks with the room above them in `secV`, A1 and the sound tracks with the room below them in `secA` - and then `_editTlSecSync`: the video section's height `D.splitHeight(view.split, …)` (the splitter dragged: `view.split`, px, kept with the cut's view; double-click: null again, fitting the video tracks - up to 70 % of the room when not all fits), the headers' column set to the same pixel heights (the readout as tall as the ruler), and the video section held down at V1 (`ui.vPinned`, set by its scroll event: until scrolled up). A section's scroll moves its headers (`_editTlHeadsTo`, which marks the value so the headers' echoed scroll event is not taken for a hand's - it would scroll the section back); the headers' scroll bar moves the section. **Anything that measures a row against a section must use `_editTlRowBox(row)`** (the part its section shows, null when scrolled away; `edge`: a strip at the section's edge) - the marquee, the ghost, the trim highlight, the selected edit's marks and the filmstrips (`_editMediaDraw` clips each block to its own section) do; `_editTlRoomAt` reads the rooms per section (above `secV` / below `secA` counts while the room is scrolled out of view). **The wheel** (`_editTlWheel`, on `.edit-main`): Ctrl zooms about the pointer; Shift makes every track of the section under the pointer a notch taller or lower (`_editTlSizeBy`: `view.heights` per row, and `view.scale` per section for rows of no height of their own - a new track; over the ruler or the splitter both sections; a browser that turns Shift+wheel into deltaX is read too); Alt, or the wheel over the headers, scrolls that section; plain wheel moves along the film as before (over the ruler too), and up and down only when there is nowhere to move along.
