While Shift+wheel turns the panel is redrawn once a frame, and `_editTlSizing` holds off new filmstrip requests like a drag does until the wheel rests 250 ms (then one redraw asks for the right sheets); rows under 28 px lose their volume line on the redraw. **The readout** (`ui.tc`, left of the ruler over the headers): the playhead as `HH:MM:SS:FF` at the cut's fps or as a frame number (`D.playheadLabel`), written by `_editTlTcDraw` from `_editTlPlayhead` only when its text changes; click switches, right-click chooses (and Go to…), double-click opens a field (`D.parsePlayhead`: a timecode with 1-4 parts, a frame, `+n` / `-n`; Enter goes, Esc / leaving does not). The mode is per viewer, localStorage `imageviewer.viewer.editTimecode` (in try/catch).
- **Several clips, takes, recreate** (`imageViewer_editTakes.js`, `EditTakesMixin`; arithmetic in the data module, tests `tests/test_edit_takes.mjs`, `tests/test_edit_takes.py`, `tests/test_edit_takes_browser.py`). Ctrl+click toggles a clip in the selection, Shift+click takes the range on one track; `_editTlSel` stays the last clip clicked and `_editTlMulti` ({list, sig}) is the list it belongs to - held to the cut by `selSignature` (counts and refs), so an edit that adds / removes / reorders clips, an undo or any other `_editTlSel` lets it go (`_editTlSelList` is the one reader). Outlined `.msel`; `viewerState().edit.selected` is the list `[{name, file, in, out, track, id}]` (id `V1:3` / `V2:0`, in/out of the source, 50 at most) plus `edit.otio`; text / dissolve selections stay one object as `edit.selected_item`. Right-click a clip: "Recreate with changes…" (dialog `.edit-recreate`, saves a dirty cut first, then `agentYViewerBridge.recreate({text, clips, otio})`), and "Takes" / "Retime to fit" for a clip with takes. **Takes**: `clip.takes = [{file, version, created, note, active, len?}]` (`metadata.imageviewer.takes` in the .otio, `timeline_io.clean_takes`; the active take's file is also the media reference's `target_url`, set through `may_use`); `addTake` / `switchTake` / `retimeToFit` in the data module and `timeline_io.add_take` do the same arithmetic. POST `/imageviewer/take` ({otio, takes:[{from_file, file, version, note}], retime}) writes the takes into the .otio (tmp + replace) and sends ComfyUI's `imageviewer.edit.take` ({otio, takes (with created), saved, retime}); each panel's `editTlTakeIn` applies it to every open cut that is that .otio or uses `from_file` as one undo step, and with `saved` to the saved baseline too (so the take is not an unsaved change). **A cut changed by agentY** (`edit_cut` in agenty_core): POST `/imageviewer/cut` ({otio, ops?}) - no ops reads the cut (`edit`, `length`); ops (`timeline_io.apply_ops`: `add` a file at `at` seconds on A2../V2.. or the first track with room, `set` in/length/at/volume/name (V1: in/length/gap, rippling), `remove`; V1 and A1 take no placed clip) are saved through `timeline_io.change` = `save`/`rebuild`, all or none, and ComfyUI's `imageviewer.edit.changed` ({otio, edit, said}) goes out; `editTlChangedIn` (editTakes mixin) makes that the open cut's cut AND saved baseline as one undo step, detaching the old cut and the undo stack (refs moved); unsaved user edits are the step before (one Ctrl+Z back). Tests `tests/test_edit_cut.py`, `tests/test_edit_cut_browser.py`. `ImageViewerSend` writes provenance (`provenance_write.py`: `agent_metadata/<name>.agenty.json` key `provenance` + `agent_metadata/<name>.prov.json.gz` in a folder beside the file, or the old ones right beside it when a file has them; secrets redacted) so a shot can be made again.
- **Playing a cut** (`imageViewer_editPlay.js`, `EditPlayMixin`; what is on screen at a moment is `pictureAt(cut, t, live)` - the clip of the highest live video track, else the cut's own, else black - and what is heard under it `soundItems`): hooked in like previz, by a question asked first - `_editPlayOn` / `isEditTab()` - in `getImgCount`, `setFrame`, `play`, `stop` and `switchTab`, so the whole transport (scrub, step, loop, the ctrl-dragged range, the fps field) works on the film's frames. The clip at the frame is shown from its own file through the elements everything else already reads: a picture in `imgBase`, a film in `videoBase`. There are two `<video>`s taking turns - the one on screen IS `this.videoBase`, the other holds the next clip sought to its in point - and they change places at a cut, so a cut costs no loading; the viewer's own element is handed back in `editPlayLeave`. **A dissolve is played as one**: `pictureAt` gives the clip coming in and, as `under`, the one going out with `mix` (0..1); the outgoing clip keeps running in a second layer and the incoming one lies over it at that opacity, their volumes crossing the same way (`_editPlayUnder` / `_editPlayMix`). Which layers those are depends on what the clips are - the two `<video>`s, the `<img>`, and a second `<img>` (`_epImgB`) for picture into picture. `updateTransform` skips an unchanged transform, so every layer the player owns takes the viewer's zoom and pan from `_editPlayMirror` (called from `updateTransform` and after each show), not from being `videoBase` at the right moment. While playing, **the film's clock is ONE clock, the wall clock** (`ep.t` at `ep.wall`, `_editPlayNow` = the main window's `performance.now()`, also popped out), set again only where the film jumps (`_editPlayShow(t, true)`: a seek, a loop going round; at a cut the clock runs on, what it is past the cut kept) - NOT the clip's own `currentTime`: that stands still for ~50 ms while the next clip starts at every cut (and while a `<video>` waits for data), and with it as the clock every sound track ran ahead and was sought back each time it was 0.15 s ahead - the same stretch heard twice on every track, several times in ten seconds of a cut of short shots. Everything follows the clock (`followPlan` / `followRate`, `FOLLOW`, tested in `tests/test_edit_play_follow.mjs`): the `<video>` on screen every frame (`_editPlayFollow`), the bed and each sound-track clip (`<audio>` elements, `_editPlaySounds`, every 200 ms, every frame with keyed volumes) - a little drift nudges the rate (at most 5 %, 1 % steps), only past `FOLLOW.seek` (0.25 s) is it sought, and a sought element is left to settle (`settle`, and while `seeking` / `readyState < 3`); a sound is sought where its clip starts and where the film jumps (`jump`), never for drift. The clip after a cut, ready in the other `<video>`, is started muted and unseen its start-up time before the cut (`_editPlayPreroll`; the time learnt per player from how far off the last one came in, `_epLag`), so the picture is on the clock at the cut. `tests/test_edit_play_audio_browser.py` plays real media (ffmpeg, `HARNESS_MEDIA_DIR`, `HARNESS_MEDIA_DELAY_MS`) and counts the seeks. `_videoOnTimeUpdate` / `_videoOnMeta` / `_videoOnEnded` stand down while it is on. `pictureAt` / `playAt` / `filmFrames` in the data module are its arithmetic.
- **One History for all tabs** (`imageViewer_historyData.js` - pure, tested in `tests/test_history_data.mjs`; `HistoryMixin`; the browser side in `tests/test_history_groups_browser.py` and `tests/test_history_on_cut_tab_browser.py`; asked for by Sebastian 2026-11-04). There is ONE stack, `history[HISTORY_KEY]` (`"all"`), newest first, and `historyKey()` always answers it; every push goes through `pushHistorySnapshot(tab, snapshot, { dedupe })`, which stamps `fromTab` (the tab it was made in) on the first frame, de-dupes against that tab's newest (`liveIndexOf`), shifts what points in by index (`onHistoryPrepended`: the open index, the compare pair, every tab's `tabViewState.historyIndex`) and trims to the limit (one number now, default 100; the old 40-per-tab default is not taken over from a saved state). Methods that take a history `key` ignore it (older callers passed a tab's key). **The strip keeps its tiles**: `renderHistoryPanel` makes a tile once per snapshot (`_historyTiles`, a WeakMap by the snapshot, with a signature of what the tile shows - mode, thumbnail URL, group, label, tag, tab, `historyAgentTagSig`) and on every pass only re-paints what depends on its place (`dataset.idx`, title, highlights); the handlers read the index off the tile, never a captured one. A tile whose thumbnail failed is made again. Headings are kept the same way (`_historyHeadings`), and the strip's children are not replaced when they are already the right ones. A new render used to remake all N tiles twice (switchTab, then showInViewerTab). **Saved sequences are packed**: `persistViewerState` writes every snapshot of several frames (history and `allTabs`) as ONE frame - its first, as it is - carrying the rest as differences from the frame before in `_imageViewerSeq` (`packSnapshot` / `unpackSnapshot`, `imageViewer_historyData.js`: a string as [shared start, middle, shared end], any other value as [value], a key gone as []); `restoreViewerState` unpacks, and an older save is read as it is. An older viewer reading a new save sees a sequence as its first frame. About 3.6x smaller (500 snapshots with sequences: 2.1 MB -> 0.6 MB), against ComfyUI's page sharing ~5 MB of localStorage. **A click shows the snapshot in the tab it was made in** (`openHistorySnapshot` switches there first, so switchTab hands the tab it leaves its own picture back), or with that tab closed in the Viewer tab (below); the open tile is the snapshot looked at, else the newest of the tab that is up (`_historyOpenIndex`). On a cut's tab (`historyBorrows`) a plain click selects AND loads the snapshot into the Source viewer (`_historyToSource` -> `editSrcLoadItems`, opening it; Ctrl / Shift only select, a double-click opens it in its tab); the old "History of" dropdown is gone. **Groups** are the `group` the frames carry (ImageViewerSend's, or the user's): the strip is drawn by group (`historySections`: the group used last first, newest first inside, "No group" headed only when there are groups); a heading is DRAGGED up or down the strip to order the groups (Sebastian 2026-11-10: `historyGroupOrder`, a list of names with "" for No group, persisted beside `historyCollapsed`; `historySections(..., order)` puts the groups not in it first by recency, then the order; `_historyWireGroupDrag` on the strip lands it before the heading whose middle is below the pointer, `moveHistoryGroup` / `moveGroupInOrder`; once there is an order a group new to it is pinned on top at its first render, so renders never shuffle headings; a rename keeps the place, `renameInOrder`), the arrow keys and Ctrl+Shift ranges follow that order (`historyDisplayIndices`); a heading folds its group (`historyCollapsed`, persisted), takes thumbnails dropped on it (`_historyDragSnaps`) and is renamed by its ✎ or a right-click (`_historyGroupRenameField`, an input in place of the name - Enter / leaving it renames, Esc does not; `renameHistoryGroup(from, to)`: a name that is there merges the two, a folded group stays folded; a single click rebuilds the strip, so a double-click on a heading never reaches the same element); the thumbnail menu's "Move to group ▸" (`_historyGroupMenuRows`, a `.thumb-ctx-keep` row that keeps the menu up) lists the groups, "New group…" and "No group" - `setHistoryGroupOf` sets every frame, and the copies tabs show. **Migration**: a saved per-tab history is merged on restore (`mergeTabHistories`: taken in turns, every tab's newest first, in tab order, each stamped with its tab). Closing a tab keeps its snapshots, except a dropped file's (its blob: URLs die with the tab); dropped files' snapshots are never saved. Clear clears the whole history (with a filter on, what it shows). **The one Viewer tab** (`HISTORY_VIEW_TAB` = `folder_history`, "🖼 Viewer"; asked for by Sebastian 2026-11-04): media that is only looked at has no tab of its own any more - a loader node sent to the viewer, files opened from the file browser, files dropped in, exports all go through `showInViewerTab(snapshots, {show, nodeId, dedupe})`: each a snapshot of the History made in that tab (the first on top), the first shown. Tabs of their own are left for documents (cut, Paint, 3D / world, Motion, code, dataset). **Roto and SAM3 are modes of the Viewer tab**: a Roto / SAM3 Collector node's picture is a group of it too ("Roto: <source>", "SAM3: <source>", or its `tab_name`), every snapshot a node sent remembers it (`fromNode`, `snapshotNode`; folded `send_<id>` tabs get it from the key), and the Viewer tab's node (`tabSourceNodeIds[folder_history]`, which the Parameters panel and `ensureToolNode` read) is the one of the snapshot on screen (`openHistorySnapshot`; a change rebinds the tool only if it moves it, `_toolRebindIfMoved`). A tool brought onto a node by the canvas selection shows that node's newest snapshot when the Viewer tab is up (`_toolShowNodePicture`). **What a Send to Viewer node or the agent sends is a group of the Viewer tab, not a tab** (asked for by Sebastian 2026-11-05): `updateData` asks `_feedGroupFor` (a `ImageViewerSend` node: group = its tab's old label, `senderTabInfo`; a send naming a `tab_name` with no such node: `namedTabInfo`'s label) and hands the render to `takeRenderInViewer(snapshot, {group, nodeId, focus})` - a group the frames carry (agentY's stage) wins over the tab name. **It is put on screen only while the newest is**: the Viewer tab up, not comparing, live or on the top snapshot (`currentHistoryIndex === 0`); looking at an older one or at another tab it only joins the History. `focus_tab` / `open` (the agent asked) always show it. **`group` names it; `tab_name` is its old name**: Send, Roto and SAM3 nodes keep a `tab_name` input in its place (saved workflows hold widget values by position) but the JS hides it and moves a value it holds into `group` (`adoptOldTabName`); Python takes `group or tab_name` and stamps it on the frames (`_name_frames`, also in `_push_tab` and `_send_model`); `/imageviewer/push` takes `group`, else `tab_name`, else "agentY". On restore the old media tabs (`isMediaTabKey`: `loader_*`, `folder_<time>_<n>`, `dropped_*`, `folder_exports`) and the old feed tabs (`isFeedTabKey`: `named:*`, and `send_*` keys the graph says are a Send node's - `_isSendTabKey`; a send tab restored before the graph is there is folded at the next update, `foldTabsIntoViewer`) are folded into it (`foldIntoTab`, a feed tab's snapshots without a group put into one named as the tab was), in the first one's place, showing what the one that was up showed. **Opening a space**: each of the bar's 3D / Motion / Paint / Timeline menus starts with "Open 3D… / Motion… / Paint… / Edit…" (`spaceOpen(kind)`, `imageViewer_barGroups.js`): the tab of that kind that is open, else the newest recent file still there (Paint and Motion: `imageViewer_recentDocs.js`, written on open and save; cuts: Open recent's list), else an empty one (a white canvas, an empty cut, the Motion tab that asks for a scene, an empty scene `previz:new-<n>`, whose empty scene is kept on restore). A second document still opens as a second tab. Tests: `tests/test_viewer_spaces_browser.py`, `tests/test_recent_docs.mjs`. A Viewer tab showing a dropped file is saved showing its newest kept snapshot. A node's right-click "Send to Image Viewer" inside a selection of several is "Send N to Image Viewer" and sends every selected node that has media (`selectionAround`, right to left so the strip reads them left to right; `sendSelectionToViewer(ctx, nodes)`), each into the History. agentY's selection says `tab: "all"`, `tab_label: "History"`, and each item its own `tab`; `viewerState().tabs[].history` counts the snapshots made in that tab.
- **Contact Sheet of 3+ history items** (`imageViewer_contactGrid.js`, `ContactGridMixin`; the layout maths `gridLayout` is pure, tested in `tests/test_contact_grid.mjs`, the browser side in `tests/test_contact_grid_browser.py`). Compare turned on (`toggleCompare`) with 2+ thumbnails Ctrl-selected compares THEM (`_tryEnterMultiCompare`): `_multiCompare = { key, snaps }` holds the snapshots in selection order (the selection Set's insertion order), the first two are pinned as the ordinary `historyCompare` pair, so every wipe works unchanged; Contact Sheet chosen with 3+ selected adopts them too. With 3+ the Contact Sheet is a grid of its own (`.contact-grid` in the viewport, the `grid-mode` class hides `#contact-container`), capped at `CONTACT_GRID_CAP` (16). While it is up (`_gridOn`) it owns the transport like the Edit tab does: `getImgCount` / `setFrame` / `play` / `stop` hand over to `_gridFrameCount` / `_gridSetFrame` / `_gridPlay` / `_gridStop`. One clock: the longest clip plays natively and is the master; every other `<video>` follows it on the same frame INDEX, corrected each animation frame by `followRate` (2 % steps - a rate change is not free) or a seek past 0.25 s; a shorter clip holds its last frame until the sheet loops. `_gridSync()` is the one place that turns it on/off and re-lays it; it runs from `toggleCompare`, `setSliderMode`, `exitHistoryCompare` and every compare-ticker frame (which also catches the paths that drop `historyCompare` directly). A pop-out pauses moved `<video>`s, hence `_gridAfterMove` from `_afterViewportMoved`. viewerState reports it as `compare.mode = "contact"` with `items`, `shown`, `indices`, `grid`; agentY's `[VIEWER]` line is just `compare=contact N items`.
- **VFX on a clip** (LTX-2.5's IC-LoRA passes; `src/clip_vfx.py` + `clip_vfx_routes.py`, the workflows in `src/clip_vfx_workflows/` - the SAME files as agentY-core's `ltx2_5_vfx_*` templates, API format, text encoders on the CPU, no API key; `js/imageViewer_clipVfx.js` `ClipVfxMixin`, pure part `imageViewer_clipVfxData.js`; tests `tests/test_clip_vfx.py`, `tests/test_clip_vfx.mjs`, `tests/test_clip_vfx_browser.py`). A film's thumbnail menu has "✨ Convert Footage ▸" (named so by Sebastian 2026-11-11; the code keeps "vfx") (`clipVfxMenuRow`, a `.thumb-ctx-keep` row): Alpha matte, SDR to HDR, Reframe… (width x height, multiples of 64, presets), Upscale… (refine / restore, FullHD / 4K). `clipVfxDialog` -> `clipVfxRun`: POST `/imageviewer/clip_vfx/prepare` (`may_serve` source; `missing` - paint_generate's check plus `EXTRA_LOADERS` for LTX's loaders - answers 409 before anything is written) cuts the clip to its longest 8n + 1 frames (`plan`; alpha in runs of `ALPHA_CHUNK` 121) and writes each run into `<input>/clip_vfx/` through the Edit panel's renderer (`write_part` = a one-clip cut through `timeline_render`, sound kept; a clip already in the input folder at 8n + 1 frames is read as it is); the page queues the graphs with `_paintQueue` / `_paintWait` (Paint's, one `this`), progress and Stop in the dialog; POST `/imageviewer/clip_vfx/finish` joins a long alpha clip's runs (`join`, `ltx_vfx/<stem>_alpha_v###.mp4`), finds an HDR pass's EXRs (`ltx_vfx/hdr_exr/<stem>_<job>/`) and removes the parts; `_clipVfxLand` puts the film (and the EXR sequence) into the History in the source's group (`resultGroup`: else "VFX: <name>"), not switched to. Seeds stay as tested. Closing the dialog leaves the run going.
- **Dependencies** (`deps_graph.py`, `deps_provenance.py`; the tab in `js/imageViewer_depsPanel.js` / `imageViewer_depsLayout.js`; POST `/imageviewer/deps`, `same_origin`, root through `may_serve`). What a file was made from and what was made from it, read in ComfyUI - no agentY host. `deps_graph.py` is a COPY of agentY-core's `agenty_core.utils.dependencies` (same records: `provenance.deps` in the sidecar, `agent_metadata/<input>.derived.json`), `deps_provenance.py` the read side of its `provenance` (sidecar, PNG text chunks, video tags through PyAV - never ffprobe, no process) plus the prompt part of `regenerate.param_index`. `tests/test_deps_parity.py` runs both on the same files (with agentY's interpreter, skipped without agenty_core): change one, change the other. The Send node records both ways at save (`provenance_write._record_deps`); a file saved by another node is worked out from its embedded graph when first looked at (the POST writes it back). Each node's frame is coloured by still / film (`depsMediaOf`, by extension) with a caption of its models (`depsModelCaption`, both in `imageViewer_depsLayout.js`); the route adds `models` per viewable node from `deps_provenance.models_of` / `graph_models` (only the nodes that fed the file's own output; viewer-only, so outside the parity test). A file made outside a graph gets `generator` / `tags` from `deps_provenance.generator_of`: the sidecar's `generator` key (agentY's Magnific watcher), else the file's C2PA manifest (`c2pa_read.py`: JUMBF + CBOR from PNG `caBX`, JPEG APP11, MP4 / MOV `uuid`, WebP `C2PA`; read, never verified), else "magnific" in its name; the caption shows the tags (`depsCaption`). Tests `tests/test_c2pa_read.py` (`tests/c2pa_fixture.py` builds the stores). On the canvas a left drag on empty space draws a box (`_depsMarquee`, `nodesInBox` in the layout module; Shift / Ctrl adds), `rec.sel` is the selection (`.picked`, pruned by `_depsSelSync` after each render), Ctrl+click toggles, a drag moves every picked node, Esc / Ctrl+A; panning is the middle button, Alt or Space + drag. Nodes may lie at negative coordinates (`applyPlacement`'s `x0` / `y0`, used by `_depsFit`).
- `file_writer.py` / `model_writer.py` / `previz.py` / `roto_raster.py` — saving frames in VFX formats, saving meshes, previz render folders, rasterising roto shapes.
- A MESH / 3D-file input to `ImageViewerSend` is saved in the 3D format picked in `file_format` (glb, gltf, obj, ply, stl, usd/usda/usdc — `model_writer.MODEL_FORMATS`, appended after the image and video formats): `model_writer.convert` reads it with trimesh (USD through `usd_io.display_proxy`) and writes it, USD by its own pxr writer. An image or video format on a 3D input keeps the input's own format; a 3D format on an image falls back to png. FBX has no writer here.
- `nodes.py` — `ImageViewerSend`, `ImageViewerRoto`, `ImageViewerSAM3Collector`, `ImageViewerScene3D`. Node → viewer is a `imageviewer.viewer.update` websocket message; the nodes also return a `ui` dict so their files appear in ComfyUI's history and Assets panel (the frontend suppresses the inline node preview instead of the node withholding `ui`).

## Import and export: one vocabulary, one set of rules

The viewer's ways in and out grew one feature at a time and were then brought under one rule. New work follows it.

**Four words, and they do not overlap.** *Open* shows a file in the viewer. *Add* brings a file into what is being worked on (a scene, a cut). *Save* keeps the working document (a scene, a cut) where it already lives. *Export* writes a deliverable (a film, frames, a USD stage, a PNG). "Render" is the button inside an export dialog, not a fifth idea. No "Import", "Load" or "Download" in new UI.

**`export_rules.py` decides, for every export:**
- *what a format is* (`VIDEO`, `video_settings`): a container means the same codec wherever it is offered. MOV is ProRes 422 unless H.264 is asked for; MP4 is H.264; WebM is VP9. Quality is `high` / `medium` / `low`, and each encoder's number for those is there and nowhere else. `/imageviewer/export_options` serves the table to the page.
- *where a file goes and what it is called* (`target`): with a sequence and shot (a context node on the canvas, sent by the page as `context`) the bEpic layout, `<Seq>/<Shot>/<kind>/v###/<shot>_v###_<name>.<ext>`; without, `imageviewer/<kind>/<name>_v###.<ext>`. Under ComfyUI's output folder, always the next version - an export never writes over an earlier one. `/imageviewer/export_target` previews it without creating anything.

**The shared pieces** (`imageViewer_exportUI.js`, plain functions): `openExportDialog` (name, format, codec, quality, the feature's own rows, and "Goes to" - the path asked from the server before anything is written), `openFilePicker` (the file browser's listing as a question: `/imageviewer/browse` again, so the same folders and refusals; it opens where the kind is kept, `/imageviewer/export_places`, and remembers the folder per `id`), `notice` (a toast; there is no `alert()` and no `prompt()` for a path anywhere), `canvasContext` (the sequence and shot the graph names). `sequence_target` / `sequence_frame` are the rule for numbered frames: in a shot they lie in the version's folder, without one in a folder of their own name.

**Where each feature stands** - all of them follow the rule:
- *Edit panel*: Export… renders by the rule; a new cut is saved by it. Export to Resolve… lands beside the cut, as a folder, and takes the next version too.
- *Annotations*: versioned, in the shot's folder when there is one.
- *Previz*: Save scene, Export USD (dialog, versioned), Open scene, Open USD / Alembic (picker). The render lands by the rule too: frames are uploaded into `output/previz/<name>/` (the take's working folder), then encoded to the next version, or - kept as stills - moved (`/imageviewer/previz_publish`). `output/previz/<name>.latest.json` (`previz.note_render` / `latest_render`) says where the take went, which is how the scene node still finds it by `render_name`; a new take removes the note, never the earlier version. Takes and scenes from before the rule (`output/previz/<name>.mp4`, `output/3d_scenes`) are still read, and the picker offers the old folder.
- *What is on screen* (`imageViewer_exportView.js`, `export_view.py`, POST `/imageviewer/export_view`; the Export Frame or Clip command - it has a hotkey and no button): the current frame as a PNG, or the whole clip / sequence as a film - those two and no more. A picture is exported as shown (through the colourspace picked, without exposure); a video is a one-clip cut through `timeline_render`, so it keeps its sound. On a 3D scene it opens the previz render dialog, on an Edit tab the cut's export.
- *A frame dragged off the timeline* goes to `<kind folder>/frames/`, named after its clip and frame and so not versioned: the same frame twice is the same file.
- *Send to Viewer*: a `codec` widget, **last** among its inputs (saved workflows hold widget values by position - anything new goes after it), shown for `mov`; `auto` is the rule's default, so a MOV from the node is ProRes now unless H.264 is picked.

**A film a `<video>` cannot play** (ProRes; 10-bit or 4:2:2 H.264) is shown through an H.264 copy made on first request and cached in temp (`media_resolve.video_proxy`, called from `_file_response`; a key frame a second). Both copies clear each decoded frame's picture type before encoding (`_no_picture_type`): libx264 takes an I as an order, so a ProRes film (every frame an I) came out all key frames and stuttered when played. Only what is looked at goes through it: exports, cuts and extracted frames read the film itself.

**In and out of a panel are buttons at the top left of its body**, at the Outliner's size (`.previz-btn.previz-icon`), in one order: New, Add (Lucide's circle-plus, `icon-circle-plus`), then what the panel has of its own, then Save / Export (`icon-save`, a menu). Outliner: New · Add · Edit · Save/Export/Render. Edit panel: New · Add (files, canvas nodes, a new track) · Save/Export, and no second Save at the right. They were once two small glyphs in the title bar and were overlooked there; a title bar holds a name and a close button, nothing else. A panel that takes files takes the same drop - the `application/x-imageviewer-history` payload - as the Edit panel and the viewport (`previzAddDropped` for the Outliner; on an Edit tab the viewport adds clips).

**Folding and maximising a panel** (`imageViewer_mixinDock.js`; Sebastian 2026-11-10, Houdini's maximise asked for, its split tree left for later): two `.pt-btn`s left of the close in every title bar (`#<id>-fold-btn`, `#<id>-max-btn`, Lucide chevrons / maximize-2 - minimize-2, `_dockBarIcons`), a double-click on the title folds too. `collapsed: true` on the layout entry (kept by `applyDockData`; a tab group folds as one; `dockToggleCollapsed`, `isPanelCollapsed`): `.dock-collapsed` shows the title bar only, flex `0 0 auto`, no splitter beside it, the bottom rail `auto` high when all its panels are folded. `_dockMaxId` (not saved; `dockToggleMaximized`, `isPanelMaximized`): `_dockMaxSync` from `applyDockLayout` makes that panel `position: fixed` over the container from `.main-area`'s top to `.controls`' top (fixed, because `.main-area` clips), re-placed every animation frame of its own window while up, so moving or resizing the viewer or popping it out keeps it in place. Test `test_a_panel_folds_…` in `tests/test_viewer_tabs_browser.py`. (`_dockEntry` is the older `{side, entry}` finder; `_dockEntryOf` returns the entry.)

**Panels are containers; the button left of the name switches what one shows** (Sebastian 2026-11-12, Blender's editor-type button; `imageViewer_mixinDock.js`). Every title bar starts with `.pt-switch` (`#<id>-switch-btn`, Lucide panels-top-left + a caret, `_dockSwitchIcon`); it opens `_dockSwitchMenu`: the panels that can be up now, by space, from `panelSwitchGroups()` in `imageViewer_barGroups.js` (Viewer: History, Files, Parameters - "Channel Box" on a 3D tab -, agentY; then the current space's panels from the bar's GROUPS, filtered by `_barGroupCan`; the Edit timeline only on a cut's tab), the current one ticked, `{ head }` entries as headings (`.previz-menu-head`, `_previzOpenMenu`). `dockSwitchPanel(from, to)` swaps the two layout entries' slots AND their place fields (`size`, `tab`, `behind`, `collapsed`, `hidden`): the new one comes up where the old was, the old goes where the new was - hidden if that was hidden, else shown there, so nothing open is lost; a tab mate simply comes to the front. Maximised follows; the rail is widened to the newcomer's min; the Edit panels' wanted state is updated. **The look**: rails have 3 px padding and transparent 5 px stack splitters, each panel in a rail is a card (1 px outline, 7 px radius, `overflow: hidden`); the title bar is 26 px, 11 px text; `.panel-titlebar` is a size container, so a narrow panel (the History strip) drops the max button and caret below 130 px and the name and fold below 100 px. Tests `tests/test_panel_switch_browser.py`.

**Dock panels as tabs** (`imageViewer_mixinDock.js`): a panel dropped onto the MIDDLE of another (the outer thirds still mean before / after) shares its place - two more fields on a layout entry, `tab` (the group's name) and `behind` (every member but the one in front). A panel that is behind is open (`isPanelDocked`) but not drawn (`isPanelFront`); the front panel's title bar names them all, and a press on a name brings that one up (`dockTabFront`, which also fires `_onPanelShown`). Anything that toggles a panel should bring a behind one to the front rather than close it (the bar's groups do). `dockPanelTo` takes a panel out of its group; members stay next to each other in the rail's list (`_dockRegroup`).

**The lower bar** (`imageViewer_barGroups.js`): playback at the left; then the panels in three groups, each one button that opens a short menu upwards - ComfyUI (its own logo, from the frontend's `/assets/images/`): History, Parameters · 3D (Lucide `box`): Outliner, Channel Box, Curve Editor (the panel once called Animation Curves; its home is the bottom rail), on a 3D tab · Timeline: Edit, Effects, Inspector; everything else (layout, shape overlay, page dock, files, run, clear) at the right end. It is done with CSS `order` and a spacer: no button is re-parented, the old History / Parameters buttons stay in the DOM hidden and still carry the handlers and the lit state. A new panel goes into a group, not onto the bar.

**Where an export ends up on screen**: `exportLanded(paths)` puts it in the History (made in the one Viewer tab, `showInViewerTab(.., {show: false})`), without switching to it. The Edit panel's film still opens in a tab of its own, beside the cut's, and an annotation joins the History, made in the tab it was drawn on.

**Kept on purpose:** the old routes' behaviour without `rule: true` (an older cached page, agentY) - new behaviour is asked for by that flag or a new route, never by changing what an existing request does.

### Paint (a picture in layers)

A small picture editor for the user and the agent: layers, a brush, selections, masks, adjustment layers, text, crop / resize, and Generate (heal, extend, upscale, and AI fills through agentY). Files: `js/imageViewer_paintData.js` (the arithmetic, pure), `imageViewer_paintEditor.js` (the canvas, the tools and the three panels' contents; knows nothing of the viewer), `imageViewer_paintPanel.js` (`PaintPanelMixin`, the glue), `imageViewer_paintIcons.js` (Lucide shapes); server side `paint_math.py` (the same arithmetic), `paint_io.py` (`PaintDoc`, .ora read/write, .psd read/write), `paint_ops.py` (named changes, the agent's), `paint_routes.py` (the routes and sessions).

- **A tab like Motion's**: key `paint:<source path>` or `paint:new-<n>` (in `USER_TAB_PREFIXES`); the editor covers the viewport (`.paint-view`, the panel's `paint-mode` class); its other parts are the dock panels `paintLayers`, `paintProps` (the layer, its mask, Fill), `paintTool` (the tool's options and colours), `paintEffects` (style and smart filters, always there for a pixel layer), `paintGenerate` (built once, so the prompt survives redraws) and `paintHistory` (`PAINT_PANELS`, part names `layers` / `props` / `tool` / `effects` / `generate` / `history`, a `.paint-slot` each; `refreshProps` redraws props, tool and effects), up only with a Paint tab. Props, Tool, Effects and Generate ship as tabs of one place (`tab: "paint"`); a layout saved before a panel existed gets it as a tab behind its `DOCK_PANELS[id].tabWith` (`_normaliseDockLayout`). `paintTabLeave` keeps which were in front (`st.front`) and `paintTabEnter` puts them back; `host.showPanel(part)` / `paintShowPanel` take a part or an id. The bar's "Open Paint…" item (`spaceOpen("paint")`) brings up the Paint tab that is open, else the last picture opened or saved in Paint, else a new white 1920 x 1080 canvas (`paintNewBlank`) - not an empty tab: with no picture the brush's hidden pointer made its buttons hard to hit (`_cursor`: the arrow without a document). A Paint tab's key is a PATH: anything that finds its tab button goes through `CSS.escape` (closeTab once did not, and a Windows path's backslashes kept the button from closing). No time: the bar counts no frames there (`getImgCount` 0, `setFrame` a no-op). Opened from the bar's Paint group, a history item's "Edit in Paint", the file browser (kind `paint` = .ora / .psd, or a picture's menu), or `imageviewer.paint.open` (POST `/imageviewer/push` with `panel: "paint"`, or an .ora / .psd pushed). An unsaved tab is marked `.paint-dirty` and says so once on close.
- **One arithmetic, twice**: `paintData.js` and `paint_math.py` composite, blend (W3C separable formulas; "add" is Linear Dodge), adjust and filter in the same order with the same rounding (`floor(x * 255 + 0.5)` after every layer), so an export, the .ora's merged image and the agent's result are the picture the user saw. Change one, change the other; `tests/test_paint_parity.py` (through `tests/paint_parity.mjs`) compares them pixel by pixel.
- **Sessions and raw pixels**: the server holds each open document as a session (`_Book`, at most 6, random 32-hex token). Pixels travel RAW - straight RGBA, gzip (`CompressionStream`, header `X-ImageViewer-Enc: gzip`) - never as a canvas PNG, which premultiplies. `POST /imageviewer/paint/open`, `GET /imageviewer/paint/px`, `POST /imageviewer/paint/stage` (into memory only, under the token, so a host check is its fence), `POST /imageviewer/paint/save` (the editor's `toSave()`: meta, the staged layers / masks / selection, and `keep` - the layers unchanged since the last save; `write: false` only syncs), `/export` (png / jpg over white / webp / tif / psd / ora, by the export rule, kind `paint`), `/describe`, `/snapshot` (PNGs in `<input>/paint` for a brief), `/doc` (the agent's). Every POST but `stage` is `path_access.same_origin` JSON; every path goes through `is_allowed` / `may_serve`. Also served under `/api/imageviewer/paint/*`.
- **Saving**: an .ora is saved over itself; a picture, a .psd or a new canvas is saved as the next version by the export rule (`paint`), never over the source. The .ora is OpenRaster (Krita, GIMP) plus our `imageviewer.json` (adjustment layers, masks, text, the selection). The .psd is 8-bit RGB with pixel layers, masks, blend modes and the merged image; adjustment layers are BAKED on export (the answer says so); reading a .psd takes its pixel layers and names what it left out.
- **The agent** (agentY-core's `edit_image`): POST `/imageviewer/paint/doc` with `doc` (a path or token) and `ops` (`paint_ops.OPS`, all or none, layers named from 1 at the TOP, by name or "active"); without ops it answers the outline. A picture or .psd becomes a new .ora version. Then `imageviewer.paint.changed` {path, token, written, said, from} goes out; each panel listens itself (`_paintState`) and takes it in with `replaceWith` as ONE undo step (and as saved when `written`). On the picture open in Paint it works on the LIVE tab: `viewerState().paint` carries the session's `token` (and `selection_box`), and `doc` = that token with `sync: true` makes the route ask the page first (`imageviewer.paint.sync` {token, req}; the page's `paintHandOver` answers with a `save` that writes nothing, carrying `sync_req`, or `failed`; `page_sync` waits `SYNC_TIMEOUT`), then runs the ops and SAVES - over the .ora, or a new canvas / picture as the next version by the export rule (`save_new`) - and the changed event says `synced`, so the tab is marked saved and relabelled to the new file. Nothing has to be saved by hand first, and nothing of the user's is lost. Tests: `test_paint_routes.py` `test_agent_on_the_live_tab_…`, browser `test_the_agent_works_on_the_unsaved_picture_and_saves_it`.
- **Blur brush, size / opacity drag** (Sebastian 2026-11-10): `blur` is a brush (Shift+R toggles it with smudge; Photoshop keeps both under R) whose `_dab` calls `blurDab` (`imageViewer_paintDraw.js`: a premultiplied box blur, two passes of `blurReach(radius)` = radius / 4 in 1..12 px, of the pixels as they are NOW, mixed in by cover x strength, so it accumulates within a stroke; selection and locked alpha honoured; works on a layer, its mask and Quick Mask - `st.blur`, set in `_strokeStart` and `_quickStroke`). The drag (`imageViewer_paintBrushDrag.js`: `brushDragValues` / `brushDragLabel` pure, `BrushDragMixin` on the editor, tests `tests/test_paint_brushdrag.mjs` and browser `test_blur_brush_and_the_size_opacity_drag`): Photoshop's Alt+right-drag (Windows) changes size sideways and hardness (or opacity, by a preference) up and down; here sideways is the size (the circle's edge follows: diameter + 2 dx / zoom, max `DRAG_MAX_SIZE`), up / down the opacity (`DRAG_PX_PER_100` px for 0..100), Shift the hardness, Liquify the strength. Started by a LEFT press while B is held (`_bKey` sets `_bHeld`; Sebastian 2026-11-11: holding B alone did it before, now the click is needed) or Alt + right button (`_down`); `this._bd` while on - `_move` hands every move to `_bdMove` first, `drawOverlay` draws `_bdDraw` (the circle where it began, a red fill showing hardness / opacity, the values beside it) in place of the pointer's circle; ended by the button's release, any press, or the window's blur. B tapped (no press while held) picks the brush on RELEASE when a brush tool is up (so B held on the eraser sizes the eraser); with any other tool B picks the brush at once and holding it then drags the brush. `this._brushTools` is `BRUSHES` for the mixin.
- **Pen pressure**: the brush and eraser read a pen's `PointerEvent.pressure` (Wacom through Windows Ink, an Apple Pencil; `pointerType === "pen"` only - a mouse says 0.5 and is taken as 1) and every in-between position (`getCoalescedEvents`). Properties' "Pen pressure" switches per tool: Size (on; `PRESSURE_MIN_SIZE` of the size at the lightest) and Opacity (off; `stamp`'s `flow`, the stroke still keeps the most any dab gave). `pressureDabs` spaces the dabs by the radius where they lie. Tests: `test_paint_data.mjs`, and `test_paint_browser.py` draws with a real pen through CDP (`Input.dispatchMouseEvent`, `pointerType: "pen"`, `force`).
- **Tools beyond the brush**: the **clone stamp** (S; `opts.clone`, a brush with `st.clone` = `{src, sw, sh, sx, sy, dx, dy}`, `D.cloneAt`): Alt+click sets the source, the first stroke after it sets the offset, Aligned keeps that offset for later strokes, Sample takes from the layer as it was before the stroke or from the composite; not on a mask. **Transform** (V, Ctrl+T; the tool's id is still `move`): a session `this.tf = {L, src, p, before}` over the active pixel layer, `p` = `D.tfIdentity` / `tfMatrix` (t + R·Shear·Scale·(u - c)); handles scale (Shift keeps the shape, Alt about the middle), outside rotates (Shift: 15°), Ctrl+edge handle shears; nearest pixel while dragging, `tfPixels` bilinear (premultiplied) when let go; a whole-pixel move keeps the layer's own buffer; the mask is transformed with it (`tfMask`). Enter / double-click apply (ONE undo entry, "Move" or "Transform"), Esc / Ctrl+Z cancel; anything else that changes the document applies it first (`_tfSettle` in `change`, `setSelection`, `liveBegin`, strokes, `toSave`). **Transform selection** (`tfsel`, Shift+V; `TOOL_GROUPS.transform` = move + tfsel, so it is under the Transform tool's right-click; Sebastian 2026-11-09): the same session with `tf.float` - `_floatBegin` lifts the selected pixels of the active pixel layer onto a temporary layer `<name> (floating)` above it (cut out there, `this.sel` null while it floats), `_floatCommit` lays it back with `_mergeInto` (Merge down's body) and the selection through the same matrix (`tfMask`), ONE undo step "Move selection" / "Transform selection" from the snapshot before the lift; Esc / no change restores that snapshot. Not on a smart layer. **Fill** (Shift+F5, Edit > Fill…): a section at the top of Properties (`openFill`, `host.showPanel`), a colour into the selection or the whole layer at an opacity.
- **Options bar**: the row under the menus (`optBar`) holds the active tool's controls, built by the same `_toolOptions` as Properties' tool section; a change in one rebuilds the other (`_optSync`). The transform's numbers are live in both (`_tfInputs`).
- **Groups and picking several** (asked for by Sebastian 2026-11-08): a group is a layer of `kind: "group"` (no pixels; `blend` is `pass_through` or a mode, `collapsed`, opacity, mask) and every layer may name its group in `parent`. The list stays FLAT, bottom first, in tree order: a group's layers lie right under its own entry (`fixTree` / `paint_io.fix_tree` restore it, in `cleanDoc` / `clean_meta`, after every `change` and every agent op). `composite` / `paint_io.composite` walk the tree (`childIndexes`): Pass Through lays the layers straight on (with opacity < 1 or a mask on a copy, then `mixBack` / `mix_back`, premultiplied); another mode lays them on nothing first, then that picture like a canvas-sized pixel layer. A parent not in the document counts as the document's level (a layer laid alone). `from` / `to` and `upto` count only the document's level; `_keepBelow` keeps no cache for a layer inside a group. The editor's `picked` (a Set beside `doc.active`; `setActive(id, mask, "toggle" | "range")`, `pickedLayers()`, `_panelRows()`) is what Layers' commands take: `deleteLayer`, `duplicateLayer`, `moveLayers(ids, target, above | below | inside)` (the panel's drag; the middle of a group's row is inside), `groupLayers` (Ctrl+G), `ungroup` (Ctrl+Shift+G), `mergeDown` (several: `mergeLayers`; a group: `mergeGroup`; else the layer under it IN ITS GROUP), `setPickedProps` (opacity, blend, lock, the eye), the Move tool and arrows (`_moveSet`, drag `mmove`, one live step). Folding is not an undo step. Files: .ora nested `<stack>`s (`isolation` auto = Pass Through), .psd `lsct` folders (divider 3, open 1 / closed 2, key `pass`); a group holding an adjustment layer is baked whole for Photoshop. Agent ops: `group`, `ungroup`, `merge_group`, `into` on add_layer / add_adjustment / add_text / move_layer; `outline` rows say `kind: group`, `layers_inside`, `in_group`. Tests: `tests/test_paint_groups.py`, `test_paint_parity.py` `test_groups`, `test_paint_browser.py` `test_layers_pick_several_group_drag_move_and_save`.
- **Clipping masks and filters** (Sebastian 2026-11-08): `clip: true` (not on a group) clips a layer to the nearest unclipped sibling under it; an adjustment base has none (laid as it is). `layList` / `_lay_list` gather a base and its clipped run: `layClipGroup` / `_lay_clip_group` lay the base alone (Normal, opacity 1, its mask; a group base isolated as Normal), each clipped pixel layer by `layClipped` / `clip_over` (Co = Cb + (B - Cb) t, the base's alpha kept) and a clipped adjustment as usual, then that picture with the base's opacity and blend. A hidden base hides its clipped layers. `_keepBelow` keeps no cache for a clipped layer. .psd: the record's clipping byte, written and read. Editor: Alt+click a row / Ctrl+Alt+G (`toggleClip`, `_clipBase`). Filters beyond blur / sharpen are page-only (`imageViewer_paintFilters.js`, no Python twin; tests `tests/test_paint_filters.mjs`); every filter runs on the picked pixel layers (`_filterTargets`, `applyFilter`) through `filterDialog` - a live preview put into `pix` without an undo step, put back on Cancel (the dialog's `opts.preview / onInput / onCancel`), shown at most once an animation frame with the newest values (never debounced: a wait for the input to rest showed it only when the slider was let go); `dialog` puts a `.paint-dlg-range` slider beside every number field with a range (`dialogStep`).
- **Mask handling** (Sebastian 2026-11-08): `density` (0..1) and `feather` (px) on a mask are settings, not pixels: read where the mask is read (`maskAsItActs` / `paint_io.mask_values`: feather first, then `1 - density * (1 - m)`), the editor caching the feathered mask by `bufVer` (`_maskOf`); the .psd bakes both; agent op `mask` `from: "settings"`. Quick Mask (Q, `toggleQuickMask`): `this.quick` paints the selection itself (`_quickStroke`, the tint canvas `_tintDraw`), Q again ends it. `setViewMask(id, mode)` (Alt+click the mask thumb, `\` = `toggleViewMask`, Properties' "Show on the canvas", the Layers menu; Esc or `\` again ends it) shows a mask on the canvas and makes it the part painted (`setActive(id, true)`), `viewMaskMode` "alone" (grey) or "overlay" (red where it hides, Shift+`\`), with `.paint-maskbar` over the canvas (`_maskBarDraw`); `_viewMaskFollow` (from `setActive` and `_afterHistory`) keeps it on the active layer's mask while a mask is picked and ends it when pixels are (Sebastian 2026-11-10). `\` is AltGr+ß on a German keyboard, so the key is matched on `e.key` before the Ctrl branch, and on `e.code` Backslash without modifiers. **A layer dropped on a layer** (`layerToMask(src, target, {keep})`, the row's middle 30-70 % while ONE pixel layer is dragged onto a non-group row, `.drop-mask`; Alt keeps the layer): the mask is the layer's luminance x its alpha (its own mask counted, opacity and blend not), doc-sized, fill 0, replacing a mask there; one step "Layer to mask" / "Layer to mask (replaced)". Test `test_a_mask_on_the_canvas_…`; Shift+click toggles it; Ctrl+click a thumb `loadSelection(L, fromMask, replace|add|subtract)`. While the mask is picked (`_maskUp`) Fill, Delete (`clearSelection`, "Clear (mask)") and the bucket go to the mask (`_fillMaskArea`). Tests: `test_paint_parity.py` `test_mask_density_and_feather`, `test_paint_groups.py`, `test_paint_browser.py` `test_quick_mask_and_mask_handling`.
- **Pen paths** (Sebastian 2026-11-08): `doc.paths` = `[{id, name, closed, pts: [{x, y, ix, iy, ox, oy, smooth}]}]` (handles absolute; a cubic per segment), only present when there are some; part of the doc's JSON, so `change` / `liveBegin` undo them and the .ora's `imageviewer.json` keeps them (not the .psd: a note). Arithmetic twice: `cleanPath(s)` / `flattenPath` (about one piece per 3 px of the control polygon) / `pathMask` (polygonMask, even-odd) / `strokeMask` (distance to the polyline, width / 2 - d + 0.5) / `movePaths` in paintData.js and `clean_path(s)` / `flatten_path` / `path_mask` / `stroke_mask` / `move_paths` in paint_math.py (`test_paint_parity.py` `test_pen_paths`). The tool is `PenMixin` (`imageViewer_paintPen.js`, onto the editor's prototype: `_penDown` / `_penMove` / `_penUp` (drag kind `pen`), `_penKey`, `_penDraw`, `_penOptions`; `pathId`, `pathPt`, `_penOpen`), `pathToSelection`, `paintPath(fill|stroke)`, `maskFromPath`. Crop / canvas size / image size move them (`_reframe`, `imageSize`, `_shift_all`, `_resize_doc`). Agent op `path` (`points` [[x, y] | [x, y, ix, iy, ox, oy]] or `path` by name; `do` keep / select / fill / stroke / mask / remove); `outline` lists `paths`. Tests: `tests/test_paint_pen.mjs`, `tests/test_paint_paths.py`, `test_paint_browser.py` `test_pen_paths_select_fill_stroke_and_save`. **The path tools beside it** (`imageViewer_paintPathTools.js`, `PathToolsMixin`): `PATH_TOOLS` = pen, `penadd`, `pendel`, `penconv`, `pathsel` (A), `pathdirect` (Shift+A); `TOOL_GROUPS` gives them ONE toolbar place (`_groupSlot`: shows the tool used last, `_groupShow`; right-click opens `.paint-flyout`, a row of icons, `_toolFlyout`; `toolBtns[t]` is the slot for each); drags `pathmove` (whole path or the picked anchors, `moveAnchors`) and `pathbox` (`anchorsInBox`); the picked anchors are `_pathPts` = {id, set} (`_pathPicked`); arrows `nudgePath`, Del `deletePickedAnchors`; Shift+P cycles the pen's four. Tests `tests/test_paint_pathtools.mjs`, browser `test_path_tools_flyout_path_and_direct_selection_add_delete_convert`.
- **Distort / Perspective / Warp** (`imageViewer_paintWarp.js`: the arithmetic - `homography` (8x8 elimination, canvas quad -> the layer's rectangle), `convexQuad` (anything else is refused), `perspectiveLayer`, `perspectiveDrag`, `warpGrid` / `warpAt` (Catmull-Rom through every grid point, the edge extended in a straight line) / `warpLattice` / `warpLayer` (S sub-quads a cell, two triangles each, barycentric back to the source), premultiplied bilinear; tested in `tests/test_paint_warp.mjs` - and `WarpMixin`: a session `this.wp = {L, src, mode, n, pts, start, before}` like the free transform's, `wpBegin` / `_wpDown` / `_wpMove` (drag kind `wp`, nearest pixel and a coarser lattice while dragging) / `wpCommit` (one undo step, `Distort` / `Perspective` / `Warp`) / `wpCancel`; `_tfSettle` applies it too, Ctrl+Z cancels it). The mask is cut to the layer's rectangle and goes through the same mapping. Agent op `distort` (`corners`, four, convex; `paint_ops._homography` / `_sample` with numpy, the same rounding - `test_paint_parity.py` `test_distort`). Warp is the page's only. Browser test `test_distort_perspective_and_warp`.
- **Healing** (`imageViewer_paintHeal.js`; arithmetic tested in `tests/test_paint_heal.mjs`): seamless cloning after Pérez et al. 2003 as source + membrane - `membrane` (harmonic fill, SOR Gauss-Seidel, coarse to fine above 32 x 32), `seamlessBox` (premultiplied, the edge's difference carried in), `blendHealed` (by the stroke's cover x opacity), `findSource` (the ring around the region against rings 1.15..2.75 sizes away in 24 directions, never overlapping), `spotHeal` (a membrane-only fill when nothing fits), `patchArea` (source / destination). Tools `spot` (J), `heal`, `patch` (Shift+J cycles); spot / heal are BRUSHES whose stroke is the ordinary one (`st.heal`, `st.healOpacity`; the spot shows a dark wash, the heal its source like the clone stamp) and `_healFinish` replaces the stroke's pixels from `st.orig` in `_strokeEnd` (one undo step, "Spot healing brush" / "Healing brush"); `SOURCED` = clone + heal share `cloneSrc`, Alt+click, Aligned, Sample and the new source angle / scale / flip (`D.cloneMatrix`, `cloneAt`'s `m` about the anchor `ax, ay` where the offset was set) and Show overlay (`_cloneOverlay`, the canvas drawn through M^-1 inside the brush). Patch: a press inside the selection drags it (`_patchDown`, drag kind `patch`), elsewhere a lasso; `patch(dx, dy, mode)` one step. Page only. Browser test `test_spot_healing_healing_brush_patch_and_clone_overlay`.
- **Brush tips** (`imageViewer_paintAbr.js`: `readAbr` - .abr v1 / v2 sampled brushes, v6 / v10 `8BIM` `samp` sections, PackBits; `shrinkTip` (`MAX_TIP_SIDE`), `tipFromPixels` (dark paints); `TipsMixin`: tips kept in IndexedDB `imageviewer-paint-tips`, `importAbr`, `_abrFiles`, `pickAbr` = `tipsDialog` (`.paint-tipsdlg`: Choose .abr files… - its file input made in the editor's own document -, a drop zone, the built-in and the user's tips), `tipFromSelection`, `tipInvert`, `tipDelete`, `_tipOptions`, `_tipThumb`). **Built-in tips** (`imageViewer_paintTipSet.js`, `BUILTIN_TIPS`, `builtinTips`, tested in `tests/test_paint_tipset.mjs`): 14 tips drawn from seeded numbers when Paint starts (~80 ms), id `builtin:<key>`, `builtin: true`, never stored and never deleted (Invert makes a kept copy) - no brush file of anybody else's is shipped: the web's free sets were either unverifiable public-domain claims or not reachable from here. `opts.brush` / `opts.eraser` carry `tip`, `angle`, `direction` (Sebastian 2026-11-08: the tip points along the stroke - `D.directionTurn` reads the way once the pointer is max(2, radius x 0.15) px on, blended half and half with the way before; `st.way`, its `dir` added to the angle in `_dab`; the first dab waits in `st.pending` until there is a way, `_strokeEnd` lays it as it is after a plain click; a Shift-click line takes the line's way; browser test `test_brush_tip_direction_points_the_tip_along_the_stroke`), `flow` (1..100 %, brush and eraser: `st.build`; `D.stamp` then lays cover + a x build x (1 - cover) per dab instead of the most any dab gave, so one stroke builds up to its opacity; test `test_brush_flow_builds_up_within_a_stroke_up_to_the_opacity`), `scatter`, `sizeJitter`, `angleJitter`; `_tipInto` puts them on a stroke, `D.stamp(…, angle)` draws `st.tip` (its longer side = the diameter), `D.jitter` / `D.seeded` vary each dab. Page only, not in the document (a tip is the user's, not the picture's). Only checked against synthetic files (`tests/test_paint_abr.mjs`); browser test `test_brush_tips_abr_import_angle_and_tip_from_selection`.
- **Gradient, shapes, dodge / burn / sponge, smudge** (`imageViewer_paintDraw.js`: `gradientShare` / `gradientStops` / `gradientColor` (premultiplied between stops) / `gradientPixels` / `layOver`, `shapePath` (a shape is a pen path: rect, rounded, ellipse - four cubics at `KAPPA` -, polygon, line), `shapeBox`, `snap45`, `smudgeStart` / `smudgeDab` (a premultiplied buffer the brush carries, laid down by cover x strength, picking up what it put down); `DrawMixin`: tools `gradient` (Shift+G, drag kind `grad`, into the layer in the selection or into the mask - `_fillMaskArea` now takes a grey per pixel) and `shape` (U / Shift+U, drag kind `shape`, a new pixel layer `Rectangle 1` ... or `doc.paths`)). Dodge / burn / sponge are BRUSHES (`TONERS`, O / Shift+O): `st.tone`, `D.toneAt` inside `stamp` from the pixel before the stroke (so one stroke changes a pixel once), not on masks; smudge (R) is a brush whose `_dab` calls `smudgeDab` instead of `stamp`. Twins in `paint_math.py` (`gradient_share`, `clean_stops`, `gradient_color`, `gradient_stops`, `gradient_pixels`, `shape_path`; `test_paint_parity.py` `test_gradients_and_shapes`) for the agent ops `gradient` and `shape` (`tests/test_paint_draw.py`); the brushes are page only (`tests/test_paint_draw.mjs`); browser test `test_gradient_shapes_dodge_burn_sponge_and_smudge`.
- **Layer styles, locked transparency, the clipboard** (`imageViewer_paintStyle.js` - `cleanStyle`, `styleOn`, `dilate` (a disc, grown row by row: the largest within `floor(sqrt(r^2 - dy^2))` on each row dy away), `shiftMask`, `shadowOffset`, `styledPixels`, `styledCached` keyed by `BUF_VER` (the editor's `bufVer` IS that WeakMap now) - and its twin `src/paint_style.py`; `imageViewer_paintLayerFx.js` `LayerFxMixin` - the Properties section `_styleSection`, `setStyle` / `clearStyle` / `openStyle`, `toggleLockAlpha`, `_keepAlpha`, `copy` / `cut` / `pastePicture` / `pasteKey` / `pasteFromMenu`). `L.style` = `{shadow, glow, stroke, overlay}` on a pixel layer (`cleanLayer` / `clean_layer` keep it, and `lockAlpha`); `layPixelLayer` (paintData) / `lay` (paint_io) draw a styled layer as a canvas-sized picture - shadow, glow, stroke under the layer, the overlay tinting it - laid with the layer's opacity and blend; a clip group's base too, a clipped layer without its own style. A styled layer's stroke bumps its buffer every dab and redraws the whole canvas. `.psd`: `baked_for_psd` bakes the style (a note); lockAlpha is the record's flag bit 0 (read back as `lockAlpha`). Agent: `set_layer` `style` (merged; an effect null takes it off; null the whole style) and `lock_alpha`; `outline` rows `style`, `lock_alpha`. Locked transparency: strokes are `st.lockAlpha` (no layer growth; the eraser paints the second colour), smudge keeps alpha, fill / bucket / gradient go through `_keepAlpha`, Delete fills with the second colour. Ctrl+V is taken WITHOUT preventDefault so the browser's paste event comes (`_pasteWire`, window capture, armed for 1.5 s; a 300 ms fallback pastes the editor's own `_clip`); a clipboard picture of `_clip`'s size after our own copy is taken as `_clip` (exact, its place kept). Tests: `tests/test_paint_style.mjs`, `tests/test_paint_style.py`, parity `test_layer_styles`, browser `test_layer_style_lock_transparency_and_the_clipboard`.
- **Select > Modify / Colour range, guides, rulers, snapping** (`imageViewer_paintSelect.js` - `expandMask` / `contractMask` (dilate of the inverse) / `borderMask` (half out, half in) / `smoothMask` (feather, then >= 128), `colorRange` (RGB distance averaged over the channels, 1 - d / fuzziness, times alpha), `snapValue` / `snapSpan` / `rulerStep`; twins in `src/paint_select.py`, parity `test_select_modify_color_range_and_guides`; `SelectGuidesMixin`: `modifySelection`, `colorRangeDialog` (previewed), the View menu, `addGuide` / `_setGuides` / `clearGuides`, `_guideDown` (a ruler press, or the Move tool on a guide: drag kind `guide`, off the canvas deletes), `_guidesDraw` (last in `drawOverlay`), `_snapPt` / `_snapBox` / `_snapMove` (`SNAP_PX` screen px: guides, the canvas's edges and middle, other visible pixel layers' boxes) used by the marquee / ellipse, `mmove`, the transform's move, gradient, shape and guide drags). `doc.guides` = `[{axis: "x" | "y", pos}]` (half pixels, `D.cleanGuides` / `paint_math.clean_guides`, only present when there are some; `moveGuides` / `move_guides` in `_reframe`, `imageSize`, `_shift_all`, `_resize_doc`); the .ora keeps them. Keys: Ctrl+R, Ctrl+; (`e.code` Semicolon), Ctrl+Shift+;. Agent: `select` modes `expand` / `contract` / `smooth` / `border` (`by`), `color_range` (`color`, `fuzziness`, `sample` picture | layer, `combine`), op `guides` (`add`, `remove`, `clear`); `outline` lists `guides`. `_prunePicked`: an active layer that is not among the picked (made active by itself, e.g. a new layer) is then the only one picked. Tests: `tests/test_paint_select.mjs`, `tests/test_paint_select.py`, browser `test_select_modify_colour_range_guides_rulers_and_snapping`.
- **Liquify, smart filters** (Liquify: `imageViewer_paintLiquify.js` - a field of offsets the size of the layer (`liquifyField`), `liquifyDab` (push reads the field from before the dab, kept for the dab's box only; reconstruct, twirl_cw / ccw, pucker, bloat; `falloff` (1 - d²/R²)²), `liquifyInto` (bilinear, premultiplied, only the dab's box) - and `LiquifyMixin`: tool `liquify` (Shift+Ctrl+X, Filter > Liquify), a session `this.lq = {L, src, f, before}` started at the first press (the layer grown to the canvas, its buffer COPIED: `_snap` holds buffers by reference), drag kind `lq`, `lqCommit` (one undo step "Liquify") / `lqCancel` / `lqRestoreAll`, `_lqOptions`; `_tfSettle`, `setTool`, undo and Esc / Enter know it. Page only, tests `tests/test_paint_liquify.mjs`. Smart filters: `imageViewer_paintSmart.js` - `L.filters` = `[{type, on, params}]` on a pixel layer (`cleanFilters`, `MAX_SMART`; types blur, sharpen and `ADJUST_TYPES`), `smartLayer(W, H, L, px)` = the layer through them in order on a CANVAS-sized copy (so a blur reaches past its box), cached by `BUF_VER`, used by `layPixelLayer` (before the style) and the clipped layers; `SmartMixin` (`addSmartFilter`, `setSmartFilter` - patch, `{move}`, null -, `clearSmartFilters`, `bakeSmartFilters`, `smartAdjustDialog`, `_smartSection` in Properties, the "sf" tag). The module is imported by paintData and imports it: nothing of D is used while it loads (`smartTypes()` is a function). Twin `src/paint_smart.py` (`clean_filters`, `smart_layer`, used in `clean_layer`, `lay`, `_lay_clip_group`; `baked_for_psd` bakes them with a note); parity `test_smart_filters`; agent `set_layer` `filters` (a list in order, or null), `outline` rows `filters`; tests `tests/test_paint_smart.py`, browser `test_liquify_and_smart_filters`.
- **Smart layers** (Sebastian 2026-11-08: "smart layers that maintain the resolution of the original image"; `imageViewer_paintSmartLayer.js` - arithmetic + `SmartLayerMixin` - and its twin `src/paint_smartlayer.py`; parity `test_smart_layers`, tests `tests/test_paint_smartlayer.mjs` / `.py`, browser `test_smart_layer_keeps_its_resolution_…`). `L.smart = {src, sw, sh, m: [9], cut?}` on a pixel layer: `m` maps source pixels to the LAYER's own coordinates (canvas less `L.x`, `L.y`), so a plain move needs nothing; the canvas matrix is `move3(L.x, L.y)·m`. Sources live beside the document by id (`editor.srcs` Map `{px, w, h, levels}`, `PaintDoc.sources`), shared by duplicates. `render` draws a source by a projective matrix (premultiplied bilinear, from a mip level `levelFor` / `halveRGBA` while shrunk below 1/2) into `boxOf(corners)`, cut to the canvas (`cut`) past `max(DRAW_LIMIT, 4·W·H)` or `MAX_SRC_SIDE`; `_smartUncut` redraws cut ones after a change. A change whose pixels `_smartDraw` did not draw turns the layer plain (`_smartKeepOrForget`; server: `apply_ops` compares px refs against `doc._smart_drawn` and says so). Painting / filters / liquify / warp refuse it (`_smartRefuses`, `_pixelTarget(.., {smartOk})`), masks excepted; transform / distort sessions preview on the drawn pixels and commit by `_smartAfterSession(L, G, from)` (G canvas -> canvas), as do flip / rotate / scale / image size. Transport: part `src:<id>` (stage, `px?src=`), `toSave().sources` sends each once (`saved` key `s:<id>`), `_paintLoad` fetches the ones the editor lacks, `commit` keeps old ones or makes the layer plain with a note. .ora `smart/<id>.png`; .psd plain + note. Edit contents (`paintEditSmart`): a new transparent canvas of the source's size in its own tab (`smartFor`), and its save (`_paintSmartBack`) is `replaceSource` on the parent. Agent: `add_layer` from a file is smart unless `smart: false`; op `rasterize`; `outline` rows `smart`.
- **The adjustment panels** (`imageViewer_paintAdjustUI.js`, `AdjustUIMixin` + `ADJUST_CSS` in the editor's style sheet; Sebastian 2026-11-08: "like Photoshop / Affinity, with histograms"): `_adjustEditor(t, get, live, end, hist)` is the ONE editor of an adjustment - an adjustment layer's Properties section (`hist` = `_adjHistUnder(L)`: the layers under it composited, shrunk to about 0.4 MP through `_previewDoc`, kept by `version`), Filter > <adjustment> (into the pixels) (`adjustPixelsDialog(t)`: the filterDialog's live preview with that editor, Before held, Reset, OK one step "<label>"), and a smart filter that is an adjustment (`_smartSection`, the layer's own pixels; Curves allowed now). `_adjDrawHist` draws `l` grey, `rgb` as the three channels added, a channel in its colour, and the counts AFTER the adjustment (`remapHist` / `remapLum` through `D.adjustLuts`) as an orange line when they differ. Levels: handles on `.paint-ltrack` (grey handle = `gammaToShare`), the channel select edits `levels.r / g / b` (`levelsOf` / `levelsPatch`; null = no change), Auto = `autoLevels` (0.1 %; on RGB every channel). **Levels per channel is in the model**: `cleanAdjust` / `paint_math.clean_adjust` keep `r` / `g` / `b` (each null or levels), `adjustLuts` / `adjust_luts` apply the master then the channel's own (parity `ADJ` in `test_paint_parity.py`). Curves: `CURVE_PRESETS`, points dragged off the graph or Del go, arrows nudge, In / Out fields. Threshold: drag on the histogram. The plain `dialog()` with `preview` has Before (held: `onInput(v, false)`) and Reset (every field to its default). Tests `tests/test_paint_adjust_ui.mjs`, browser `test_adjustment_panels_histogram_levels_per_channel_curves_presets_and_into_the_pixels`.
- **After 1.22.0** (Sebastian 2026-11-08): `PaintEditor.d` is a GETTER (`root.ownerDocument`): popped out, the editor lives in the other window, and a `this.d` kept from the constructor drew every frame through the main window's `requestAnimationFrame`, which a browser stops for a window behind another - a drag showed nothing until let go, the filter dialogs' histograms came only when a slider moved, Import .abr's file input was clicked in the wrong window. `_adjWhenShown` draws at once, at the next frame and on a resize. `TOOL_GROUPS` also has `select`, `paint` (brush + spot, heal, patch) and `shape` - the shapes as pseudo tools `shape:<kind>` (`SHAPE_TOOLS`; `setTool("shape:ellipse")` is the shape tool with that kind; `_groupEntry`). The eyedropper dragged past 3 px is a box (drag kind `pick`, `_pickMove`, `_pickBoxDraw` with a swatch) whose `D.areaAverage` (alpha-weighted, sampled to 262144 pixels) becomes the colour. Tests: `test_popped_out_a_stroke_shows_while_it_is_dragged` (main window's rAF stubbed), `test_filter_histogram_is_there_before_anything_is_touched`, `test_selections_brushes_and_shapes_share_a_toolbar_place_each`, `test_eyedropper_dragged_takes_the_average_of_a_box`, `test_brush_tips_dialog_built_in_tips_and_importing_an_abr`.
- **Clean room**: no Adobe source or assets, and no code from other editors (GIMP, Krita - GPL); write from behaviour, papers and Adobe's published PSD spec (Sebastian 2026-11-08; the README says so).
- **Quick preview on big pictures** (`imageViewer_paintPreview.js`, `PreviewMixin`; tests `tests/test_paint_preview.mjs`, browser `test_big_picture_previews_while_dragging_and_draws_in_full_when_let_go`): a 12 MP picture took ~0.5-2 s per whole-canvas redraw, so drags showed nothing until let go. `requestRedraw(null)` during a live edit (`_liveSnap`) or a drag of kind `mmove` / `tf` / `wp` / `grad` on a picture over `PREVIEW_MIN_PIXELS` seen at < 1/2 (zoom x devicePixelRatio, `previewFactor`) goes to `requestPreview`: the document at 1/k (`_previewDoc`: every buffer shrunk by `shrinkRGBA` / `shrinkGray`, cached per buffer by identity and `bufVer`; style sizes and blur radii divided by k, masks unfeathered) composited onto `canvas.paint-preview` over the canvas; `over` puts a layer's preview-sized pixels in its place (`_previewTransform`: the free transform at the nearest pixel on the shrunk layer, `this.pix` untouched until let go; `_previewGradient` from `_gradMove`). `_up` and `liveEnd` call `_previewSettle` (one full `requestRedraw(null, true)`), a slider resting `REST_MS` too; a full `redraw()` hides it and takes over a pending full frame. Never during a stroke. `_keepBelow` keeps the last composite under a layer (`_belowKept`, keyed by `_belowSig`: the layers below as JSON plus their buffers' identity and version; dropped by `_restore` / `setDocument`), so the next stroke on that layer starts at once.
- **Solo, dropping on the buttons, Save as, the History** (Sebastian 2026-11-08): `soloLayers(ids)` (Alt+click an eye, the Layers menu) shows only those layers with their groups and what is inside them, keeping `_solo = {key, ids, was}` (every layer's visibility before the first solo); the same ids again or `endSolo` puts `was` back; each is an undo step, and a hand on an eye, undo / redo and `setDocument` forget `_solo`. A row dragged onto the foot's Duplicate / Delete (`dupBtn` / `trashBtn`, `.drop-hot`) is `dropOnButton(ids, "dup" | "del")`: picked = those, `duplicateLayer({whole: true})` (never "Layer via copy") or `deleteLayer()`. The row's `effectAllowed` is `copyMove`: with "move" alone the browser refused Duplicate's "copy" drop and nothing happened (synthetic DragEvents skip that check, so `test_real_drag_…` drags with the mouse). **Masks only**: a mask thumbnail is draggable on its own (`_maskDrag`, its dragstart stopped so the row does not drag) and goes only onto the trash (`deleteMasks(ids)`, one step "Delete mask" / "Delete N masks"); with a mask picked, Ctrl / Shift+click on rows picks more masks (`setActive` keeps `editMask`, only layers with a mask), `pickedMasks()` is that list, and Delete in the panel, the trash pressed and `maskCmd("delete")` take all of them. **Save as…** (`paintSaveAs`, a `.paint-saveas` dialog inside the editor's box: name, format ora / psd, folder + Browse… = `openFilePicker` with `folder: true`; starts in the file's folder, else `export_places("paint")[0]`) POSTs `/imageviewer/paint/save_as` (`paint_routes.save_as`: `file_name` keeps no folder in the name, `is_allowed` on the folder, which is made if missing; 409 `{exists, path}` unless `overwrite`); the tab then works on that file (`_paintRelabel`). A new .ora / .psd (Save as, a Save that wrote a new file, Export) goes to the History (`_paintLanded` -> `exportLanded`): `/imageviewer/browse_frames` answers an .ora / .psd with a cached PNG of how it looks (`paint_io.preview_png`, ComfyUI temp, keyed by path / mtime / size; an .ora's `mergedimage.png`, a .psd composited) as `path` and the file as `paint`; `_browserFramesFor` matches answers by `paint || path`, and the History's "Edit in Paint" opens `paint`. Tests `test_paint_routes.py` `test_save_as_…`, browser `test_solo_and_dragging_layers_onto_duplicate_and_delete`, `test_save_as_dialog_…`.
- **Layers and History**: a double-click on a layer's name renames it - counted in the row's click (`_rowClick`), because the first click can rebuild the list; Del / Backspace delete the layer (or its mask while that is picked) when the Layers panel was the last thing pressed (`_layersFocus`). The History panel (`refreshHistory`, from `_emit`) lists "Open", every step kept and the undone ones dimmed; a click is `goTo(i)`, undoing / redoing as many steps at once (`_histDropped` counts steps fallen off the stack).
- **Generate**: heal (no button since 2026-11-09 - Sebastian had "Fill around" removed; `paintGenerate(…, 'heal')` and the plain extend still use it), plain extend and plain upscale are done here (`_paintApplyOps`: sync, `paint_ops` on the token with `write: false`, describe, reload, `replaceWith`). The AI kinds (fill, remove, edit, background, select_subject, extend / upscale with `ai`) run a READY-MADE workflow (`src/paint_generate.py`, built-ins in `src/paint_workflows/` + `index.json`; Sebastian 2026-11-08): POST `/imageviewer/paint/generate` crops the selection box plus `CONTEXT` room, scales it to the entry's `size` (about `mp` megapixels, multiples of `mult`), writes `<input>/paint/gen_<job>.png` (alpha = 255 - selection, so LoadImage's MASK is the selection; `_mask.png` for a LoadImageMask slot), fills the slots (titles `paint:image` / `paint:mask` / `paint:prompt` / `paint:output`, else `detect_slots`' guesses), the entry's `set` / `aspect` / `scale`, new seeds, 1-4 graphs; 409 `{missing}` names models / nodes not here (`missing`: `LOADERS` against `folder_paths`, classes against `nodes.NODE_CLASS_MAPPINGS`). The page queues each (`_paintQueue`, `queueWithAuth(api, 0, {output})` - `imageViewer_comfyAuth.js`: ComfyUI's own Run puts the Comfy account token and API key on `api` only around its own `api.queuePrompt` (`extra_data.auth_token_comfy_org` / `api_key_comfy_org`), so a graph queued straight through `api.queuePrompt` ran its API nodes signed out; `comfyAuth` finds the frontend's Pinia stores on `#vue-app` by their methods (`getWorkspaceAuthToken` / `getIdToken`, `getApiKey`; localStorage `comfy_api_key` as fallback) at every queue, keeps nothing, and the node's "Send to Image Viewer" run uses it too; tests `tests/test_comfy_auth.mjs`), waits from the moment it is queued (`_paintWait`: `executed` for the output node, `execution_error`, `execution_success` -> `/history`, a 5 s `/history` poll; its third argument hears `execution_start` / `executing` / `progress` for the bar and gets a stop - `/queue` delete, then `/interrupt` with `prompt_id` only once that prompt has started, so nobody else's run is interrupted), POSTs `generate_place` (`place`: the picture covered to the box, an `add_layer` at it with `mask: "selection"`; `mask` from file; `select` from file; `resize` + layer) and applies all variations' ops as ONE undo step. The choice per action: `choices.json`, the user's own `<action>.json` in `~/.comfyui_image_viewer/paint_workflows/` (`IMAGE_VIEWER_PAINT_WORKFLOWS`), GET / POST `/imageviewer/paint/workflows`; edited in Settings (`renderPaintWorkflowEditor` in `imageViewer_settings.js`: Use the canvas = `app.graphToPrompt().output`, Load API file; a UI-format file is refused) and in Generate > Workflows… (`paintWorkflowsDialog`). The choice `agent` keeps the old way: save, snapshot and ask agentY (`_paintAsk`, `agentYViewerBridge.feedback`) with a brief that holds the exact `edit_image` call. **Seen while it runs** (`imageViewer_paintGenStatus.js`, `GenStatusMixin` + `GEN_CSS` on the editor): `genBegin(kind, what)` / `genUpdate(id, {text, share, value, max, stop})` / `genEnd(id, ok, msg)`; the pressed `[data-gen]` button flashes and is `.busy` while a run of its kind goes (re-applied when the section is rebuilt), `.paint-genbar` above the status line (state in `data-state`: run / done / failed; a moving bar when `share` is null - API nodes say nothing of how far they are; Stop). `_paintRun` drives it; heal and the plain extend / upscale get a line too. **Models** are found by file name in every listed file of the loader's folder (`locate` / `resolve_models`: `diffusion_models`+`unet`, `text_encoders`+`clip`, any subfolder of any model path, case and slash ignored) and the queued graph is pointed at the name ComfyUI lists. **Partner nodes** take their words in `model.prompt` (`PROMPT_INPUTS`) and seeds in `model.seed`; every seed is below 2^31 (`SEED_MAX`: Qwen and Seedream refuse more); an entry's `size.min` holds the short side up (Seedream 1024) and `dims` [node, w input, h input] is told the work size. Built-ins for edit: FLUX Kontext, Qwen Image Edit 2511 (local, plain / Lightning), Qwen Image 3, GPT Image 2.5, Seedream 5.0 Pro, Nano Banana 2 / Pro / 2.1; GPT Image 2 / 2.5 inpaint for fill / remove / extend. Generate › Workflows… is `ed.dialog(…, {wide: true})` (labels wrap, selects fill the row). **The built-ins** (Sebastian 2026-11-09: every image editing model for fill / remove / extend / edit, background removers incl. ComfyUI-RMBG's, Magnific / Crystal / Topaz / Recraft / SeedVR2 upscalers, SAM3 select objects; all from pre-made workflows, never the agent) are built by `scripts/build_paint_workflows.py <agentY-core checkout>` from ComfyUI's official templates (`RECIPES`: trimmed of show-only nodes, slots titled, `models` / `nodes` read off the template; do not hand-edit the generated files). `index.json` keys: `format` (`ui` = kept as the canvas saves it, else API), `mask_in` (takes a mask; otherwise an edit model: `prepare` blends the selection to `GREY` 128 in the picture, the words from `GREY_PROMPTS`, the result laid back through the selection), `tune` (node refs - id or title, `paint:tune` - whose inputs the dialog offers), `optional` (slots dropped when unused, `drop_nodes`: SAM3's `paint:prompt` / `paint:box`), `scale` [ref, input, "number"?], `prompt_input`, `downloads_itself` (RMBG's own model folders: warned once, `first_run`), `models` (name, Hugging Face url, directory). `listing` leaves out entries whose `nodes` this ComfyUI lacks (`installed`) and gives `missing` with urls. **Not on the canvas** (Sebastian 2026-11-09 took that back out; `imageViewer_paintCanvas.js`, pure parts tested in `tests/test_paint_canvas.mjs`): a `ui` built-in is turned into a prompt by `promptOffCanvas` - the workflow configured into a NEW graph of the root graph's class, `app.graphToPrompt(thatGraph).output`; ComfyUI registers a subgraph's node type only from `app.rootGraph.events`' `subgraph-created`, so the new graph's event is handed on to the root's and every type it registered is put back afterwards (else a subgraph node came out with no `class_type` - seen on ws26, checked against the real frontend 1.58.7); a node with no `class_type` is refused by name - kept per session (`_paintPrompt`, `_paintPrompts`) and sent as `graph` (`resolve_workflow` takes it); an API one runs as the server has it. The user's canvas is never touched. **Extend empty areas** (`extendEmpty` in `SelectGuidesMixin`, `emptyAround` / `EMPTY_GROW` 8 px / `EMPTY_PROMPT` in `imageViewer_paintSelect.js`): the selection = 255 - the active layer's alpha on the canvas (outside its box 255), dilated into it, one undo step, then the `fill` action with the prompt or EMPTY_PROMPT. "Select subject" is shown as **Select foreground** (the action id stays `select_subject`). **Model files** (`_paintModelsReady`): asked first (POST `/imageviewer/paint/models` = `download_plan`, sizes by HEAD), then POST `/imageviewer/paint/download` (`start_download`: a thread, `https` + `huggingface.co` only - `_allowed_url`, names through `_safe_model_name`, into `folder_paths.get_folder_paths(dir)[0]`, `.partial-<job>` then rename; `{job}` polls, `{job, cancel}` stops), progress in the gen bar. **Dialogs** (`paintGenerateDialog`, host `generateDialog`: Upscale…, Select objects…): model (+ plain resize), Times / the words + "Look inside the selection's box" (`box`), and `_paintTuneSpecs` - each `tune` node's class (the server's copy, or `uiClassOf` the template) through `/object_info/<class>` -> `tuneSettings` (no seeds, sockets, links, `NOT_TUNED`, the scale input) -> `dialogFields`; picking another model reopens it; OK keeps the values (`_paintTune`, localStorage `imageviewer.paint.tune.<id>`, sent as `tune` on every run of that workflow, `apply_tune`: plain inputs that are there only) and makes that model the action's choice. Mattes (`matte_of`): the alpha when the picture has any, else its brightness; Select objects finding nothing is an error. Tests `tests/test_paint_generate.py`, browser `test_generate_runs_its_workflow_and_the_result_is_a_new_layer`, `test_generate_says_at_once_that_it_runs_and_shows_how_far_it_is`, `test_settings_page_replaces_a_generate_workflow`, `test_upscale_dialog_runs_its_workflow_without_touching_the_canvas`, `test_extend_empty_areas_selects_around_the_layer_and_fills_it`.
- **Tests**: `tests/test_paint_data.mjs` (brush, clone, transform, bucket, selections, document rules), `tests/test_paint_parity.py`, `tests/test_paint_routes.py` (routes over real HTTP), `tests/test_paint_browser.py` (the real viewer in `tests/harness/paint.html` against the real routes, Playwright).

### Comp (a node graph of pixel operations)

A space of its own beside Motion (Sebastian 2026-11-10: two spaces that share dialogues; Comp opens on the node editor; NO ComfyUI node - a render goes to the History and onto the canvas).
