Files: `js/imageViewer_compModel.js` (the document and the pixels, pure), `imageViewer_compEditor.js` (graph, Properties, Layers and the picture; knows nothing of the viewer), `imageViewer_compPanel.js` (`CompPanelMixin`, the glue), `imageViewer_compComfyTab.js` (the graph in ComfyUI's bottom panel); server side `comp_model.py` (the numpy twin), `comp_io.py` (files, footage, writers), `comp_routes.py` (the routes).

- **The document** `*.comp.json` = `{comp: 1, format: {width, height, fps, frames}, nodes: [{id, type, name?, x, y, disable?, inputs: [ids], <params>, tracks?}], output}`. The GRAPH is what is stored; Layers is `layerStack(comp)`, a reading of it (the B pipe's Merges are layers, a Merge's A branch its picture, a Merge inside an A branch a group), and every Layers edit is a graph edit (`addLayer`, `moveLayer`, `removeLayer`). Node types `NODE_TYPES` (read, constant, roto, paint, merge - Nuke's operations -, copy, transform, crop, grade, colorcorrect, lumakeyer, chromakeyer, erode, laplacian, channelmerge, channelmath, ocio_colorspace, ocio_display, keyer, blur, motionblur, depthblur, defocus, shuffle, premult, unpremult, retime, vectors, output) are in both languages and the parity test compares the tables. Keys are Motion's (`tracks: {param: [{f, v, ease, ti?, to?}]}`, `valueAt` from `imageViewer_mgScene.js`, ported to Python), so Properties uses Motion's rows, stopwatch and diamond (`MG_CSS`, exported from `imageViewer_mgEditor.js`) and Motion's `History` for undo.
- **The heavier nodes** (`imageViewer_compOps.js`, imports nothing - compModel imports it and re-exports `blank` / `blurImage`; the numpy twins are in `comp_model.py` itself, which must stay loadable on its own): Crop (pixel area, or a ramp of `softness` at the centres), Shuffle / Copy (the numbers as they stand, premultiplied), Keyer (chroma: chromaticity c / (r+g+b) against the screen's, as a share of the screen's distance from grey; despill pulls the screen's strongest channel to the larger of the other two), Roto (the viewer's roto data in `roto` - see the Roto bullet below -, `roto_matte` = `roto_raster.rasterize(.., frames=[f])` on the server, the page's `ctx.matte` in renderNode), Vectors (Horn-Schunck on a pyramid, 2 warps a level, Jacobi; `detail` half / quarter skips the finest levels and brings the field up bilinear; out rg = forward, ba = backward, in COMP pixels), MotionBlur (a gather along the vectors, edge repeated), DepthBlur (`layers` blur sizes, each pixel's circle of confusion shared between the two nearest, numerator and weight blurred alike; `filter` lens blurs each through Defocus's disc), Defocus (`apertureRows` / `aperture_rows`: the aperture's span per row - a disc, or a polygon of `blades` with `rotation` and `aspect` -, each pixel the mean over it from running sums along the rows, a row's end pixels weighted by their cover; `bloom` multiplies rgb by 1 + bloom x (L - threshold) above the threshold first; the edge repeated). **Time**: `renderNode` memoises by node AND frame; Retime, Vectors and MotionBlur without vectors render their input at other frames, and `sourceNeeds(comp, id, frame)` lists every `{node, frame}` a picture reads (the editor loads them all first, keyed `id@frame`). `HEAVY` nodes' results are kept between renders in `ctx.cache` keyed by frame, scale and `upstreamKey` (their own and every upstream node's settings). Everything is float64 between steps and stored float32 where the other twin stores it: keep the operation order identical (no `**`, explicit products). The numpy blur works down the columns on a transposed copy (`_box_axis`): numpy sums along the last axes several times faster. **Roto is the viewer's Roto tool** (Sebastian 2026-11-10: one code for both; `imageViewer_compRoto.js`, `CompRotoMixin`): while a Roto node is on show in the Parameters panel the editor's `rotoSection` hands `o.roto.mount(n, slot)` a slot, and the panel binds the tool to a stand-in node (`_cpRoto.fake`, `_compRoto: true`) whose `roto_data` widget reads / writes the node's `roto` through `editor.setRoto` (writes within 600 ms are one undo step; a props render that finds `roto` changed - undo - reloads the tool); `_toolRef`, `_toolDraw` (sized 100 % there) and the mask canvas are seated in the editor's `stage` (`_cpRotoSeat`; the draw layer hears its own mousedown, the comp stops the viewport's), `updateToolOverlay` lays the reference over the comp canvas (`_cpRotoOverlay`, `canvasBox`), `_toolImgSize` is the comp's format, `_toolShowDock` stands down in `_cpParamsMode`, and the graph-selection rebinds (`_onGraphSelectionChanged`, `_toolFollowSelection`, `_toolRebindIfMoved`) leave a `_compRoto` node alone; `st.toolOn` keeps the stage's left-button pan off it. The preview matte is `rotoMatte` (exported from imageViewer_roto.js: the mask preview's `_rotoLayerAlpha` on an object made from RotoMixin, at W x H, `spp` = W / comp width), kept by `_cpRotoMatte`. Test `test_a_roto_shape_drawn_with_the_viewers_roto_tool_…`, `tests/test_comp_roto.py`. A colour row's ⌖ (`startPick` / `pickAt`) takes the colour of what goes INTO the node.
- **ColorCorrect** (`imageViewer_compColor.js`, imports nothing; twin `color_correct_image` in comp_model.py; Sebastian 2026-11-10: "more like Color Finesse than Nuke's ColorCorrect"): params `<range>_<name>` for master / shadows / midtones / highlights (hue, saturation %, brightness, contrast %, gain / gamma / pedestal and their `_r` `_g` `_b`, `tint_hue` / `tint` = the hue offset wheel), `contrast_centre`, `shadow_end` / `highlight_start` (`rangeWeights` on luma^(1/2.2), smoothsteps, normalised), `secA_*` / `secB_*` (an HSV hue / saturation / luma qualifier, then hue / saturation / brightness), `mix`, `view` (a range's or a secondary's matte). Order: unpremult, hue turn about grey, saturation, brightness, contrast (pivot power), hue offsets (shadows add x 0.1, the rest multiply), RGB (x gain + pedestal, ^1/gamma), secondaries, mix. A range's value combines with the master's: multiplied for %, gain and gamma, added for the rest. The specs carry `tab` / `range` / `label`: `ccSection` in the editor shows them as Color Finesse's tabs, a range bar, `ccWheel` (one undo step a drag: `st.hist.push` at the press) and `ccLumaGraph`.
- **The cache, rendering ahead, the timeline's marks** (Sebastian 2026-11-13): `renderNode`'s `ctx.cache` keeps the HEAVY nodes' results by default, every node's with `cacheAll`, only `cacheIds`' when given; the key is frame, scale, `upstreamHash` (cyrb53 over a node's settings and its inputs' hashes, once per render through `hashes`), the sources handed over (`sourceNeeds` + `tokenOf`: a WeakMap token per decoded frame object, so pixels arriving later are a new key - the old HEAVY key missed that) and which OCIO LUTs had arrived (`lutSig`); `cacheKeep` keeps the Map in use order within `cacheBytes` (`resultBytes`, `__bytes` on the Map) or `cacheMax`; `keysUsed` tells the caller each key. The editor's `heavy` Map is that cache for every node (`CACHE_BYTES` 640 MB); `noteFrame` remembers the viewed node's key per frame (`frameKeys`), `cachedFrames()` is the frames whose key is still kept for the viewed node, scale and graph as they are now. `aheadSoon` (after each draw, not while `o.playing()`) waits 400 ms, then `aheadRun` renders the next frames (at most `AHEAD` 48 and half the cache) one per timeout, caching only the viewed node (`cacheIds`), stopping at a newer draw (`renderGen`) or playback; `renderAhead()` runs it at once (tests). `o.onCache` -> the panel's `queueCacheMarks`; `renderCacheMarks` on a comp tab draws `cachedFrames()` into `#timeline-cache`. Test `test_frames_are_kept_rendered_ahead_and_marked_on_the_timeline`, `test_comp_model.mjs`.
- **The GPU preview and the region of interest** (`imageViewer_compGpu.js`, `createCompGpu(doc)` -> null without WebGL2 + EXT_color_buffer_float; Sebastian 2026-11-13): `GPU_TYPES` (constant, merge, grade, premult, unpremult, transform, reformat, crop, blur, output) are fragment shaders on RGBA32F textures in the CPU's row order (texelFetch, `gl_FragCoord` = data rows, no flips), following renderNode's arithmetic (bilinear at centres through `mat.inv`, transparent outside; the three box passes with the edge repeated; Merge's `MERGE_OPS` by index; Grade's A / B / gamma; bottom-left `dy` for other sizes; the mask stage). Reads and every other node are made by `renderNode` (its cache) and uploaded. `canRender` refuses a graph with any extra channel (`channelsAt`). `render(comp, id, f, ctx, roi)`: `ask` walks the region back up (per pixel: the same pixels, laid bottom left; blur: grown by its reach; transform / reformat: the corners through the inverse, 2 px more; other nodes: everything), each pass is scissored to its node's need; the read-back picture has `roi` when one was asked (pixels outside are 0 - never kept). Editor: `picture(id, f, scale, pics, roi, aheadOnly)` = the shared cache by `C.resultKey` (renderNode's own key, so the CPU and the GPU share `heavy`), else the GPU, else renderNode; `visibleRegion(scale)` is the part on screen when under 70 % of the picture shows; `layoutCanvas` asks again when a pan / zoom leaves `lastRoi`; the bar's GPU button / `setGpu`, localStorage `imageviewer.comp.gpu`; a GPU error turns it off for the session (`gpuWhy`, in the info line); `renderInfo()` = {how: cpu | gpu | gpu, region | kept}. Render ahead uses it too. Tests `test_the_gpu_preview_makes_the_cpus_numbers` (six graphs within 1e-4 and a region), `test_the_editor_draws_on_the_gpu_and_only_the_part_on_screen_when_zoomed_in` (SwiftShader in headless Chromium has float targets).
- **Bounding boxes** (preview only; `scanBox` / `boxOf` / `boxUnion` / `boxClip` / `boxGrow` / `withBox` in compOps): a picture may carry `bb` = [x0, y0, x1, y1], a PROMISE that every channel is 0 outside it - never set it on a buffer that is filled later. Set by Read and Roto (`scanBox`), kept by Grade, `conform` (shifted by dy), the mask stage (union), Crop (box ∩ input), `resample` (the input box's corners through the matrix, a pixel more; only those output pixels are worked), `blurImage` (the box grown by the three passes' reach + 1, blurred as a piece - exact because the piece's edge is 0 as the picture is there), `mergeImages` (over / under / plus / screen / atop with A boxed: B copied, only A's box worked). The numpy render has none; the parity `box_cases` (sparse pictures) check the numbers stay. Roughly 8x for a small blurred element over 1080p.
- **Mask and mix on every node** (Sebastian 2026-11-13; `MASKABLE` / `OWN_MIX` / `maskIndex` / `maskMix` in compModel, `MASKABLE` / `OWN_MIX` / `mask_index` / `mask_mix` in comp_model.py): the colour, filter, keyer and channel nodes get a LAST input "mask" and params `mix` (unless they had one - `OWN_MIX`: grade, colorcorrect, channelmerge, channelmath apply it themselves and the stage then uses 1), `mask_channel` (kind `chan`, `from: "mask"` - `channelSelect` lists the mask input's channels, no constants) and `mask_invert`; both tables are extended by a loop after NODE_TYPES, so the parity test compares them. After a node's case (and its `write`), `maskStage` / `mask_stage` gives in + (result - in) x mix x m, picture and every extra channel of either side (a missing one counts 0), the input and the mask conformed bottom left to the result; nothing is done without a mask and with mix 1. Merge keeps its own mask. Parameters shows mask_channel / mask_invert only with the mask connected. Export to Nuke bakes a masked node, and a node with a mix of its own that is not 1 or keyed (`_bakeable`). Tests: `mask_cases` in `tests/test_comp_parity.py`, `test_comp_model.mjs`, `test_a_masked_node_or_a_mix_is_baked`.
- **Mattes and channels** (`imageViewer_compMatte.js`, imports only compOps; twins `luma_key_image`, `chroma_key_image`, `erode_image` / `_morph_rows`, `laplacian_image`, `channel_merge_image`, `channel_math_image`): LumaKeyer (ramps up at low over low_soft, down at high over high_soft, clip, invert), ChromaKeyer (screen's dominant channel less the larger other, against the screen's own; despill to max / average of the other two; output premultiplied / matte / despilled), Erode/Dilate (min / max over a box or disc - extremes along rows per half-width shared, then down the rows; fractional sizes mix the two whole radii), Laplacian (4 x centre less 4 neighbours of the picture blurred by size, or highpass = picture less blur; x gain + offset), ChannelMerge (B, A: one channel each, `CM_OPS`, into `output` channels, mix), ChannelMath (`MATH_OPS` with a value).
- **OCIO** (`src/comp_ocio.py` over `color_io`'s config; `js/imageViewer_compOcio.js`): the working space is the config's scene-linear role (`color_io` "linear"). A transform is a spec `{src, dst}` or `{src, display, view}` (blank = working, "*" = the default display / view; `resolve`). Exact on the server (`apply_rgb`, the render's `render_node(..., ocio=comp_io.ocio_hook())`, a Read whose `colorspace` (kind `ocio_cs`) is a config name - `comp_io.Sources` converts the file's values into the working space). The page gets each one baked (GET `comp_lut`: a 48^3 LUT behind a shaper - `log2:-12:6` for a scene-linear source, `lin:0:1.25` otherwise - or `matrix` (3 x 3 + offset, exact) when the optimized processor is matrices only) and applies it with `lutColor` (twin `apply_lut`; parity cases use a synthetic LUT). `ocioNeeds` lists the nodes' specs for the editor to fetch; the bar's display menu (`st.display`, localStorage `imageviewer.comp.display`) draws the picture through one. `needLuts` keeps ONE request per spec (`lutAsk`) that every draw waits for, and a refusal for `LUT_RETRY_MS` with its reason in the info line: a second draw during a slow first bake (an ACES 1.x config reads its LUT files) once drew the plain picture and the first, stale, was thrown away - the menu seemed to do nothing. A v1 config gives no encoding, so the working space (and names with lin / ACEScg / ACES2065) count as scene-linear for the shaper. **Exposure and channels** are the VIEWER's own: `#exposure-control` itself stands in the comp's bar after the display menu (`o.barControl`, read by every `renderBar`, so a rebuilt bar keeps it; `_cpExposureSeat` from `_cpSeat` rebuilds the bar, `_cpExposureHome` puts it back in the viewport in `compTabLeave`; `.mg-bar #exposure-control` is static there; presses / wheel on it stop there), `#color-in-sel` hidden under `comp-mode`; `applyExposure` calls `compViewSync`, which hands `exposure` / `channelView` to the editor's `setViewing({ev, channel})` (`VIEW_CHANNEL` / `COMP_CHANNEL` map the names); E + drag on the stage (`o.exposureHeld` / `o.nudgeExposure`) and the R / G / B / A keys (`o.setChannel`, again: RGB) go through the viewer too. The viewer's channel menu has Alpha now (`#imageviewer-channel-alpha`). `viewEncode(img, out, {gain, channel, color})` in the model is the comp's one encoder (rgb x 2^ev, a channel as grey, then the display transform or sRGB; the alpha as it is) - only what is shown, never the comp or the render. Render `display` / `view` encode PNG / TIFF / JPEG / films through it (`comp_io.display_encoder`); EXR stays working-space linear. Node kinds `ocio_cs` / `ocio_display` / `ocio_view` are selects filled from GET `comp_ocio`.
- **Pictures have their own size** (Sebastian 2026-11-11: a Reformat used to lay its input into a box inside the one comp size, black around it): `formatAt(comp, id)` / `format_at` is a node's size in comp pixels from the graph alone - a Reformat's `reformatSize` / `reformat_size` (width x height, or the input x scale), Read / Constant / no input the comp's format, the rest their first input's (a Merge B's). `reformatLayout(n, f, inFmt, scale)` / `reformat_layout` give the matrix and the output pixels (centre on: middle on middle; off: bottom left on bottom left; no `black_outside` any more). Pixels are always round(size x scale). Pictures of other sizes meet bottom left on bottom left (Nuke's rule): `conform` / `conformPlane` in both twins lay A, the mask, ChannelMerge's and Copy's A, MotionBlur's vectors, DepthBlur's depth, a lens flare's dirt and a WRITES node's own picture onto the size they meet, and A's extra channels too. A node's numbers are in pixels of its input (`inFmt`): Transform's default centre, Crop's default box, LensFlare (drawn at its input's size). Roto draws on the comp's format and its matte is conformed. The editor shows the viewed picture at its size (`viewFmt`, `layoutCanvas`, the info line says "640×640"), draws handles in a `<g>` shifted by `downBy(inFmt(n))` (`st.hoff`, which `dragPatch` subtracts), `pickAt` and `canvasBox` (the Roto tool) likewise. The render's film is `format_at(output)` big; Export to Nuke writes ONE Reformat (its centre / box knobs), Transform centres and Crop boxes in the input's size. Tests: `test_comp_model.mjs` (reformat, bottom-left merge), parity size cases, `test_comp_nuke` `test_a_reformat_is_one_reformat_…`, `test_comp_routes` `test_a_reformat_before_the_output_…`, browser `test_a_reformat_makes_the_picture_its_new_size_…`.
- **Tracking and warps** (`imageViewer_compWarp.js`, imports nothing - the arithmetic; twin `src/comp_warp.py`, loaded by comp_model through `_warp_module`; parity `warp_cases` in `test_comp_parity.py`, node tests `tests/test_comp_warp.mjs`; the handles and panel sections `imageViewer_compWarpUI.js` (`drawHandles`, `section`, `OWN_KINDS`, `escape`, given a context by the editor's `wctx()`); browser `test_tracker_corner_pin_lens_and_grid_warp_on_the_picture`). Every warp is a backward map (U, V per output pixel, NaN = nothing) through `sampleMap` / `sample_map`. CornerPin `to1..4` / `from1..4` (1 BL, 2 BR, 3 TR, 4 TL, y down; `pinCorners`), `homography` = 8x8 elimination with partial pivoting in both languages (pure Python, not numpy, for parity), `invert`. STMap (inputs src, stmap; u / v channels, U = u·w, V = (1-v)·h; the output is the MAP's size, `formatAt`), VectorWarp (offsets in pixels x `amount`). LensDistortion: r_d = r (1 + k1 r² + k2 r⁴), r normalised by half the diagonal about `center`, undistort by 12 Newton steps, `zoom`, `output` image | stmap; `solveLens(lines)` = Nelder-Mead on the lines' straightness (page only, writes k1 / k2). GridWarp: `src_points` / `dst_points` (`gridPoints`, `lattice`), a thin-plate spline on normalised coordinates (JS its own solve, numpy `np.linalg.solve`), `distortion` mixes. Tracker: `points = {points: [{id, name, on, pattern, search, keys: [[f, x, y, score]]}]}`, `motion` translate | rotate | scale | perspective, `transform` none | stabilise | match-move, `reference`; `trackMotion` / `solveMotion` (Procrustes or a homography), `bakeTrack(comp, id, "stabilise" | "match-move" | "corner pin")` makes an unwired keyed Transform / CornerPin beside it (its picture equals the tracker's). PlanarTracker: `plane = {keys: [[f, x1, y1 .. x4, y4, share]]}` (`quadAt`). Tracking is the server's (`src/comp_track.py`: NCC by FFT + summed-area tables, a 3x3 quadric subpixel peak re-centred twice, the pattern renewed below 0.8, lost below 0.45; the plane frame to frame by features and a robust DLT, then refined against the reference frame - no drift): POST `/imageviewer/comp_track` {path, comp?, node, from, to, points | quad} -> a job, GET `?job=` (state, done, total, keys, plane), POST {job, cancel}; the page's `compTrack` polls it and `startTrack` writes the keys live as ONE undo step. Not done: a full lens model (tangential, anamorphic), tracking on the page.
- **Light** (`imageViewer_compLight.js`, imports only compOps; twins `glow_image`, `flare_image`, `flare_ghosts`, `dirt_spots`, `mulberry32` in comp_model.py): Glow (threshold / softness on luminance or max, tinted, `steps` blurs halving from `size`, averaged x intensity, added or `effect_only`); LensFlare (inputs src, dirt; `position` in comp px, null = the middle; sizes are shares of the diagonal x `size`; glow (Rg²/(Rg²+r²))², rays exp(-perp²)(1-along/L)², a halo per channel at radius x (1 + (k-1) 0.03), ghosts at light + t x 2 (centre - light) from `mulberry32(seed x 7919 + 17)`, disc or polygon (`polyEdge`), dirt = the dirt input's luminance or spots from `mulberry32(seed x 104729 + 3)`, lit by Rd²/(Rd²+r²)). The JS loops per pixel, numpy per ghost / spot in its box: the order of the sums is the same.
- **3D, deep, views** (Sebastian 2026-11-13, the Nuke gap list's part 6). **3D**: `imageViewer_comp3d.js` (pure) / `src/comp_3d.py` (numpy twin; both loaded by the model, `_sibling()` in comp_model.py so it loads with or without the package): Y up, the camera down -Z, `eulerXYZ` = Rx·Ry·Rz in degrees (the 3D space's own convention), 4x4 row major; `cameraAt(p, eye, W, H)`: k = focal / (haperture / 2), ky = k·W/H, an eye moved along the camera's x with an off-axis shift k·eye / convergence (zero parallax there). Nodes camera3d (no picture: blank), card3d (`cardMesh(aspect)`), geo3d (`shapeMesh` cube / sphere / cylinder, white without an image), project3d (the projector's picture by its camera; `faces` front = only faces turned to it), scene3d (4), pointcloud (image + its `channel`, `mode` distance / inverse, `step`, squares `size` px), render3d (scene, camera, bg; `samples` n x n per pixel, perspective-correct, near-clipped, ONE depth-tested buffer per object, then per sample the objects sorted by z (stable) and laid with over, averaged down; `depth.z` = nearest sample with alpha; feeds deep nodes as one sample per object per pixel). A geometry node viewed on its own is seen through the comp's first camera. **Deep** (`imageViewer_compDeep.js` / `src/comp_deep.py`): `{w, h, n: Uint32 counts, s: Float32 x 6 (r g b a z zback)}`, front first, stable at one depth; deepread (GET `/imageviewer/comp_deep`: `comp_io.Sources.deep` -> `comp_deep.read_deep`, the first deep part of an OpenEXR 3.5 file, A missing = 1, ZBack = Z; gzip counts then samples, `X-Comp-W/H`, at most `DEEP_MAX` samples; `deepPlace` lays the file's size bottom left like a flat Read), deepfromimage, deepmerge (4), deepholdout, deepcrop (`zfar` 0 = no limit, `outside`), deepflatten; any flat node reading a deep one gets it flattened; zback is kept, never split. `renderNode` keeps deep results in `dmemo` (numpy `("d", id, f)`), the page's in `sourcesCtx`'s `deepSource` by `"deep:" + srcKey`; `write_deep` (deepscanline ZIPS) is for tests. **Views**: `format.views` (two or more, `cleanViews`); `viewNode(n, view)` lays a node's `split[view]` over it and fills a Read's `%V` / `%v` (marking `__view`, so `srcKey` = `id@f#view`); `eyeOffset` spreads the views evenly about the middle; `ctx.view` / `view=` is the view made (default the first); oneview, joinviews (v1..v4), anaglyph (colour / grey), sidebyside (side by side / over under, double size) render other views through `inView` (a sub-context with its own memos); `sourceNeeds(comp, id, f, view)` walks per view. The render (`run_job`) makes one versioned output per view, `<name>_<view>`, `result.views`; `compLand` lands each. **Editor**: param kinds `vec3` and `view`; the bar's `.cp-views` menu (Stereo / a view / Views… / none; `st.eye`); Split / Join per view (`.cp-split`, `splitOff` / `unsplit`, `tnode` is the object a row writes); a Camera's import row (`.cp-camimport`) -> panel `_cpImportCamera`: "scene" = `imageViewer_compCamImport.js` `sceneCameras` / `sceneCameraSamples` over every open 3D tab's scene (`evaluateIn`, parents composed with pivot and offset; aimed cameras are read without their target, said in a notice), "file" = GET `/imageviewer/comp_camera` (`comp_io.parse_camera_text`, `.chan`: `frame tx ty tz rx ry rz [vfov]`, frames shifted to start at 0); `cameraKeys` makes linear keys a frame, focal from the vertical fov through the film back and the comp's shape (`focalFor`). Export to Nuke: these types are `NOT_PICTURES` in comp_nuke (written only when a native node reads them; `plan.skipped`), the render node after them is baked. Tests `tests/test_comp_3d.mjs`, parity `scene_cases`, `test_comp_routes` (deep EXR, camera file, per-view render), browser `test_3d_nodes_views_a_camera_from_a_3d_shot_and_a_deep_file`. Not done: deep EXR output, slabs split by zback, lights / shading in 3D (flat colour only), a camera's aim target. Views were built before Sebastian said stereo is not needed (2026-11-13): kept as they are, not to be extended. The GPU preview stands down for a comp with views (`picture()` in the editor); the cache keys carry the view (`resultKey`, `sourceSigOf`).
- **Extra channels** (Sebastian 2026-11-11; `imageViewer_compChannels.js`, imports nothing; twins in comp_model.py's "channels" section; parity `channel_cases` in `test_comp_parity.py`, which compares the extras too; `test_comp_model.mjs`, browser `test_extra_channels_…`): a picture's extras are `{ "layer.chan": Float32Array(w*h) }` ([H, W] float32 in numpy) kept BESIDE it - `renderNode`'s `xmemo` / `exOf`, numpy's `memo[("x", id, f)]` / `ex_of` - and handed out as `img.ch` by renderNode (`render_node(..., channels=True)` -> `(img, ex)`). A node's case may return `X(img, ex)` (numpy: a tuple); otherwise its first input's extras pass (merge / copy / channelmerge: `unionExtras`, B's first), SPATIAL nodes (transform, reformat, crop, blur) put them through the same operation four at a time (`spatial`, `mapExtras`), retime takes them from its frames (`mixExtras`). Nodes `addchannels` (`parseChannels` / `expandChannels`: a layer is its r g b a when new), `removechannels` (remove / keep; r g b a turn black), Shuffle (`out` layer, picks any channel / 0 / 1 / "-" = not written - `shuffleChannels`; old r/g/b/a picks still read). `WRITES` = the nodes with a `write` param (constant, roto, keyer, lumakeyer, chromakeyer, vectors, lensflare): "rgba" is as before, else `writeTarget(base = input 0 or blank, its extras, P, target)` - a layer gets P's r g b a, a channel P's channel of the same letter, any other name P's alpha. Roto does it itself (`rotoWrite`, with `premult`: on = the input cut by the matte, the old look; off = its colour kept, the matte into the target). Param kinds `chan` / `target` / `layer` / `chanlist` (`channelSelect` in the editor, its options from `C.channelsAt(comp, id)` - the graph alone, no pixels; "New channel or layer…" swaps in a field). The bar's `.cp-layer` menu shows a layer (`layerPicture`; one channel as grey). Not yet: extras in renders (EXR layers) and in the Nuke script (`_bakeable` refuses a Shuffle / Constant writing into them).
- **The Paint node** (Sebastian 2026-11-13; `imageViewer_compPaint.js` - pure, imports Paint's `dabAt` / `dabsAlong` - and its twin `src/comp_paint.py`, which `comp_model` loads beside itself when it is loaded on its own; parity `paint_cases` in `test_comp_parity.py`, rules in `tests/test_comp_scopes.mjs`): inputs src, reveal; `strokes` = `[{tool: color | clone | reveal | erase, pts (pixels of the input, y down), size, hard, opacity, spacing, color (linear, straight), offset (where clone / reveal read from, relative), dt (frames), life: all | single | from | to | range, f, f1, link?, off?}]`, cleaned by `cleanStroke`. A stroke's cover is the MOST any of its dabs gave a pixel (Paint's brush), times opacity; colour is premultiplied over, erase multiplies everything by 1 - k, clone / reveal mix in S = (the picture before the stroke, or the input / reveal input at f + dt) moved by -offset (`resample` of `mat.t`). `link` = a Transform node: points go through its matrix at f times the inverse at the stroke's f (`linkMatrix` / `link_matrix`; a later Tracker can answer the same). `sourceNeeds` lists the dt frames. The tool (`imageViewer_compPaintTool.js`, `createPaintTool(api)`): the section in the Paint node's first pane (`.cp-ptools` Select / Brush / Clone / Reveal / Eraser, the brush rows `.cp-pbrushrows`, the list `.cp-prow`), the brush in localStorage `imageviewer.comp.paintBrush`; the stage's pointerdown asks `paintTool.down` before the pan; a stroke is one undo step (pushed at the press, rendered live); Alt+click sets a clone source; [ ] size; Del / Esc on a selected stroke. Round tips only - Paint's sampled / .abr tips are not in the numpy twin.
- **Viewer tools** (Sebastian 2026-11-13; arithmetic `imageViewer_compScopes.js` - `compareImages`, `wipeLine`, `sampleAt`, `boxStats`, `histogram`, `waveform`, `vectorscope`, `markOutOfRange`, `guideRects`, tests `tests/test_comp_scopes.mjs`; glue `imageViewer_compViewTools.js`, `createViewTools(api)`; browser `tests/test_comp_paint_tools_browser.py`): the bar's **Viewer ▾** (`.cp-vtools`). B (`2` on a selected node, `.cp-btag` in the graph) is drawn by `draw()` with the same sources (`bNeeded` adds its `sourceNeeds`) and `compose`d with A after the layer pick and before `viewEncode`: wipe (`.cp-wipe` + `.cp-wipeh`), difference, mix. `afterEncode` reads the 8-bit picture for the scopes (`.cp-scope`) and the sampler (`.cp-sample`, Shift+drag a box) before `markOutOfRange` stripes it. Guides (format box = the comp's format at the bottom left of the picture shown, EBU safe areas, centre) are SVG in `.cp-over`. Settings in localStorage `imageviewer.comp.viewTools`; B is not kept. Display only: the render never sees them. Not done: a proxy / ROI box (the Comp-space thread's ROI work).
- **The rest of the node set** (keying, time, colour, filters, AI; 2026-11-13): the table is `EXTRA_TYPES` in `js/imageViewer_compExtra.js` (merged into `NODE_TYPES`; `extraMake` / `extraReads` / `extraLuts`, called from compModel's default case, `sourceNeeds` and `ocioNeeds`), the maths in `js/imageViewer_compFilters.js`; the numpy twin of both is `src/comp_extra.py` (`bind(comp_model)` at its end, `make` from `render_node`'s default via `_Api`). Parity: `extra_cases` in `tests/test_comp_parity.py`. Retime has a `vectors` blend (`vectorBlend`, flow at `RETIME_FLOW`). Files: `src/comp_files.py` - `.cube` read here (3D red-slowest -> the comp's blue-fastest; 1D sampled onto 33^3), other LUT formats through an OCIO FileTransform; a LUT node's spec is `{file}` (`ocioKey` includes it; GET `comp_lut?file=&comp=`, `comp_io.ocio_hook(comp_path, allow)`); ASC CDL read / written (GET / POST `comp_cdl`, 409 unless overwrite; the CDL node's Import / Export, `<comp>_<node>.cc` beside the comp). AI nodes (`AI_KINDS`): `results = {frames: {f: file relative to the comp}, keys: {f: aiKey}}`; the node shows its result through a synthesized Read (`aiRead`, id `<node>#ai<f>`, the nearest earlier frame's) or passes its input; an inpaint blends by its mask input's alpha (float plate kept outside). Run = POST `comp_ai` (`src/comp_ai.py`: renders the node's input at full size, hands it to `paint_generate.prepare(..., picture=)` with an inpaint's mask as the selection; Paint's choice per action - matte background, depth / relight edit with words, inpaint fill / remove, upscale) -> `_paintQueue` / `_paintWait` -> POST `comp_ai_place` (the answer to `<comp stem>_ai/<node>/<node>_<kind>.<f>_<run>.png`, a new name each run) -> `editor.aiResult` (one undo step); `C.aiKey` marks results out of date (Parameters only). Panel glue `_cpPickLut`, `_cpCdlImport` / `_cpCdlExport`, `_cpAiRun`. Param kinds `prompt` (a text box), `lutfile`, `ai` (not drawn as a row). Tests `tests/test_comp_files.py`. Not in the Nuke export as native nodes (baked).
- **Copies, Reads without a dialog, loose layers** (Sebastian 2026-11-13; tests `tests/test_comp_copy_layers.mjs`, `test_nodes_are_duplicated_…` / `test_a_read_comes_in_without_a_dialog_…` / `test_the_layers_show_a_read_before_it_is_wired_…` in `tests/test_comp_browser.py`, the route in `tests/test_comp_routes.py`). **Copies**: `C.copyNodes(level, ids)` -> a fragment, `C.pasteNodes(level, frag, {dx, dy, at, outer})` -> new nodes (ids new where taken, wires and expression links among the copies kept - renamed in two steps -, wires from outside only with `outer`; never an Output or a group's ports). Editor: Ctrl+D `duplicateSel`, Ctrl+C / X / V `copySel` / `cutSel` / `pasteClip` (Ctrl+Shift+V = `outer`; under the pointer while it is over the graph, `st.gmouse`), the module's one `clipboard` (so across comps and groups), Alt + drag on a node = `dragNodes(e, id, {dup: true})` (the copies are made at the first move, one undo step; Alt + drag on empty graph still pans); a backdrop takes what lies on it (`copyIds`). **Reads**: a new Read opens no dialog (it did, from `addNodeAt`); its File row has **Open** (`o.openFile` -> `_cpOpenFootage`: POST `/imageviewer/comp_open_dialog`, `src/native_dialog.py` - comdlg32 through ctypes on Windows, tkinter elsewhere, IN this process: no program is started, the no-process rule holds; `same_origin` + `path_access._is_local`, one dialog at a time (409), 501 without a desktop; it only NAMES a path, `allowed` says whether `path_access` lets it be read) and falls back to the viewer's file browser (`…`, `_cpPickFootage`) on any other answer. Desktop files dropped on the graph / Layers / picture go through `timeline_upload` (`o.uploadFiles`). The first footage dropped into a graph with no wires at all is wired into the Output. **Layers**: `layerStack` gave the BOTTOM layer no chain and no source (its id was marked seen before its branch was walked), so a Read as the background was "Background" with no footage band - fixed. `C.layerRoot(comp, viewed)` -> `{id, loose}`: what the Output reads, else the node looked at, else the end of the largest tree nothing reads; the panel reads `layerStack(comp, root.id)` then, says so (`.cp-note.cp-loose`, "Wire into the Output") and every layer edit wires it first (`adoptLoose`). `C.looseSources` = Reads in no layer, drawn as `.cp-lloose` rows with "+ layer" (`C.layerFrom`: a node that is there made the top layer).
- **Graph organisation** (Sebastian 2026-11-13; `imageViewer_compGraph.js` / `src/comp_graph.py`, `imageViewer_compExpr.js` / `src/comp_expr.py`, `src/comp_tools.py`; tests `tests/test_comp_graph_org.mjs`, `graph_cases` in `test_comp_parity.py`, `tests/test_comp_tools.py`, `test_a_group_is_written_flat_…` in `test_comp_nuke.py`, browser `test_groups_backdrops_dots_expressions_and_the_tool_library`). Node types `dot`, `group` (`nodes` = its own graph, ids its own), `group_input` (`index` = its port; `inputsOf(n)` gives a group's ports), `group_output`, `backdrop` (`w` / `h`), `sticky` (`text`), category "Graph"; any node may carry `tile` (its colour in the graph). **Everything renders FLAT**: `flattenComp` / `flatten` (at the top of `renderNode`, `formatAt`, `channelsAt`, `sourceNeeds`, `ocioNeeds`, `render_node`, `format_at`, `comp_nuke.build`) lays a group's nodes out as `<group>/<id>`, aliases Dots, ports and groups to what they pass on (`__alias` / `_alias`, `resolveId`), drops backdrops and notes, a disabled group passes input 0; `flatNode(comp, fid)` finds the tree node of a flat id. `normalizeLevel` / `_normalize_level` normalise each level (16 deep). **Expressions**: `node.expr = {param: "text" | [per component | null]}` on keyable num / int / vec2 / color params (`exprable`), evaluated in `paramAt` / `param_at` with the node's scope (`__scope`, non-enumerable in JS, a `Scope` whose deepcopy is itself in Python: that graph, then the ones around it); a loop or no number leaves the value; `round` = floor(x + 0.5), `%` = fmod, `random` / `noise` from the shared `hash32` - the two languages must agree (the parity cases). `upstreamKey` follows expression references. A link is `linkExpr` (`Node.param[.c] * s + o`), read back by `parseLink`; `ungroup` renames refs (`renameRef`). The script export bakes an expression to a curve. **Editor**: `st.scope` = the group path, `gc()` = `G.levelView` of it (every graph edit works on it unchanged), `fid(id)` the flat id, `st.view` flat; `mutate` keeps a group's `inputs` in step with its Input nodes; Ctrl+G / Ctrl+Shift+G (`groupNodes` / `ungroup`), double-click / Ctrl+Enter in, Ctrl+Backspace or `.cp-crumbs` out, B = `backdropAround` the selection (dragging its `.cp-bdhead` carries `backdropHolds`), `.` a Dot, Colour… (`tileMenu`); the Parameters row's `=` (`exprWrap` / `exprLine`, `.cp-exprline`: a field per component checked by `checkExpr`, the ⇢ link row, Bake to keys, Take off). **Tool library**: `toolFragment` (one group, inputs null, at 0,0, `tool: {name, note, created}`) saved through POST `/imageviewer/comp_tools` {op list | get | save | delete} (same_origin; `~/.comfyui_image_viewer/comp_tools/<name>.comp.json` or `IMAGE_VIEWER_COMP_TOOLS`; `clean_tool` re-checks it through `comp_model.normalize`), listed under Tools in the Tab menu (`insertTool`, × deletes).
- **Clean room** (Sebastian 2026-11-10, as for Paint): every Comp node is written from published maths and observed behaviour; no code, shaders or assets from Nuke, After Effects, Color Finesse, Keylight or GPL sources. docs/pages/comp.md says so.
- **One arithmetic, twice**: linear, premultiplied RGBA float, bilinear at pixel centres with transparent outside, Paint's three box blurs for a Gaussian, Nuke's Merge and Grade formulas. `renderNode(comp, id, frame, {scale, source})` in JS at a proxy scale, `render_node` in numpy at full size; `tests/test_comp_parity.py` (through `tests/comp_parity.mjs`) compares them float for float. Change one, change the other. A source may come SHRUNK (`ow` / `oh` = the file's size): `fitMatrix(.., k)` with k = scale x ow / w, which is what "none" needs.
- **Footage in the preview** goes through the server's own reader: GET `/imageviewer/comp_pixels` (`comp_io.Sources`, `may_serve`) answers float16 premultiplied linear RGBA, gzip, shrunk to `max` (`X-Comp-W/H/OW/OH`), so EXR, sequences (`#` runs or `%04d`) and films (PyAV, read forwards) preview as they render; the page caches decoded frames (`_cpLoadSource`, 768 MB LRU).
- **Routes** (`comp_routes.register`): GET / POST `/imageviewer/comp` (read / save: `is_allowed`, the suffix; POST `same_origin`), POST `comp_new` (by the export rule, kind `comp` -> `comps/`, `<name>_v###.comp.json`), GET `comp_source`, GET `comp_pixels`, GET `comp_formats` (`comp_io.formats` = `file_writer.formats()`: the Send to Viewer node's menu and per-format tables), POST / GET `comp_render` (a job in a thread; stills through `comp_io.write_still(img, path, ext, enc, options)` = `file_writer._write_image` - the node's writer and options `{bit_depth, compression, quality, compress_level}`; `still_pixels`: a float file premultiplied linear, others straight display values, JPEG / BMP over black; EXR without OIIO through `comp_nuke.exr_bytes`; several frames named `<stem>.<first_number + i, padded>.<ext>`, defaults 1001 / 4; one frame by `target`; a film through `comp_io.VideoWriter` (PyAV, no process) with `export_rules.video_settings`, the node's 1..100 quality as `film_quality`; file_writer imports torch only if there; cancel with `{job, cancel}`). `/imageviewer/push` opens a `*.comp.json` (`push_open.split_comps`, `imageviewer.comp.open`); the file browser opens one on double-click.
- **The tab**: `comp:<path>` (or `comp:` which only asks: New / Open), dock panels `compGraph` (bottom, up by default) and `compLayers` (a tab behind it, off); the selected node's settings take the **Parameters** panel over (`_cpParams`: `.comp-params` in `paramsPanel`, class `comp-mode` hides ComfyUI's widgets, a tool's options and the lock; the params monitor stands down on `_cpParamsMode`; the title is the node's name; `compPropsPanel` is that box) - there is no Properties panel of its own (Sebastian 2026-11-10: it doubled up); several selected nodes each get a panel of their own, as Nuke's Properties bin (Sebastian 2026-11-11, replacing the shared multi-edit of 2026-11-10): `renderProps` -> `nodePane` per node (`.cp-pane[data-pane]`, the primary first with `.first`, then the rest newest first, at most `MAX_PANES` 10; the title folds - `st.folded` -, × deselects), `paneBody` fills `pane` (ccSection / rotoSection append there); `targetsOf(n)` is `[n]` now, so `writeAll` and the mixed-value code reach one node; the Roto tool mounts on the first pane only; number labels drag to change (`scrubLabel`; Shift x10, Ctrl x0.1, one undo step), Up / Down step a field and keep its focus (`data-fk`, `st.refocus`); handles on the picture as a compositor's (`imageViewer_compJack.js`, pure, tested in `test_comp_model.mjs`; drawn by `drawJack` / `drawCropBox`, dragged through `dragPatch` - one undo step, a key when animated): the Transform's jack (the middle and the ring's inside move, Shift along an axis, Ctrl the centre alone with the translate making up for it so the picture stays - `jackDrag("centre")`; the ring scales evenly, its squares one axis, the right bar turns (Shift 15°), the top bar skews), the Crop's box (edges, corners, inside), LensFlare's light (`handlesOf`); the Transform has `skew` [kx, ky] (x by kx·y, then y by ky·x; `mat.k` / `_k`; Nuke `skewX` / `skewY` = -k, `skew_order XY`, not checked inside Nuke); the **Curve Editor** (`imageViewer_compCurves.js`, `createCompCurves`, dock id `compCurves`, part `curves`, a tab behind `compGraph`; built from `curveUI.js`; `curveLines` gives a line per node, animated param and axis; a drag is one undo step through the editor's `begin` / `end`); Houdini's gestures, as `bepicCanvasAlign.js` / `bepicCanvasCut.js` in bepic_templates do on ComfyUI's canvas: A held + drag (`st.held`, set in `keyDown`, let go on keyup / blur) is `layoutDrag` - `C.layoutTargets(items, axis)` (a row or a column, `LAYOUT_GAP` two cells, by `st.batch` - when each node joined the selection, `syncBatches` - then position), `layoutAxis`, a 180 ms ease-out, one undo step; Y held + drag is `cutDrag` (`.cp-cutline`, `C.wiresCrossed` per pointer segment, the wires lit `.cp-wire-cut`, `C.cutWires` on let go, one `mutate`); a free node dragged onto a wire goes into it (`C.wires` / `wireCurve` / `wireDistance` / `wireUnder` / `spliceNode`, the lit wire `.cp-wire-hot`); the graph's grid (`C.GRID` 15, `snapTo`, `alignNodes` in the model; `st.grid` {show, snap} in localStorage `imageviewer.comp.grid`; G / S on the graph, Shift while dragging places freely; the Align menu `alignItems`); the bar's New / Open / Save are Paint's icons (`paintIcon`), Save's menu `fileItems` (`popMenu`), Save as = `compSaveAs` -> POST `/imageviewer/comp_save_as` (409 `{exists}` unless overwrite; the old tab closes, the new file opens); the viewer's `handleKeyDown` asks `compKeyDown` first (the editor's `handleKey`: its keys while the focus is in one of its parts - the viewer's hotkeys took G before); the node menu is ONE (`tabMenu(at, {place, actions})`: Tab, Add and right-click; a field that finds a node by name, category or id, the list by category, scrolling - the graph's wheel stands down over it - and as tall as the room below it); popped out, the editor draws through its root's window (`win()`, `moved()` from `_cpAfterMove` in `_afterViewportMoved`: the window behind stops its frames and the picture stayed empty); time is the viewer's bar (`_cpFrameCount` / `_cpSetFrame`, the fps and last-frame boxes -> `compSetFormat`). Render… and Export to Nuke… are in Save's menu only (no bar buttons; Sebastian 2026-11-11); Render… is the shared `openExportDialog`; `compLand` puts the result in the History (`exportLanded`) and on the canvas (`previzNodeForRender`, the History-drag road).
- **Layers is a timeline** (Sebastian 2026-11-13, a layer editor with lengths and dope-sheet keys; clean-room): dock `compLayers`, a tab behind `compGraph` under the picture (`tabWith`; a saved layout is moved there once, `_dockCompLayersHome`, flag `imageviewer.viewer.compLayersBottom`). A layer's length is its MERGE's `in` / `out` (frames; `out` the first frame without it, Motion's rule; absent = the comp's ends; only a Merge has them, `normalizeComp` / `normalize` round them and drop an out not after in; `moveLayer` swaps them with the rest); outside, the Merge passes B (`mergeLive` / `merge_live`, parity cases in `test_comp_parity.py`); the .nk export writes `disable {{"frame<in || frame>=out"}}` on the Merge2. Model: `layerRange`, `setLayerRange`, `layerNodes` (the Merge + its A chain), `layerKeys` ({id, prop, f}), `slideLayer` (in / out, a Read's `offset` and every key of the layer's nodes together). Editor (`renderLayers` and the `lt*` functions in compEditor): `lbarEl` + `lscroll` are made once (drags capture the pointer on `lscroll`; `ltDragging` stops rebuilds the drag does not ask for; `ltLive` rebuilds from the comp at the press each move - one undo step); a row per layer (twirl, eye, swatch, kind, name, operation, mix | `.kl-bar` over a `.kl-foot` band of a Read's footage from `sourceInfo`, summary diamonds), unfolded (`st.lt.open`) a `.cp-lsub` row per keyed param of `layerNodes`; `.cp-lruler` scrubs; a press on an empty lane is a box (`.kl-box`) or, without a move, a seek. Keys picked `st.lkeys`; moves through Motion's `Mo.moveKeysMany`, deletes `Mo.removeKeysMany` (the last key of a param leaves its value). Keys (`ltKey`, while Layers has the focus): Del, `,` / `.` (Shift 10), Ctrl+A, Esc, `[` / `]` trim to the playhead (by `e.key`, so AltGr layouts work). Bar ends snap to the playhead within 6 px (Shift off). The shared parts are `imageViewer_keyLanes.js` (pure: `rulerStep`, `barEdit`, `barPart`, `pickMode` - the 3D view's modifiers -, `pickKeys`, `allPicked`, `boxesMeet`; `KL_CSS`, installed by `installCompStyles`), also used by Motion's timeline: its `.mg-bar-range` trims at the ends and slides with Alt (`barDown`, `Mo.setLayerRange` / `Mo.slideLayer`; a plain press in the middle stays the track's scrub), its key picking is `pickMode` / `pickKeys`. Tests: `test_comp_model.mjs` (the last four), `test_comp_nuke.py`, browser `test_layers_have_a_length_and_keys_on_a_timeline` (`COMP_SHOTS`), `test_viewer_browser.py` `test_a_layer_bar_trims_at_its_ends_and_slides_with_alt`. Not done: footage time remapping on a layer, parenting, a layer's own transform row (a Transform node in the A branch is that).
- **Export to Nuke…** (`src/comp_nuke.py`; Save's menu, `compNukeDialog`, POST / GET `/imageviewer/comp_export_nuke` - a job like the render's, `compJob` waits on either): `build(comp, info_of, nk_path, bake_dir, bake)` -> `(text, plan)`. `NATIVE` types become stock nodes (y flipped to Nuke's y-up: translate, centre, crop box, rotate sign; a Read gets a Reformat into the comp's format; the Output a Write to `<stem>_render/`); anything else (or a ChannelMerge Nuke cannot express, `_bakeable`) is baked - the route renders that node's own picture with `render_node` per frame into `<stem>_bake/<name>/<name>.####.exr` (`write_exr`: OIIO, else `exr_bytes`, a plain uncompressed half EXR) and the script reads it - or, with bake off, a NoOp. Keys are `{{curve x0 v0 v1 ...}}`, one per frame. Inputs are pushed in reverse (Nuke pops input 0 first; `push 0` for none). README beside the .nk (`readme`). Tests `tests/test_comp_nuke.py` (OpenEXR reads `exr_bytes` back), `test_comp_routes`, browser `test_export_to_nuke_from_the_bar`. Not checked inside Nuke.
- **ComfyUI's bottom panel**: the extension's `bottomPanelTabs` has `imageviewer-comp` (`compBottomTabs`); its `render(el)` calls `compBottomAttach(slot)`, which MOVES the graph element of the comp on screen there (`installCompStyles` puts the CSS into the main document), the Node Graph panel then says so with "Show it here" (`compBottomDetach`). Off a comp tab the strip says so and offers Open Comp….
- **Outputs and pipeline** (Sebastian 2026-11-13, part 4 of the pro-compositing list; `src/comp_write.py`, `src/comp_pipeline.py`, `src/comp_api.py`, `scripts/comp_render.py`, `js/imageViewer_compPipeline.js` `CompPipelineMixin`; tests `tests/test_comp_write.py`, `tests/test_comp_pipeline.py`, browser `test_write_nodes_render_…`). `write` and `metadata` nodes PASS PIXELS THROUGH in both twins (`PASS_THROUGH`; renderNode unchanged otherwise) - their work is server side. A Write: `file` (`####` at `padding`, `{comp}` `{name}` `{version}`; relative = beside the comp; empty = `<comp>_<name>/<name>.####.exr`), `file_type`, `channels` rgba / rgb / all (all = every extra channel as an EXR layer), `depth`, `compression` (zip / zips / none - RLE / PIZ left out, too slow in Python), `colorspace` or `display` / `view` (OCIO), `first` / `last` (null = the comp's), `handles`, `first_number` (= comp frame 0, 1001), `timecode` (start; else the first Read's), `metadata` on / off. `comp_write.plan` -> `render` (one pass per frame, a shared memo, films through `comp_io.VideoWriter`); its own EXR writer (`exr_bytes`, scanline, attributes incl. timeCode / framesPerSecond / imageviewer/*; `exr_header` reads them back). Metadata: `metadata_at` walks the B pipe (Read: `file_metadata` - input/*, exr/*, film tags - Metadata node set `key = value` / `[frame]`, remove by fnmatch). Reads: `missing` black / hold / error (`comp_io.MissingFrame`, 404 on comp_pixels), sequences report `gaps` and `timecode`, a `*.comp.json` is a PRECOMP (kind `comp`, rendered by a nested `Sources`, loops refused at depth 8 / by the stack), `local` (a localised copy, preferred when it exists; the `file` stays the network path). `comp_io.read` goes through `upgrade` (`MIGRATIONS`; a newer version is refused). Autosave per OS user (`~/.comfyui_image_viewer/comp_autosave/<sha1>.json` or `IMAGE_VIEWER_COMP_AUTOSAVE`; every 30 s while dirty, `_cpAutosaveWatch`; GET comp answers `autosave` when newer than the file -> `_cpOfferRecovery`; dropped on save). Relink: `search` by name (a sequence by its pattern) under a picked folder, `allow` = `is_allowed`. Localise: into `<input>/comp_footage/<folder>_<hash>/`, copied when size / mtime differ. Farm: `farm_request` (Deadline frames = comp frames + `frame_offset`, never below 0) relayed by POST comp_farm to the agentY host's `/agentY/comp_farm` (core's `deadline_farm.submit_comp`: the stock CommandLine plugin running `scripts/comp_render.py --frames <STARTFRAME>-<ENDFRAME> --frame-offset`), `farm_warnings` for local paths. The agent: POST comp_ops (`comp_api.apply`, all or none, `$N` = the Nth node added; the open tab is told `imageviewer.comp.open {quiet}`), GET comp_summary; core's `edit_comp` / `render_comp` / `submit_comp_to_deadline`. Not checked: a real Deadline job, the EXRs in other programs (OpenEXR reads them in the test).
- Tests: `tests/test_comp_model.mjs`, `tests/test_comp_parity.py`, `tests/test_comp_routes.py`, browser `tests/test_comp_browser.py` against `tests/harness/comp.html` (`COMP_SHOTS=<folder>` writes screenshots). Docs: `docs/pages/comp.md`.

### Worlds (branch `worlds`)

Walkable worlds built from reference images. The viewer only *shows* them; they are *made* by a separate repo,
`ComfyUI-bEpicWorlds` (sibling folder in custom_nodes; library, nodes, `/bepic_worlds/*` routes, MCP server, CLI).
The contract is the scene schema — `SCHEMA.md` there, `imageViewer_worldData.js` here.

- `imageViewer_worldData.js` — the world item kinds (environment, terrain, scatter, depthmesh), camera `reference`,
  scene `walk` / `world`, as pure data; `parseScene` reads them through it.
- `imageViewer_world3d.js` — a mixin on Model3DView (installed with `defineProperties`: it has a getter): sky/sun/fog
  replacing the studio rig, terrain, instanced scatter with wind, depth mesh, walking, reference overlay, feedback pins.
  World items rebuild when `_worldKey` (their settings as JSON) changes.
- `imageViewer_previzWorld.js` — the panel side: Add → World, channel-box rows from `WORLD_FIELDS`, Walk/Note buttons,
  notes POSTed to `/bepic_worlds/feedback` (kept in the scene only when that route is missing).
- A world arrives as a normal `imageviewer.viewer.update` with `scene_replace` (replace the tab's scene), `tab_label` and
  `focus_tab`; tab keys start `world:` and survive the stale-tab sweep.
- A world built around a whole-picture model has it as an ordinary `model` item with id `scene` (placed by the pack's
  `set_scene_model`), so the World tab is the only 3D view. An HDR sky (`sky.hdr`, `.hdr`/`.exr`) is read by the
  vendored `HDRLoader` / `EXRLoader` from `/imageviewer/view_file?…&raw=1` (`raw` skips the PNG display proxy, which would
  clip it); with `sky.lighting` it is also the world's PMREM environment, and `render.reflections: "sky"` keeps the
  cube capture from replacing it (`_worldSkyLight` / `_worldDropSkyLight`).

### Character rigs (3D space)

- Controls are scene items of kind `control` (`imageViewer_rigData.js`, pure: names -> humanoid roles, `buildRig`, two-bone IK, canonical T-pose space, key thinning; `tests/test_rig_data.mjs`). Each has `rest` (a 4x4 above its channels), `control {model, type: root|cog|fk|ik|pole, bone, chain, bind, hinge, lengths}`, and an IK control an `ikfk` scalar track. The rig group carries `rig {model, humanoid, roles}`. A skeleton whose names say nothing (an auto-rigger's) is matched by shape.
- `imageViewer_rig3d.js` (mixed into Model3DView) draws the controls and POSES the bones every frame (FK, then IK blended in); `rigPosesAt`, `rigSkeleton`, `rigExportData` read from it. `imageViewer_previzRig.js` is the Rig and Animation menus (rig, remove, key, mirror, IK/FK switch with no jump, bake poses onto the controls); `imageViewer_previzRigTools.js` the tools that reach the server.
- Server: `rig_routes.py` (`/imageviewer/rig/export|read_motion|tools|autorig|motion|job`). Export = the page collects, `rig_export.py` writes FBX 7.4 binary (bones keyed every frame, skin clusters, bind pose; metres, Y up; a Cluster's `Transform` is the mesh in the bone's space, inverse(TransformLink) . mesh - writing the mesh's own matrix there put the mesh off in Blender), USD (`write_usd`, UsdSkel through pxr: SkelRoot = the character's place, Skeleton with joint paths + bind / rest, SkelAnimation keyed per frame, meshes bound 4 weights per point; time codes = frames; joint names a prim cannot hold are made safe, the real ones in the Skeleton's customData `imageViewer:jointNames`) or Alembic Ogawa (meshes skinned in numpy per frame) by the export rule, kind `character`. The export is multipart, guarded by own host + Origin + the `X-ImageViewer-Rig: 1` header (same_origin wants JSON).
- Models run OUTSIDE ComfyUI's Python, from `rig_tools.json` in the viewer's user folder (or IMAGE_VIEWER_RIG_TOOLS / IMAGE_VIEWER_UNIRIG_PYTHON / _DIR / IMAGE_VIEWER_KIMODO_PYTHON) - never from the page. `scripts/rig/install_rig_tools.py` makes the venvs and writes it. UniRig (MIT; `scripts/rig/unirig_run.py`: skeleton, skin, merge to .glb) per job; Kimodo (Apache-2.0 code, NVIDIA Open Model License weights, Llama 3 text encoder; `scripts/rig/kimodo_run.py --serve`) as ONE kept worker, a request per stdin line, `@@{done|error}` answers. Installer: `--cuda auto` from `nvidia-smi` compute cap (>=12 -> cu128, else cu126); flash_attn from mjun0812's prebuilt wheel list (packages.md), picking the newest torch that download.pytorch.org AND data.pyg.org (torch_scatter/cluster) also have for this Python (`pick_flash`, tests `tests/test_rig_install.py`). Mac / `--cuda cpu`: no CUDA libraries - `scripts/rig/shims` (clean-room pure-PyTorch flash_attn MHA + varlen, spconv SubMConv3d/SparseConvTensor, torch_scatter.segment_csr, torch_cluster.fps; `tests/test_rig_shims.py`) via a `.pth` after site-packages (a real library wins); `unirig_run.py` writes `configs/task/_viewer_<task>_<dev>.yaml` (accelerator mps/cpu, 32-true) and retries an MPS failure on the CPU; `kimodo_run.py` `pick_device` (KIMODO_DEVICE, cuda, mps, cpu). Device env comes from the tool's `env` in rig_tools.json. Install button: Rig menu › Install AI rigging / motion models… (its first entry; and the auto-rig / AI motion dialogs when a tool is missing) posts `/imageviewer/rig/install`, which runs the installer with ComfyUI's Python as a job (progress = its `$ ` lines against `INSTALL_STEPS`, Cancel kills the process tree); the Hugging Face token is written to the HF token file, never answered back. Kimodo's text encoder (LLM2Vec = Meta-Llama-3-8B-Instruct, gated, + McGill-NLP's two LoRA adapters) is laid out by `scripts/rig/kimodo_text_encoder.py` (run in Kimodo's venv by `install_kimodo_text`, also `--only kimodo-text`) in `<root>/kimodo/text_encoders`, and `TEXT_ENCODERS_DIR` in Kimodo's `env` makes Kimodo load it from there: `--llama auto` takes Meta's repo when the login has access, else NousResearch's ungated copy (same files, same licence); the mntp adapter's `adapter_config.json` is pointed at the local base, its `config.json` is left alone because LLM2Vec only adds Llama 3's chat format when `_name_or_path` is `meta-llama/Meta-Llama-3-8B-Instruct` (it reads it from there for a local folder). The Kimodo worker restarts when its `env` changes. Ollama / GGUF cannot stand in: Kimodo needs LLM2Vec's bidirectional model with the adapters. Kimodo's `motion_correction` C++ extension needs CMake + a compiler: the installer pip-installs cmake/ninja, and if the build still fails installs with `SKIP_MOTION_CORRECTION_IN_SETUP=1`; `kimodo_run.py` then turns Kimodo's post-processing off (the viewer's `cleanFeet` still runs). The installer saves `rig_tools.json` after EACH tool (a failed Kimodo no longer forgets UniRig), adds `hf_xet`, and on Windows `triton-windows` matched to torch (`triton_windows_spec`). UniRig reads obj/fbx/dae/glb/gltf/vrm: `rig_routes.abc_to_obj` hands it an Alembic's first frame; `unirig_run.extract` stops with UniRig's own error when no raw_data.npz was written (UniRig's extract swallows its errors). Autorig / motion / install jobs write everything they print to `<viewer user folder>/rig_logs/` (last 20 kept; `log_file` in the job view, named in the error); `unirig_run` puts each failed step's last exception line after its `!!` line. run.py goes through `scripts/rig/unirig_launch.py`, which keeps PyTorch's weights-only checkpoint loading ON and only reads python-box's `box.box.Box` / `BoxList` (in the skin checkpoint's settings) as OrderedDict / list (`tests/test_unirig_launch.py`, needs torch 2.6+ and box). Never turn weights-only loading off. **SkinTokens** (VAST's successor to UniRig, MIT code and weights; Sebastian 2026-11-12) is a second auto-rig engine: rig_tools.json `skintokens` {python, repo} (IMAGE_VIEWER_SKINTOKENS_PYTHON / _DIR), ready when `experiments/.../grpo_1400.ckpt` is in the repo (`SKINTOKENS_CKPT`); `/rig/autorig` takes `engine` (unirig default | skintokens, `ENGINES`) and `keep_skeleton` (SkinTokens only: `--use-skeleton`, skin weights onto the model's own skeleton); the result says `engine`. `scripts/rig/skintokens_run.py` replaces SkinTokens' demo.py ON PURPOSE: demo.py starts bpy_server.py, which listens on port 59876 on every interface and unpickles what is posted (remote code execution) - never run demo.py or bpy_server.py; the runner uses SkinTokens' in-process `bpy` loader (num_workers 0, main thread) and `transfer_rigging` (always: keeps the given file's textures and size). SkinTokens' own `torch.load(weights_only=False)` is replaced by `safe_torch_load`: always weights-only, every global the loader refuses is registered as an inert class of the runner (`Inert`: NEWOBJ / REDUCE give a plain OrderedDict, nothing of the named class runs; a module torch blocks - os, sys - still refuses), and `settings_plain` turns the checkpoint's `hyper_parameters` back into plain dicts (OmegaConf `_content` / `_val`). Without CUDA or real flash_attn it asks transformers for sdpa; the shims have `flash_attn.flash_attn_interface.flash_attn_func` (not a top-level `flash_attn_interface`: that would make SkinTokens think FA3 is there). Installer `--only skintokens` (`install_skintokens`: Python 3.11 venv, a flash_attn wheel for torch >= 2.7 with no pyg requirement, its requirements less gradio / bottle / tornado, the two checkpoints and Qwen3-0.6B's config from Hugging Face); a run of every tool installs it only with an NVIDIA GPU of 14 GB (`skintokens_in_full_run`, `gpu_memory_gb`). Page: the Auto-rig dialog (title "Auto-rig") has an Engine select (`select.rig-engine`, `AUTORIG_ENGINES`, `pickAutorigEngine`: the last choice if set up - localStorage `imageviewer.viewer.autorigEngine` -, else SkinTokens if set up, else UniRig; one not set up opens the install for it) and, on a model with a skeleton, "Keep its skeleton" (SkinTokens only); agent `autorig` takes `engine` / `keep_skeleton` (agentY-core `animate_character` options). Tests: `test_autorig_with_skintokens` (stand-in SkinTokens + torch), `tests/test_skintokens_run.py` (the safe loader against a checkpoint whose pickle would run a command; the flash_attn_func shim against attention written out), `SkinTokensInstallTests`, `test_auto_rig_offers_skintokens_and_keeping_the_skeleton`. Not run yet with the real model (needs a 14 GB NVIDIA GPU).
- **Two menus** (Sebastian 2026-11-12): the Outliner's **Rig** (`.previz-rig`: building - add / auto-rig / remove, Set Driven Key, attributes, facial controls) and **Animation** (`.previz-anim`: keys and poses, IK / FK, Match, Space, the AI tools, blendshape keys, Add motion from file, bake, export) are ONE list of entries, `_previzToggleRigMenu(anchor, which)`, sorted by `rigMenuOf` / `RIG_MENUS` (label patterns, group by group; an entry no pattern names lands at the end of Animation; `which` null = everything, what the extras test opens). A new entry gets a pattern there. **The unused IK / FK side**: `rigData.idleControls(controls, blendOf)` = a limb's FK controls (its chain's) while its blend is 1, its IK control and pole while 0; `_rigApply` keeps it on the model's entry (`rigIdle`), `_rigSyncIdle` (also from `_rigSyncHighlight`) sets the control lines' `visible` (a selected one stays shown; `rigShowIdle` = show all), `_pick` skips hidden lines (three's raycaster ignores `visible`), the marquee did already. `_rigIkBlend(ik, frame)` is the one blend (`_rigSolve` uses it). The Character Picker dims them (`.pk-btn.idle`, from `view.rigIdleControls`), moves each IK button out to the side of its hand / foot in the front view and has an IK / FK switch per limb (`pickerLayout().limbs`, `.pk-ikfk`, text and title set every frame by `_pkRender`, a click = `previzRigSwitchIkFk`). Tests `test_animated_ik_fk_switch_and_the_unused_side` and the menu split in `tests/test_rig_browser.py`.
- Outliner: a loaded model with bones gets a folded "Skeleton (N bones)" row and the bone tree under it (`_previzSkeletonRows`; not scene items; a click selects the bone's FK control). The Rig menu falls back to the scene's only (or only rigged) character when nothing of one is selected; disabled entries are greyed and say why on hover; a failing entry shows an error toast. Dragging a control re-poses on every gizmo `objectChange`. Mirror pose reflects each bone's change from rest across model X (S·D·S onto the twin's rest), then places the twin controls.
- AI motion is humanoid only. `imageViewer_rigMotion.js` (pure; `tests/test_rig_motion.mjs`): SOMA joint per role, the character aligned bone by bone to SOMA's own T-pose (`SOMA_T`), poses to SOMA keys (in-between constraints, hips relative to the first key, heading as Kimodo measures it) and back (`somaToPose`, sized by hips height), `cleanFeet` (pin standing feet, floor clamp, leg IK). An in-between keeps its two outer keys and replaces what is between.
- **More AI animation** (`imageViewer_previzRigAI.js`, `PrevizRigAIMixin`, after the rig tools; Rig menu entries from `previzRigAIEntries`, spliced in after "Clean up feet"). **Restyle motion…** (`previzRigRestyle`): `M.restyleRequest` (pure, `tests/test_rig_video.mjs`) turns the character's motion over the range into Kimodo constraints - the hips' path on every model frame (`path` {frames, xz} -> a `root2d` constraint, `rig_routes.clean_path`), the first pose whole, and by `RESTYLE_STRENGTHS` (loose / medium / close) the hands and feet every 15 model frames (`effectors` on a key -> an `end-effector` constraint with those SOMA joints) or the whole body every 10; POST `/imageviewer/rig/motion` mode `restyle` (prompt required) and the result replaces the range (`keepEnds: false`). **Motion from video…** (`previzRigVideoMotion`): POST `/imageviewer/rig/video_pose` {src, start, end, step} runs `scripts/rig/video_pose_run.py` (Google MediaPipe Pose Landmarker, Apache-2.0 code and model, its own venv: installer `--only posetrack`, `rig_tools.json` `posetrack {python, model}`, env `IMAGE_VIEWER_POSETRACK_PYTHON` / `_MODEL`); `imageViewer_rigVideo.js` (pure, `tests/test_rig_video.mjs`) solves the 33 world landmarks ((x, -y, -z) = canonical space) into SOMA global turns (hips / chest bases from the hip and shoulder lines corrected by the T-pose's lean, head from ears + nose less `HEAD_PITCH`, limbs by shortest swing carried from the parent, unseen limbs kept from the frame before), the hips' place from the picture (torso size -> distance at a `fov`, a camera that stands still; `inPlace` off), the person scaled to SOMA's hips height; answered as a Kimodo-shaped motion at the film's fps (`resample` takes `srcFps`), optionally cleaned up by a Kimodo in-between of `filmKeys` (a key every 6 model frames + the path). **Read with SAM 3D Body** (`via` "sam3d" | "mediapipe" | "auto" = SAM 3D Body when `/rig/tools`' `sam3dbody` is ready; the dialog's "Read with" row, the agent's `video_motion` `via`, answered as `read_with`): POST `/imageviewer/rig/body_pose` with a FILM (`start`, `end`, `step`, `smooth` 0..1) -> `body_pose.film_parts` cuts the frames into H.264 parts of at most `PART_FRAMES` (240) at most `FILM_SIDE` (960) px in `<input>/rig_pose/film/` (PyAV in this process; once per film and choice, a manifest beside them; GetVideoComponents holds a part as one float tensor), a `video_graph` per part (LoadVideo -> GetVideoComponents -> SAM3DBody_Predict -> SAM3DBody_Smooth -> ImageViewerBodyPose); `_previzRigFilmSam3d` queues them one after the other and builds the tracker's track from each answer's `frames` (`body_pose.track_of`: the biggest person per frame, `world` as for a picture, `image` = MHR70's 2D keypoints as shares of the picture), then `solveFilm` as before. Tests `tests/test_body_pose.py`, `test_body_pose_hands_the_page_…`, browser `v1` in the pose test. **Lip sync from audio…** (`previzRigLipSync`): POST `/imageviewer/rig/lipsync` {src, fps, start, seconds, lead, smooth, strength} -> `src/lipsync.py` in this process (PyAV + numpy, no model: RMS over a frame, band shares of 32 ms, short dips = closed lips) answers open / round / wide / closed / hiss per frame; `imageViewer_rigLipsync.js` (`tests/test_rig_lipsync.mjs`) picks the character's set (ARKit, `viseme_*`, or one mouth-open shape; names matched without case or separators) and keys `bs:<morph>` tracks on the model item (the rig extras' blendshape channel; `_previzCanKeyMorphs` asks `S.setKeyframe` on a scratch item, so it says so when the scene model does not have them). Films and sounds come from the History selection (or the open snapshot) or the file picker (`_previzRigMediaRow`), resolved server side and `may_serve`-checked (`media_src`). **Layers**: every AI take (text, in-between, restyle, video) goes onto a new override layer when the dialog's box is on and the layers mixin is there (`_previzRigBakeInto`: `previzLayerBakeTarget` / `previzLayerAdd` + `previzWithLayer` around `previzRigBakePoses`). **Agent**: `rig_routes.AGENT_ACTIONS` adds restyle, video_motion, lipsync and layer_add / layer_list, constrain / remove_constraint / list_constraints / bake_constraints, pose_list / pose_save / pose_apply / pose_delete, flip_pose; `previzRigAgentRun` hands what it does not know to `previzRigAgentExtra`, which calls those mixins when present and otherwise answers that the feature is not in this viewer; `previzRigSceneAction` names the ones that need no character. Tests: `tests/test_rig_routes.py` (restyle constraints, the tracker with stand-in cv2 / mediapipe, lip sync on a tone), `tests/test_rig_tools_browser.py` `test_restyle_video_motion_lip_sync_and_layers`.
- **One pose from words** (`imageViewer_rigPoseSpec.js`, pure, `tests/test_rig_pose_spec.mjs`; `imageViewer_previzRigPose.js`, `PrevizRigPoseMixin` after the AI mixin; Rig menu "Pose…", agent actions `pose` / `pose_presets`; browser `test_the_agent_poses_the_character_on_one_frame`). Parts (`PARTS`: hips, spine, chest, neck, head, shoulder, upperArm, lowerArm, hand, upperLeg, lowerLeg, foot, toes; `.L` / `.R`, none = both mirrored; aliases like "left elbow", "knees") with moves in degrees, each a turn from the T-pose in its parent's frame (`partTurn`: e.g. upperArm down / forward / twist inwards, lowerArm bend, upperLeg forward / out / twist, lowerLeg bend, foot point / turn; a mirror keeps turns about X and flips Y / Z), laid over named poses (`PRESETS`: stand, sit, sit_floor, kneel, crouch, arms_crossed, hands_on_hips, wave, point, lie_back ... in order, "sit" + "arms_crossed"), solved by forward kinematics on `SOMA_T` into SOMA global rotations (`solvePoseSpec`: the character's heading kept, feet not pointed kept flat while the shin is upright, the body lowered until its lowest joint is on the floor unless `hips.height`) and keyed on ONE frame as a one-frame motion through `previzRigApplyMotion` (so any humanoid takes it; on a new layer if asked). A `prompt` with no preset / joints asks Kimodo for ~2 s from the current pose and keeps the last frame in place. Preset numbers were fitted against hand / elbow targets; check them on a real character. **A pose a body could hold** (`imageViewer_rigPoseFix.js`, pure, `tests/test_rig_pose_fix.mjs`; `fixPose` from `solvePoseSpec`): joint limits on the SOMA global rotations (`limitJoints`: elbows and knees are `HINGES` - only a bend about their axis, 0..155, plus a little roll about the bone; the rest `CONES` of degrees; the agent's `limits: false` turns them off), `alignHinges` (an upper arm / thigh rolled about itself so a limb read as directions bends about its hinge - for poses from pictures), and `contacts` (`readContact`: "right hand", `{part, under}`; `CONTACT_LIMBS` hand / foot / knee): the body stands on the first one, each limb reaches the floor by two-bone IK bending the way it bent (`under`: straight below its shoulder / hip), and a contact out of reach lowers the body. The Pose dialog has On the floor and Limits rows. **From a picture** (`solvePicturePose`, `tests/test_rig_pose_picture.mjs`): `previzRigPose(.., {image | landmarks, facing})` - the picture through POST `/imageviewer/rig/video_pose` (it takes a .png / .jpg ... too: `video_pose_run.py` runs the Landmarker in its image mode, one frame, `picture: true`), its 33 world landmarks solved by `rigVideo.solvePose`, the hips' yaw replaced by the character's heading (`facing: "picture"` keeps the person's turn), then `fixPose` with `hinges: true`, the limits and the contacts; the Pose dialog's Picture row (empty until picked, `_previzRigMediaRow` `auto: false`), the agent's `pose` `image` (a path) / `landmarks`. **Read with SAM 3D Body** (`via` "sam3d" | "mediapipe" | "auto" = SAM 3D Body when `/rig/tools`' `sam3dbody` is ready; `_previzRigPictureLandmarks`): ComfyUI's OWN core nodes (`SAM3DBody_Loader` / `SAM3DBody_Predict`, weights `sam_3d_body_dinov3_bf16.safetensors` in `models/detection`, Comfy-Org's repackage under Meta's SAM License - nothing of Meta's is shipped here, no venv, no detectron2): POST `/imageviewer/rig/body_pose` {src, fov} (`src/body_pose.py`: the picture into `<input>/rig_pose/` by its hash unless it is in input already, 409 `{missing}` without the nodes / weights) answers an API graph LoadImage -> loader -> predict (body only) -> the pack's `ImageViewerBodyPose` node (nodes.py: its `ui.imageviewer_body` = the people, biggest first, as MediaPipe's 33 landmarks, `body_pose.landmarks_of` from MHR70 - `MP_FROM_MHR70`, same camera axes, about the hips); the page queues it with Paint's `_paintQueue` / `_paintWait` (`tell.pick` reads a non-picture answer). POST `/imageviewer/rig/body_pose_download` fetches the weights (`paint_generate.start_file_download`), the dialog's "Get SAM 3D Body" button. Tests `tests/test_body_pose.py`, `test_body_pose_hands_the_page_…` in `tests/test_rig_routes.py`, p6 in the browser pose test; never run with the real model here. **Repeat poses**: the model item keeps `rigPoseBase` {frame: [base heading, posed heading]} (`poseBaseHeading` / `rememberPoseBase`), so asking for a pose again starts from where the character faced before the last pose, not from that pose's own hips turn.
- **Rigging extras** (pure: `imageViewer_rigExtras.js` + `imageViewer_rigDrivers.js`, `tests/test_rig_extras.mjs`; viewport: `imageViewer_rig3dExtras.js`, mixed into Model3DView; panel: `imageViewer_previzRigExtras.js`, `PrevizRigExtrasMixin`, its Rig menu entries put in by `_previzRigMenuExtras`; browser test `tests/test_rig_extras_browser.py`). **Extra channels** in `imageViewer_scene3d.js`: any track prop with a `:` is one more scalar (`isExtraProp`, `trackProps`, `addStaticReader` / `addStaticWriter`, `setStaticValue` - shared with the constraints thread's `cw:` / `ce:`); the rig's are `attr:<name>` (a custom attribute, `item.attrs[name] = {value, min, max}`) and `bs:<morph>` (a blendshape weight on the MODEL item, `item.blendshapes[morph]`, 0..1, the morph-target name as three.js reads it); `evaluate()` returns them in `at` too, and `space`. **Poses** (`capturePose` / `poseWrites`, keyed by what a control drives - `fk:<bone>`, `ik:<limb>`, `pole:<limb>`, `cog`, `root`, `face:<name>` - FK falling back to the humanoid role; rotations blended through quaternions) are kept per OS user by `src/rig_poses.py` (`~/.comfyui_image_viewer/poses/<name>.json` or IMAGE_VIEWER_POSES; POST `/imageviewer/rig/poses` op list / save / delete / rename, same_origin; `tests/test_rig_poses.py`); the Pose library window (`previzPoseLibrary`, `.rx-float`), `previzPoseSave` / `previzPoseApply(name, {ids, blend, mirror, key})` / `previzPoseList` / `previzPoseDelete`, Copy / Paste pose, `previzRigFlipPose` (whole body: `flipBonesPose` = S·(pose·rest⁻¹)·S·rest per bone and its twin, then `_previzRigChannelsFor`). **Spaces**: `control.spaces` = target ids, the keyable `space` channel (hold keys; 0 = its own parent); `_rigApplySpaces` sets the control's rest node to `spaceRestMatrix` = P⁻¹·T·(T₀⁻¹·P₀)·rest after the items are placed, before the bones are posed; `previzRigSetSpace(id, index | targetId)` keeps it where it is (`channelsInSpace`), keyed with the old space on the frame before when animated. `previzRigMatch(id, "fk" | "ik")` matches one side of a limb without switching. **Set-driven keys**: `item.drivers = [{prop, axis, driver: {id, prop, axis}, keys: [{x, v}], ease, rest}]` on the DRIVEN item; the curve's value is ADDED to the channel (so a driven channel can still be animated); applied in `applyFrame` through `_rigDriven` (`evaluateDriven`, chains to depth 6), live while a driver is dragged (`_rigDriveLive`), the IK blend through `_rigBlendOf`; `_previzRigChannelsFor` does not subtract a driven part yet. `previzRigSdkKey(driver, driven)` folds the change since the curve's first key into the curve and puts the channel back (`rest`), Maya's Key; the Set Driven Key window `previzSdkWindow`. **Blendshapes**: `_rigApplyMorphs` sets `morphTargetInfluences` by name for the `bs:` channels an item has (others left as the file / clip has them); the Channel Box lists a model's morphs under BLENDSHAPES and a control's attributes under ATTRIBUTES. **Facial controls** (`buildFaceRig`, `FACE_CONTROLS`, `findMorph` - ARKit names in any spelling): a board (`control.type "face"`, `face: "board"`) under the head's FK control beside the head, joysticks whose position X / Y drive blendshapes through drivers on the model (one cell of travel = weight 1). Not done: blendshapes in the FBX / Alembic export, extra channels in the Curve Editor.
- Clean-room: nothing from Rigify, Cascadeur, Mixamo or GPL code; UniRig's own pipeline uses bpy (GPL) only inside its own venv.
- **The round trip with Maya, Blender, Unreal** (`imageViewer_previzRigRoundTrip.js`, `PrevizRigRoundTripMixin`; the pure part `imageViewer_rigLive.js`; `rig_import.py`). Every application reads the BAKED motion of an FBX / USD export (`rigExportData` walks `applyFrame`, so whatever the rig evaluates - layers, constraints - is in it). The viewer's own rig rides along live: `collectRigLive` = the rig group and its descendants, the character's controls, and any other item whose JSON names one of their ids (`rigItems`; an item naming only the model - a prop constrained to the hand - is not the rig), plus parts registered with `registerRigLivePart(name, {save, load})` for state outside `scene.items`, plus a `fingerprint` (a few bones' model-space positions on a few frames of the export). The server packs it (`rig_export.pack_live`: zlib JSON in base64, `ivz1:`) into the character Null's user property `ImageViewerRig` (KString, flag U: Blender keeps it as a custom property and writes it back; Maya keeps extra attributes) or the SkelRoot's customData `imageViewer:rig`. Rig menu › **Add motion from file…** (FBX, USD, glTF): POST `/imageviewer/rig/read_motion` {path} (`may_serve`) -> `rig_import.read` (FBX: a binary record walker / an ASCII search for the property, plus fps, units, up axis; USD: the skeleton's animation sampled per time code, `skel_to_model` = the skeleton prim's place in the file less the viewer's own SkelRoot placement - Blender turns an imported Y-up armature +90 X on its Xform instead of its bones); an FBX / glTF's motion is read in the page by three.js (`_previzRigLoadedMotion`: model space = what the top bone hangs under, the bind pose as rest only where it lies where the nodes do, the clip that moves most bones; a Z-up file turned Y up). `previzRigMotionPlan`: the rig can come back (`canRestore`: same bone names) and the file is unchanged since export (`sameFingerprint`) -> "Bring the rig back as it was" (`restoreRigLive`: ids kept, new ones only where another character's would collide, the model id remapped, through `parseScene`; works on a character with no rig yet); else bake (`retarget`: bones matched by `boneKey` - no namespace, no mixamorig - then by humanoid role; each matched bone takes the source's rotation from rest in model space onto its own rest, the top one also its move scaled by height, the rest keep their lengths; then `previzRigBakePoses`). The agent: rig action `add_motion` {path, how: auto|restore|bake, from}; `export` takes `format: usd` and `live`. **Vendored FBXLoader patch** (`generateRotationTrack`): r180's `interpolateRotations` walks curve X's keys only, so a rotation keyed on one axis (Blender writes a constant axis as two keys) lost its motion; the three curves are put on the union of their times first. Not done: constraints / layers as Maya-native nodes (they arrive baked), the character node's own animation coming back (move the hips), USD customData through Blender (it drops it: a Blender-saved USD bakes). Tests `tests/test_rig_roundtrip.py` (writers vs reader, the cluster convention, UsdSkel's own skinning, and Blender itself importing both when `bpy` is importable or `IMAGE_VIEWER_TEST_BPY_PYTHON` names a Python that has it), `tests/test_rig_live.mjs`, `tests/test_rig_roundtrip_browser.py` (rig back from FBX unchanged, bake, USD onto Unreal-named bones, and `ThroughBlender`: exported here, saved again by Blender, brought back), `test_usd_export_and_the_rig_coming_back` in `tests/test_rig_routes.py`.
- Tests: `test_rig_browser.py`, `test_rig_tools_browser.py` (routes answered in-test with the real writers; FBX loaded back with FBXLoader, Alembic with abc_io), `test_rig_routes.py` (real runners against a stand-in UniRig folder and stand-in torch/kimodo modules).

### Constraints (3D space)
