- Data `imageViewer_constraintData.js` (pure, no imports): `item.constraints = [{id, type: parent|point|orient|scale|aim, name, on, weight, maintainOffset, targets: [{id, w, offset}], skip: {t, r, s}, aim?: {aimAxis, upAxis, upType: scene|vector|object|objectrotation|none, upVector, upObject}, offset? (aim)}]` on the DRIVEN item, applied in order. `readConstraints` / `pruneConstraints` are called by `parseScene` (targets that are gone, and their weight tracks, are dropped; `previzDelete` prunes too).
- **Weights are channels**: the scene3d "extra track" rule - any prop with a ":" (`isExtraProp`, `trackProps`, `addStaticReader` / `addStaticWriter`, `setStaticValue`; shared with the rig-extras branch's `attr:` / `bs:`) - `cw:<cid>:<targetId>` (a target's weight, 0..10, relative) and `ce:<cid>` (the constraint's own, 0..1, blends with what came in). Static values live in the constraint (`w`, `weight`); keyed, they are ordinary scalar tracks: Channel Box rows, Curve Editor channels (`_previzConstraintCurveChannels`), timeline ticks.
- Solver `imageViewer_constraints.js` (pure, own clean-room matrix / quaternion maths, column-major like three): the view's chain `root = parentInner · rest · T(pos+pivot) · R(Euler XYZ) · S`, `inner = root · T(-pivot) · offset`; a target is its ROOT matrix; a constraint sets the driven root, taken back into channels (Euler nearest the item's own, so no flips), skipped axes kept, then blended by its weight. `solver(scene, frame).world(id)` (memoised, a loop falls back to unconstrained), `constrainedLocals`, `captureOffsets` (maintain offset, against the constraints before it), `cannotConstrain` / `dependsOn` (no loops), `bakeScene` / `bakedCopy` (Export USD sends a baked copy; the scene keeps its constraints). The view: `Model3DView._applyConstraints` in `applyFrame`, before `_rigApplyAll` (a constrained control poses its bones; a camera gets its world matrix).
- Panel `imageViewer_previzConstraints.js` (`PrevizConstraintsMixin`): the Constrain button / menu (targets first, follower last; Maintain offset in localStorage `imageviewer.previz.constraintOffset`), the Channel Box's CONSTRAINTS section (`_cbConstraintRows`, part of the box's row signature via `_cbConstraintSig`; constrained channels `.constrained`, blue), and the agent-facing methods `previzConstrain(type, {driven, targets, maintainOffset, weights, skip, aim})`, `previzRemoveConstraint`, `previzListConstraints`, `previzSetConstraintWeight`, `previzSetConstraintOn`, `previzEditConstraint`, `previzConstraintUpdateOffsets`, `previzSelectConstraintTargets`, `previzBakeConstraints`.
- Tests `tests/test_constraints.mjs`, `tests/test_constraints_browser.py` (three's world matrices == the solver's, through groups, pivots and a camera). Not done: bones as targets (constrain to a rig control instead), no-jump space switching (keyed weights switch, the item may jump).
### Animation layers (3D space)

- Data (`imageViewer_scene3d.js`): `scene.animLayers` = `[{id, name, mode: additive|override, weight, mute, solo, lock, tracks: {weight: keys}}]` bottom first, `scene.activeLayer` (null = base), per item `item.layerTracks[layerId][prop]` shaped as `item.tracks` (`readAnimLayers`, parsed in `parseScene`; keys on unknown layers dropped). `evaluate(item, f)` / `valueAt` stay the BASE alone; the whole stack is `evaluateIn(scene, item, f)` / `valueIn` / `currentValue` - the shared hook `registerSceneEvaluator(name, order, fn(scene, item, frame, at) -> at)` (agreed with the clip sequencer: base 0, sequencer 100, layers 200). Everything that places or shows an item (model3d, rig3d's ikfk, Channel Box, gizmo maths, Rig menu) uses evaluateIn.
- Editing: the key functions (`track`, `setKeyframe`, `removeKeyframe`, `setTrack`, tangents, `keyframeFrames`, `editValueAt` for the Curve Editor) work on the ACTIVE layer of the scene the item is bound to (`bindScene`: `parseScene`, `addLayer`, `previzScene()` when the scene has layers). `baseTrack` / `allKeyframeFrames` / `sceneKeyframes` see the base / every layer. A locked layer refuses (`editLocked`; the page says so via `previzLayerRefuseLocked`). A pose made on screen goes through `solvePose` / `keyPose` / `setPose`: what to key on the active layer so the WHOLE stack shows the value, found by probing a copy of the item through `evaluateIn` (every blend is affine in the layer's value; pairs of probes get past ikfk's clamp, one correction step for anything else). `setPose` keys when asked, when the prop is keyed on the layer, or whenever the active layer is not the base.
- Blend (`imageViewer_animLayers.js`, pure; `tests/test_anim_layers.mjs`): additive `v + w·L` (scale `v·(1 + w·(L-1))`), override `v + w·(L - v)`, per Euler component, only props the layer keys; mute / solo (`liveLayers`); weight keyable (`layerWeightAt`); ikfk clamped after. `mergeLayerDown` samples every frame of the keyed range, solves the lower layer against the stack without the merged one, `thinKeys`. `extractToLayer`. Panel: `imageViewer_animLayersPanel.js` (`AnimLayersMixin`, dock id `animLayers`, 3D bar group; `previzLayerAdd`, `previzWithLayer(id, fn)`, `previzLayerBakeTarget(modelId, layerId)` for AI takes; browser test `tests/test_anim_layers_browser.py`, `ANIM_LAYERS_SHOTS=<dir>`). AI motion (`previzRigApplyMotion(..., {layer})`, the dialog's "On a new animation layer", on by default) bakes onto a new override layer weighted 1 only over the take's frames (hold keys at lo-1 / lo / hi+1). Clean-room: written from the documented behaviour of DCC layers, no code from any program.

### Clip Sequencer (3D space)

- `imageViewer_clipSeqData.js` (pure; `tests/test_clip_seq.mjs`) is the model: `scene.sequencer = {v, sources, tracks}`; a source is keys per item (frames from 0, `top` / `parent` / control `type` / `role` / `bone` per entry, `match` = the item Match lines up, a rig's COG) or `kind: "file"` (a model's own animation by index); `tracks[0]` is the BOTTOM track; a clip is `{source, start, length, in, out, speed, loop, weight, blendIn, blendOut, pre, post, target, offset {p, yaw, pivot}, accumulate, mute}`. `parseScene` only keeps `data.sequencer` (a JSON copy); `normalizeSeq` tidies it.
- Evaluation is a scene evaluator: `scene3d.registerSceneEvaluator(name, order, fn)` / `evaluateIn(scene, item, frame)` / `valueIn` (shared with the animation layers, which sit at order 200; the sequencer is 100). The module registers itself on import (model3d imports it). Anything placing an item for a frame must use `evaluateIn` (model3d does; the Channel Box shows `valueIn`). The evaluator is pure in (scene, item fields, frame) - no caches by identity (the layers' key solver evaluates probe copies). Override = blend towards the clip (rotation by quaternion slerp, `eulerNear` picks the Euler solution nearest the base so curves never flip), additive = the change since the clip's `in`; overlapping clips of a track cross-fade (`trackActive`). `offset` / `accumulate` act on a source's TOP entries through their rest matrices (`offsetMatrix`, `matchPoint`, `cycleTravel`, `matchToPrevious`). File clips come out as `at.fileClips` and model3d's `_seqDriveMixer` weights the mixer's actions.
- `bakeToKeys` (every frame, then `reduceKeys`); `withClipsBaked(scene)` is the copy the USD export sends (Python reads keys only). Render and the rig exports go through the viewport, so they play the clips as they are.
- The panel is `imageViewer_previzClips.js` (`PrevizClipsMixin`, dock id `clips`, bottom rail; CSS is its own `<style data-clipseq>`). Edits go through `_csEdit` (snapshot JSON before, `previzChanged`, one undo step); a drag keeps the press's sequencer and recomputes each move from it (`_csDrag`), holding the strip's start (`_csDragSpan`). Keys via `clipSeqKeyDown`, asked by `handleKeyDown` while the panel has the focus. AI motion with `asClip` (dialog checkbox, agent arg `as_clip`) bakes as before, then `previzClipsLandTake` takes the keys into an `ai` source, restores the controls' tracks and stacks the clip on an "AI takes" track. Browser tests: `tests/test_clip_seq_browser.py` (`CLIPS_SHOTS=<folder>`), `test_ai_takes_land_as_clips_in_the_clip_sequencer` in `tests/test_rig_tools_browser.py`.
- **The agent's scene actions** (agentY-core's `animate_scene`; `imageViewer_previzAgentScene.js`, `PrevizAgentSceneMixin`; arithmetic `imageViewer_agentSceneData.js`, pure, `tests/test_agent_scene_data.mjs`; browser test `test_the_agent_makes_clips_and_renders_a_turntable_of_each` in `tests/test_rig_tools_browser.py`). They go the rig agent's road - POST `/imageviewer/rig/agent` (`rig_routes.AGENT_ACTIONS`, which must list every name of `SCENE_AGENT_ACTIONS`), the page's `previzRigAgentRun` -> `previzRigAgentExtra`, which hands them to `previzSceneAgent(action, args)` (`_asa_<action>`); none needs a character. Clips (`clip_list` / `clip_from_keys` / `clip_place` / `clip_set` / `clip_split` / `clip_remove` / `clip_match` / `clip_bake` / `clip_track_add` / `clip_track_set`, through `_csEdit`), `add_model`, `add_camera` (`look_at` -> XYZ Euler, `_asaLookAt`) / `set_camera`, `turntable`, `render` / `playblast` (`previzRender` / `previzPlayblast`; `clip` = that clip's frames), `key_set` / `key_delete` / `key_list` (`keyPose` so the stack shows the value; `layer` picks an animation layer), `layer_set` / `layer_remove` / `layer_merge_down`, `display_layer_add` / `_set` / `_list`, `ikfk_switch` / `ikfk_match` / `space_set` / `attr_add` / `sdk_key` / `sdk_remove`, `export_usd` (as File > Export USD), `scene_info` / `scene_set`. Items, clips, tracks, sources and layers are named by id or name. A clip's frames (`clipFrames`) are start..start+length, both shown (its keys run 0..length). **Turntable**: ONE rig per name (`Turntable` group with `turntable: true`, `Turntable cam` under it), keyed on the BASE layer per range (each clip of `clips`, or from..to): the pivot stands on the centre of the subject's box over the range (meshes and bones, sampled at 9 frames, `_asaSubjectBox`) and turns about Y (`turnKeys`: linear keys at most 90 degrees apart - a rotation key runs to the next the short way round - held at the range's end, one step short of the full turn), the camera keyed at (0, height, distance) looking down at it (`fitDistance` from the camera's fov and resolution); keys of other ranges stay, so every clip shows its own turn. `snap.scene3d` (`_agentScene3d` -> `describeScene`) feeds `viewerState().scene3d`: shot, cameras, clips, animation and display layers.

### Keys and curves (previz and roto)

- **Dope Sheet, Curve Editor tools, infinity, playblast** (3D space; clean-room). Pure edits in `imageViewer_animTools.js` (keys named `{id, prop, f}`: `moveKeys`, `scaleKeys` (tangents scaled with time), `staggerKeys`, `deleteKeys`, `setKeysEase`, `copyKeys` / `pasteKeys`, `retimeKeys` (ripple or held short of the next key), `simplifyTrack` (RDP on the drawn curve: adds the worst frame until within tolerance as a share of each axis's range; one-sided tangents, broken at corners; stepped tracks skipped; rotations unwrapped), `snapshotTracks` / `swapTracks` (buffer curves); tests `tests/test_anim_tools.mjs`). **Infinity** is per item and property, `item.infinity[prop] = {pre, post}` of `INFINITY_MODES` (constant - the default, written as no entry -, linear, cycle, offset, oscillate), in `scene3d.valueAt` (`_beyond`, then `_inside`) and its twin `usd_io.value_at` (a USD bake of an item with infinity covers the whole shot, `has_infinity`); parity on random tracks in `tests/test_scene_infinity_parity.py`. **Dope Sheet** (`imageViewer_previzDopeSheet.js`, dock id `dopeSheet`, a tab beside `curves` - `tabWith`; redrawn from `previzRefreshCurves`): Summary / object / property rows, a diamond = every key under the row at that frame; click / Shift / Ctrl, box on empty lanes, drag to move, the ruler bar's ends scale, Offset… / Scale… / Ease / Copy / Paste / Delete; its keys (`previzDopeKeyDown`, asked by `handleKeyDown` while the sheet has the focus): Del, Ctrl+C / V / A, `,` / `.` (Shift: 10), Esc. **Curve Editor tools** (`_previzCurveToolbar`, on the curves on screen): Buffer / Swap (ghost drawn `path.curves-buffer`), Simplify…, Pre / Post ∞ (drawn dashed, `path.curves-infinity`), Retime…, Euler. **Several keys in the Curve Editor** (Sebastian 2026-11-12; `imageViewer_previzCurves.js`, test `tests/test_previz_curves_multiselect_browser.py`): `_previzCurveSel` is a list of DOTS `{id, prop, axis, f}` (`_previzCurveKey` the last clicked); click / Shift toggle / Ctrl remove / Ctrl+Shift add (the 3D view's modifiers, `_previzCurveMode`), a drag on the empty graph is a box (`_previzCurveBlankDown`, `.curves-marquee`, hits by the dots' rects). Dragging a selected key moves every selected key by the same frames and value (rebuilt from the tracks at the press each move, the press's graph scale; a frame that lands on an unselected key keeps the last one that fitted; Shift locks to the first direction); a value moves only the selected axes, a retime the whole key. Every selected key shows its handles; a handle drag turns the same side of every selected key by the same screen angle (all curves share one scale). Tangents ▾ (Smooth / Linear / Stepped / Flat), Del, Ctrl+A, Esc (`previzCurveKeyDown`, asked by `handleKeyDown` while the panel has the focus). The selection is pruned to the dots drawn, except during a drag (`_previzCurveDragging`). **Zoom and pan** (Sebastian 2026-11-12): `_previzCurveView` {key, f0, f1, v0, v1} replaces the fit in `_previzCurveGeom` (`g.timeline` stays the timeline's range for clamps) while the same objects are shown (`_previzCurveViewKey`); wheel zooms about the pointer (Shift time only, Ctrl values only, `_previzCurveWheel`), middle or Alt+left drag pans, Alt+right drag zooms (`_previzCurveNavDown`, capture phase), F / the Fit button (`previzCurveFit`) goes back; `_previzCurveTicks` draws frame / value numbers (`.curves-tick`). **The list**: Ctrl+click toggles a channel, Shift+click takes the range from the row clicked last (`_previzRangeChannels`, `_previzCurveAnchor`); with several objects the channel rows are `.nested` (indented under the object's name). **Playblast** (`imageViewer_previzPlayblast.js`, 3D menu, keymap `PrevizPlayblast`): `previzRender` with `frameFilter` burning the HUD (`imageViewer_playblastHud.js`: shot, camera + focal on a 24 mm gate, frame and range, note or date, timecode / fps / size; `tests/test_playblast_hud.mjs`) into each frame; name `<shot>_playblast`, no loader node, the shot's render settings untouched, lands in the History. Browser test `tests/test_previz_dope_sheet_browser.py`.
- One key bar in the transport (Set Key, Delete Key, Autokey, ease menu), built in `previzEnsureKeyBar`; `_keyBarContext()` decides whether a press keys the previz scene or the roto tool's shapes, and `keyBarSync()` shows it for whichever is up.
- The drawing tools' options (Roto, SAM3 points/boxes, Annotate) live in the **Parameters** panel: while a tool is on, `_toolShowDock` puts it in `tool-mode` (header names the tool, lock hidden, node widgets hidden, the selection monitor paused via `_paramsToolMode`) and each tool fills `this._toolPanel`, a sibling of `#params-content`. The panel opens with the tool, closes after it only if it was closed before, and goes back to the node on 3D tabs. Nothing floats over the picture but the toolbar.
- Roto and SAM3 follow the canvas selection: selecting a Roto / SAM3 Collector node brings its tool up, selecting anything else puts a tool brought up that way away again (`_toolFollowSelection`, called from the params monitor's tick; the params lock holds it). Their toolbar buttons only create a node (`createToolNode`, left selected) — the selection does the rest. Annotate has no node and still toggles.
- Two dock panels, one look: previz's **Animation Curves** (`imageViewer_previzCurves.js`, value graphs) and **Roto Curves** (`imageViewer_rotoCurves.js`, a shape's timing between keys). Both are built from `imageViewer_curveUI.js` (panel skeleton, list/graph splitter, tangent arms), which stays plain functions so the two mixins can't collide.
- **Node parameters** can be keyed too, on nodes that declare an optional `animation` STRING input whose spec lists `bepic_animatable` names (the bEpic image nodes in `bepic_templates`: Transform, Grade, ColorCorrect, Blur, Merge, Constant, Crop, Retime). The keys are JSON in that hidden widget — a real input, so a changed curve re-runs the node. `imageViewer_paramAnimData.js` is the format and the maths (pure); `bepic_templates/bepic_anim.py` renders it and **must interpolate identically** (compare the two on random tracks after any change). `imageViewer_paramAnim.js` puts key toggles on the Parameters rows (Nuke rules: an animated value keys itself when edited; Alt-click drops a track, leaving the widget at the frame's value), and makes the key bar's third context, `params`; `imageViewer_paramCurves.js` is the **Parameter Curves** panel (dock id `paramCurves`, opened by ∿ in the Parameters header).
- A roto key's `tangents[frame]` carries `{ox, oy, ix, iy, hold}`; `roto_raster.py` must interpolate exactly as `_rotoSegEase` does, or the mask a node renders won't match the viewer.
- Each roto shape has a `mode` (union, plus, minus, intersection, xor — the bEpic Nuke ChannelMerge operations, shape = A, the shapes before it in the list = B, clamped after each). `roto_raster.MODES` / `_merge` and the viewer's `ROTO_MODES` / `rotoMerge` must stay identical. Intersection is the one mode that changes pixels outside a shape's region (to 0), so the renderer handles it on the whole frame. The viewer's mask preview composites the same way on a canvas (`_rotoRenderComposite`), sized to the viewport — not to `#imageviewer-tool-draw`, which keeps its default 300x150 box and draws the rest as overflow.
- The Roto toolbar button binds to a selected Roto node instead of creating one (`selectedToolNode`); the Roto Curves panel opens only from its button.
- `roto_raster.rasterize` stays fast by working each layer only inside the box its outline + feather/dilate/blur can reach, reusing the mask of any frame whose outlines equal an earlier one's, and rendering the rest on a thread pool (`IMAGE_VIEWER_ROTO_THREADS` caps it). A change to the matte maths must keep the box margin wide enough (3σ per blur, the dilate radius) or it clips; compare its output against the old renderer's pixel for pixel.
- Anything that moves the frame must call `_toolsFrameChanged()` (image sequences in `setFrame`, videos in its video branch and `_videoOnTimeUpdate`), or the roto overlay lags until the next unrelated redraw.

### Hotkeys

`imageViewer_keymap.js` is the single table feeding three consumers: ComfyUI's command list (so every action is rebindable in Settings → Keybinding), the panel's own key handler (which answers while the viewer is hovered), and the in-viewer help overlay. Add actions there, not ad-hoc listeners. A combo ComfyUI already owns ships unregistered and still works while hovered.

## Publishing

`.github/workflows/publish.yml` publishes to the Comfy registry **on any push to `master` that touches `pyproject.toml`**, and only from `szprivate/ComfyUI-ImageViewer`. So: bumping `version` in `pyproject.toml` *is* publishing. Push other changes freely; touch that file only when a release is intended.

Remotes: `origin` = szprivate (dev, and the one that publishes). There used to be a second remote for bEpic-studio (the org); check `git remote -v` before pushing.
